# Build Halo in AssaultCube on macOS

The engine source comes from AssaultCube Lockdown Edition v1.3.0.2, commit `1ece5af7533983bb8f827616381d282470793d90`. Engine and ENet notices are in `source/`. Stock data is read from a local AssaultCube RC1 release. The ten Halo missions, viewmodels, characters, compiled mission scripts, cinematic geometry and audio are generated from expanded Halo CE Xbox map caches. [AssaultCube v1.3.0.2](https://github.com/assaultcube/AC/tree/v1.3.0.2)

## Dependencies

Install Apple's command line tools, Python 3.9 or later, CMake, and the Homebrew formulae `sdl2-compat`, `sdl2_image`, `libvorbis` and `libogg`. The cinematic audio importer uses FFmpeg. The client uses the system OpenGL and OpenAL frameworks. `CMAKE_PREFIX_PATH` can point CMake at libraries outside the default Homebrew prefix.

```bash
brew install cmake sdl2-compat sdl2_image libvorbis libogg ffmpeg
```

Set `AC_ASSET_ARCHIVE` to the original `AssaultCube_v1.3.0.2_LockdownEdition_RC1.tar.bz2` file. The scripts also look for it in `~/Downloads`. The staging script checks its SHA-256, extracts game data and retains the bundled license in `build/runtime/docs/license.html`.

## Status

All 55 sections, in campaign order. *Traversal* is how the player gets through the section and what it collides with: retail collision is the section's original Xbox structure BSP (`packages/maps/<map>.hcol`, Retail collision for other sections); stock geometry is AssaultCube's cube grid rasterized from the BSP (no section uses it since the d40 section 3 batch; every section's `.cgz` still holds that grid for AssaultCube's flood waypoints and the stock fallback, with the baked scenery stamped in since the cube-raster stamp, Scenery collision, unverified). *Verified in the game* is the last result that was seen in the game; every one of them predates the batches listed under Not yet verified in the game, and none has been re-run since. An earlier replay also completed all 55 sections with injected positions and combat outcomes (Reference and verification), before those batches. *Notes* name the route gate's stubs (`tests/test_route_metrics.py`, Route metrics), the sections `tests/test_retail_collision.py` lists in `KNOWN_BLOCKED` or `PENDING_WALK`, and the special cases; the details are in the notes per mission below. Totals: 50 sections on foot on retail collision with a planned route (batch 6 added the Crash Site, imported from the retail BSP, and c10 section 3, over two walking assists; the scenery batch added c10 section 6, over two more; the route batch b30 sections 1 and 2, so no retail section keeps a raster route; the c10 section 5 batch, after the final integration review, c10 section 5, over one more; the d40 section 3 batch d40 section 3, the last on stock geometry, from where its stock route started), 3 driven and 2 flown on retail collision (batch 7: planned vehicle routes; all five end in their exits: d40 section 8 since batch 10, c40 section 1 since the route batch), none on stock geometry (`STOCK_SECTIONS` is empty); no stub route: the gate's known-bad table is empty (batch 5 took a30 section 2, b30 section 3 and b40 section 3 out, batch 6 c10 section 3, batch 10 d40 section 8, the scenery batch c10 section 6, the route batch c40 section 1, the c10 section 5 batch c10 section 5, from the importer's metrics of the regenerated sections; the gate itself has not been run since). Every section on retail collision carries its scenery's collision in its `.hcol` (`SCENERY_COLLISION`, Retail collision for other sections: the scenery batch's Crash Site and c10 sections 3 and 6, the c10 section 5 batch's section 5, since the batch after it the other 50, since the d40 section 3 batch d40 section 3 (13) and c10 section 1's `x50_rock`, and since the use-aware rule's batch seven named placements a script only reads or changes after their section, baked ten times since b30's three shaft crates stand in two sections (Scenery collision, Scripted scenery by use); 4,123 placements; b40 sections 4 and 6, c40 section 5 and d40 section 6 have none in their window), none of it seen in the game; the walks verified in earlier batches (a10 sections 1 to 4 and 7) predate it, and most routes were re-planned round it. Every section's encounters changed with the squad-count and major-upgrade batch (Halo's squad sizes per difficulty and major upgrades at placement, Difficulty): fewer actors on Easy and, where a squad's tag `max_count` is below its `min_count`, on Heroic and Legendary, and majors among the placed actors; none of it seen in the game. Every section's damage changed with the difficulty-table work (Difficulty): the globals' difficulty table by Halo's team rules replaces the engine's constants, so damage owned by a team hostile to the player is multiplied by enemy_damage whoever it hits (the player, the marines, and the Covenant and the Flood fighting each other) and divided back out on the owner's own side, the player's, the marines' and every actor's body and shield take their team's vitality values, and shields recharge by their team's recharge value; none of it seen in the game. Every section's fights changed again with the combat-scaling work (Difficulty, Combat by difficulty): hostile actors fire faster, more accurately and with shorter pauses on Heroic and Legendary, slower and less often on Easy, throw grenades by the level, and needles and other homing shots turn at half their rate towards the player on Easy and Normal (0.75 Heroic, full Legendary); since the melee-delay and grenade-timeout export an encounter throws one grenade per its tag timeout and Hunters and sword Elites wait their actr's melee delay after each attack (Combat by difficulty); not seen in the game either. The Flood sections' fights (c10, c20, c40, d20, d40) changed again with the Flood tag batch (Actor AI, The carrier burst, the infection pop and the speed stagger): carriers release 3 to 6 thrown infection forms instead of 4 to 8, an infection form's death pops its neighbours within 0.75 units (one at most on Heroic and Legendary), and c10's nopop forms move faster in combat on Heroic and Legendary and slower on Easy; not seen in the game. Every section's actor shields changed with the shield-leak work (Factions and special enemies, Material damage; Difficulty, Shield recharge): a Jackal's shield covers only its hand-held shield, a marine's leaks a fifth or more of every hit to the body and recharges in 1 s after 8 s, and the crewmen, captains, wounded and suicidal marines and stealth Elites have no shield; not seen in the game. Every section's reverts and Continues changed with the stood-members change (Title menu and saved games, Squad members where they stood): living squad members come back where they stood with their health and shield instead of at their starts at full vitality; not seen in the game. The cinematics of a10, a30, a50, c10 and d20 changed with the MD3 range fix (Import, build and play): 27 clips that used to be left out now play (x30's lifepods, a30's insertion pod, a50's energy lift, c10's x50 Chief and marines, d20's jump down), and saves in those missions restart their section's script on Continue; not seen in the game.

## Not yet verified in the game

Written and compiled while testing was on hold; none of it has been run in the game or by any test, and the tests that check it (registered in `tests/run.py`, listed in `tests/README.md`) have not been run either. Results reported elsewhere in this guide (the 55-section replay, the walked a10 sections, the device checks) predate all of it. The consolidated test plan for when testing resumes is at the end of `tests/README.md`.

- **Robustness and release (this batch).** The `HALO_SANITIZE` ASan/UBSan build of `halo-ac-qa` (`scripts/build-macos.sh --asan`), the 55-section soak (`tests/test_soak.py`, `tests/native/soak_qa.inc`, `tests/native/qa_memory.cpp`), the damaged-asset test (`tests/test_asset_robustness.py`) and the loader fixes it covers, the route metrics and the stub-route gate (`--metrics-only`, `tests/test_route_metrics.py`), `scripts/ci-macos.sh`, and `package-macos.sh` refusing a sanitized build (Robustness, sanitizers and CI). The metrics were produced with `--metrics-only` on this tree and show the maps on disk are what the importer writes; the gate itself has been run once, statically, to fill its known-bad table.
- **Falls, wedges, lifts and light bridges.** Fall and wedge recovery, lift and light-bridge riding, c10 section 2 on retail collision; the recovery's reset on every move that is not walking and its teleport grace.
- **Vehicles.** Vehicles, their placements and the driven Maw escape on retail collision, and the fixes made after them: bodies pushed out of vehicles and shoved by moving ones, walking routes round parked vehicles, vehicle stuck recovery and the thrown-out driver, the Maw's escape taking over a scenario Warthog, vehicles riding lift decks.
- **Grenades, equipment and weapon audio.** Plasma grenades, equipment, tag-driven weapon audio.
- **Title menu and saved games.** The title menu, progress file versions 2 and 3 (Continue resumes the mission script, the vehicles, a seated save in its seat, the ground items, the script's limits on the player and the powerups where the checkpoint left them), loadout carry.
- **Nav points and HUD script commands.** Nav points, the HUD show and blink commands, the corrected reticle registration.
- **Retail weapons and Damage fidelity.** The corrected `jpt!` damage layout, weapons.txt format 3 and equipment.txt format 2, the player's shots, melee and grenades rolling the full-strength range, range falloff to the `jpt!` lower bound, blast edges at the lower bound, actors' grenades rolling from the lower bound, EMP draining shields and stunning vehicles, actors' exact rolled shots with the 1-point floor.
- **Factions and special enemies.** The allegiance matrix and the `ai_allegiance` commands, betrayal, the retail unit teams, Hunter armour by hit region with the `jpt!` material modifiers, Hunter pairs, rage and smash, stealth Elite camouflage rendering, flying Sentinels, the Monitor and Engineers, the Monitor's and Keyes's bodies, story figures drawn outside scenes, the corrected charge flags, actors.txt format 2 and the `materials` lines.
- **Enemy and flying vehicles.** vehicles.txt format 2 with the hover, tank and flyer classes, the drivable Ghost and Scorpion, the player-flown Banshee, the vehicle guns' second triggers, actors driving and flying vehicles and firing their guns, `ai_go_to_vehicle` walkers, `ai_vehicle_encounter`, recorded Banshee flights, dropships and Pelicans drawn in play with their cargo, a30's extraction Pelicans boarded with E, c40 sections 1 and 4 flown on retail collision.
- **Script scenes and player moves.** The player moved by `object_teleport`, `object_set_facing` and `volume_teleport_players_not_inside` (and actors by `object_teleport`), Call scenes (a50's rescue, b30's ledge and shaft B scenes, d20's drop and captain scenes, d40's bridge scene) with the calling thread waiting and a skipped one running the rest of its script, a30's extraction variant by the player's site, `game_all_quiet` and `game_safe_to_speak` answering from the game, volume tests of actors and of `(list_get (players) 0)`, the presentation commands bound as no-ops, the carried-in section's in-memory checkpoint, the cleared progress warning, the credits returning to the title menu.

Fixed: `euler_rows` (`tools/halo_collision.py`) is now Halo's own placement rotation, read from the Halo CE source port in `../Halo3DS` (`matrix4x3_rotation_from_angles`: yaw, then pitch about the world's y axis, then roll about its x axis), which moves every scenery placement that is both turned and tilted (the Crash Site's `.hcol`, c10 section 3's `.cgz`, `.hcol` and `.mesh`, c10 section 6's `.hcol` were regenerated; the routes came out the same); the flown sections' flights resampled at most 7 units apart (`plan_vehicle_route`; c40 section 1's route is 1,576 rows, section 4's 167), so `tests/test_traversal_routes.py`'s 8.5-unit check between flight rows holds (it failed on both before, section 4's at HEAD already); `spawnCarried` passes over spots behind a wall, a closed door or a ledge from the player. Nothing was run.

A full `--metrics-only` run afterwards found all 55 sections current (map and route), no stub route anywhere and no planner warnings; `halo-ac` and `halo-ac-qa` build with no warnings (no C++ changed) and `check-no-retail.sh` is clean. No test was run and nothing was run in the game.
- **Scenery collision everywhere, the Crash Site's mapmodel turns and the camera check (the batch after the c10 section 5 batch).** `SCENERY_COLLISION` now lists every section on retail collision (computed from `MISSIONS` and `VALIDATED_COLLISION`, less the empty `SCENERY_LEFT_OUT`), walked, driven and flown: 54 sections, 4,099 placements; every section's map, mesh, `.hcol` and route were re-imported mission by mission, and every route still ends in its exit with no stub (29 walked routes and c40 section 1's first walk moved; the driven routes and c40 section 4's flight are the same; details and the native spawn and weapon changes under Scenery collision). The cinematic camera's orientation was compared statically with Halo's (Retail mission scripting, Camera point orientation): no mismatch, no change. `tests/test_retail_scenery.py` covers every section (sections without placements statically only) and checks `mapmodel_angles` statically; `tests/test_retail_collision.py` marks every section with scenery pending a walk. Not seen: the engine's actors and vehicles among the new scenery, the drawn props' look (environment base maps without their detail and lightmaps) and the frame rate with up to 160,000 triangles. A full `--metrics-only` run afterwards (7.5 minutes) found all 55 sections current (map and route), no stub and no planner warning; `halo-ac` and `halo-ac-qa` build (no C++ changed) and `check-no-retail.sh` is clean. - **Script vitality in points (the script-vitality change: gap 2 of the batch 10 critic).** On actors, `unit_set_current_vitality` and `units_set_current_vitality` now take Halo's points: each becomes a fraction of the unit's stored maximum, capped at 1, 0 where that maximum is 0 (`../Halo3DS/source/units/unit_scripting_commands.c:85-133`), applied to the engine's difficulty-scaled spawn health and shield; a living body set to 0 dies with no owner whatever protects it, the Monitor excepted as for `ai_kill` (`object_deplete_body`, :127-128; dealt as a death, `dokill`, not a hit). Kills of a whole body (`ai_kill`, `ai_erase`, this depletion, `damage_object` with a lethal effect) never reach `halodamage` (an `INT_MAX` hit skips it, `halodamage`'s first line), so no protection, material or hand-held shield stops them, as before; a hit an ownerless scope deals with a real amount would take the coll's indirect material (`hitMaterial`; no retail path does today: `damage_object`'s 40 is a blast already). `unit_set_maximum_vitality` (and `units_set_maximum_vitality`) re-initialises the maxima at full vitality (:54-65, `object_initialize_vitality` in `objects/damage.c:398-427`); the new full health takes the team's difficulty scale as at spawn and `ai_renew` refills to it. The stored maxima are the variant's unscaled ones as `actor_customize_unit` sets them (`ai/actors.c:2397-2405`: an actv that sets either vitality sets both, else the coll's; difficulty applies only when they are read, `damage.c:433-456`; `variantVitality`, `HaloEnemy::vitalityBody` and `vitalityShield`, `haloenemysetvitality`, `haloenemysetmaximumvitality` in `source/src/halo.cpp`, `haloactorsetstarthealth` in `halo_ai.cpp`). Checked against the caches: the crewman actv's unit vitality at +512 is 25/0, the marine assault rifle's 12/24, marine_suicidal's 5/0. What the retail scripts now give (static scan of the ten missions, Normal): a10's cryo_tech, containment_1, crossfire, fetch, first_contact, airlock_1 and knot units 1 point (1/25 for crewmen, 1/60 armoured marines, 1/100 Elites, 1/30 Grunts; shield 0), so 1 health; c10's `int_c_marines/bay_a` 0.5 of 12 (1 health, shield 0), `marine_suicidal` a 1-point maximum with no shield (1 health). The player is unchanged. Vehicles are not reached by these commands (none of the retail scripts aims them at one). The queued vitality of an attached object without its actor yet (`QueuedVitality`) and a carried actor not spawned yet (`CarriedAttach`) keep points against the script's maximum, else the variant's (`haloenemydefaultvitality`). A script-set maximum is kept by a checkpoint, a walk into the next section and a Continue: the `s squad` and `s attached` records end with an optional ` max <body> <shield>` (`writeMaximum`, `readMaximum` in `source/src/halo_script.cpp`; an older save loads with the variant's maxima, an older engine refuses a record carrying it). Judgement calls: health stays in whole points (at least 1 short of depletion), so 0.5 point is 1; non-finite values are ignored. Not changed, on purpose: a10's containment_1 `doom_crewman` dies in Halo to the scripted plasma blast at `containment_1_flag_4` (`effect_new`) after the door shuts and the Elites go braindead; that blast deals damage only with `halo_effectdamage 1` (off by default), so by default he still lives on, now with 1 point; the knot's `doom_crewman` squad, also 1 point and unprotected, falls to knot_anti's fire. Playfight still deals no damage in the engine where Halo deals 0.3 (`ai/ai.c:671-675`): no retail scene with 1-point units depends on it (the playfighting ones are protected or braindead). Tests (not run): `tests/test_actor_ai.py` item 7 and item 4's points, `tests/test_ai_attach.py` items 6, 8, 9 and 12 in points, QA hook `qa_actor_vitality`.

Fixed in the docs only: kills of a whole body (`ai_kill`, `ai_erase`, script depletion, a lethal `damage_object`) never reach `halodamage` (an `INT_MAX` hit skips it), so the script-vitality entry no longer says `ai_kill` used to be stopped by a Jackal's hand-held shield; the Status introduction names the shield-leak work; `tests/README.md`'s test plan names these batches and the 24-effect `effects.txt`. Nothing run.
- **Presentation.** Script effects (`effect_new`, `effect_new_on_object_marker`, `packages/halo/effects.txt`), camera shake, cinematic screen effects, the flashlight, the damage-direction indicator, sound class gains, the vehicles' engine loops and crash and suspension sounds, weapon zoom sounds, the flamethrower's firing loop and the plasma pistol's charge hum, the melee lunge, the rocket launcher's placement and the plain fallback reticle.

## Import, build and play

Run from the repository root with the directory that contains the expanded North American Xbox Halo CE 1.0 maps. Halo3DS's prepared `halo-source` directory has this layout. The campaign import needs all ten mission caches; the asset library also imports any multiplayer and menu caches in the same directory.

```bash
maps="/path/to/halo-source"
./scripts/import-macos-assets.sh "$maps"
./scripts/package-macos.sh
```

Open `build/Halo in AssaultCube.app` in Finder; it opens on the title menu (see Title menu and saved games). The app includes the staged data and converted files from `packages/`. `package-macos.sh` copies the non-system dylibs (SDL2, SDL2_image, Vorbis, Ogg and their dependencies) into `Contents/Frameworks`, rewrites their install names to `@executable_path/../Frameworks`, ad-hoc signs everything and fails if any `/opt/homebrew` path remains, so the app can be moved to another Apple Silicon Mac. It always verifies the full 55-section import (all `.cgz/.cfg/.mesh`, the `.hcol` files of the retail-collision missions, driven sections included, the ten `.hscb` and the weapon, equipment, actor and vehicle tables) and exits non-zero if anything is missing; pass `--partial` for a development package that only needs the Crash Site files, and set `HALO_PACKAGES_DIR` to package another asset directory. It also runs `scripts/check-no-retail.sh`, which fails if a retail-derived file is tracked, staged or not ignored (everything under `packages/` is ignored except `maps/halo_a30.cfg`), and refuses a `build/macos` configured with `HALO_SANITIZE=ON` (Robustness, sanitizers and CI). Licences ship in `Resources/licenses`; see `NOTICE.md`. Run `./scripts/build-macos.sh` and `./scripts/play-macos.sh` for source development. The campaign importer supports `--mission` and `--part` for focused map edits, writes each section's route metrics beside its map, and with `--metrics-only` writes only those (Route metrics). The script importer supports `--mission` and can regenerate the cinematic BSP meshes and models with `--rebuild`. Run `python3 tools/import_halo_scripts.py "$maps" --mission a10` before repackaging to upgrade an existing Autumn flyby mesh.

Scene meshes (the cinematic worlds, `packages/maps/halo_scene_<map>_NN.mesh` and `packages/textures/halo_scene_<map>_NN/`) are exported by `tools/import_halo_scripts.py` (and the asset library) only when missing, unless `--rebuild` is given, so an exporter change leaves the old ones on disk. `python3 tools/import_halo_scripts.py "$maps" --check-scenes` exports every campaign scene BSP into a temporary directory and lists the files that differ (`stale_scene_meshes`; about a minute, writes nothing under `packages/`); `tests/test_scene_meshes.py` runs it. Meshes compare by bytes; PNG textures compare by content since the content-currency fix (`png_content` in `tools/halo_map_art.py`: the IHDR data and the IDAT chunks inflated, every chunk's CRC checked; a PNG that does not decode is stale), since their deflate stream comes from the running Python's zlib and a Python upgrade would otherwise mark every texture stale. `write_png` (every PNG the importers write) now leaves a file whose content is already what it would write untouched (compared the same way, before compressing), so a re-import rewrites only the textures that changed. The cinematic cast (`packages/models/halo/scene/<map>/`, `export_cast` and `device_rows`) is checked too since the MD3 range fix (not seen in the game). Before it, 27 clips the scripts play failed to export and were left out of the `.hscb` without a word, so their scenes played without them: c10's x50 (the Chief's five clips, Mendoza's, Bisenti's, marine 2's and the infection form's, 13), a50's energy lift (six marines and `chief_lift`), d20's `chief` jump down and lift, a10's x30 lifepods (four) and a30's `intro_pod` airbrakes. Each carried a vertex past the MD3's signed 16-bit range at `geometry_scale` (`max(100, ceil(extent x 48))`, `model_pack_scale`): root motion takes the body far from the model's own extent (`struct.error` in `pack_vertex`). `write_export` (`tools/import_halo_models.py`) now skins every frame first (`posed_vertices`) and, when the result would overflow, packs each frame about the centre of its own box (`frame_origins`), stored as the frame record's `local_origin` under the frame name `halo_origin` (`ORIGIN_FRAME`); `md3.h`'s loader adds that origin back to the frame's vertices before `scaleverts`, so the vertex step stays the model's own (mdlscale 100 to 152 for 26 of the 27; a30's pod, whose airbrakes fly off within one frame, needs mdlscale 1433, a step of 0.014 units). A frame still too big on its own raises the mdlscale to the smallest that fits; the cfg's mdlscale (per model, `rendermodel.cpp` `mdlscale`, applied at load by `scaleverts`) divides it back out. Every `export` caller gets this (devices, vehicles, the asset library: a library model that used to fail exports on its next import; not run). An export that fits packs byte for byte as before (a rebuild with the old exporter gave the same bytes for every a10 and d20 model it could export), and stock MD3s never name a frame `halo_origin`, so they load as before; an older engine would draw the 27 clips at the scene origin. A decode of five of them as the engine loads them came within half a vertex step of the posed positions; the review's decode of all 27 against their frame boxes agreed within half a step, except one a10 lifepod clip (`object_1921_clip_69`), about 8,600 units out, at one step (the float precision of positions that far out). The three idle poses that failed (b40's and c40's `object_2993`/`object_2299`, the foglight, and d40's `object_2189`, `door small`) were traced: the current exporter writes them without error, and no named object uses those slugs any more (the foglight and the door are devices, `device_<tag>`); they were orphans of an older numbering. `export_cast` now reports every failed export (a `WARNING` line per export and a count in the mission's summary line; a clip a script names that its graph lacks, c10's `keyes x50_4110captain`, is listed apart: retail starts no animation for it either, `unit_start_user_animation`, Halo3DS `source/units/units.c:4033`), a failed export removes the directory it created, and the import prunes the directories under its map's scene folder that no export owns (`prune_scene_models`: 198 removed, from an older object numbering and the failed exports' skin-only directories). `--check-scenes` adds the cast (`check_scenes`): exports the scripts use with no `tris.md3`, orphan directories, and `.hscb` model paths out of step with the disk, each a `WARNING` and exit 1. The fix's `--rebuild` of all ten missions also refreshed 75 device and object models that older exporters had written (a non-rebuild import keeps a model on disk), and regenerated `a10`, `a30`, `a50`, `c10` and `d20.hscb` (their clip tables grew: a10 79 clips, a30 8, a50 20, c10 20, d20 5), so a saved game in those missions restarts its section's script on Continue (the `.hscb` hash changed); the other five are byte for byte the same. The use-aware rule's batch found 61 of the 68 campaign scene meshes (and their textures) stale, among them `halo_scene_d40_03.mesh`, which the d40 section 3 batch's verifier had seen, and regenerated all of them with `export_worlds`' rebuild; the multiplayer and menu maps' 14 matched. The worlds each `.hscb` lists did not change. Not seen in the game: the cinematics in the regenerated worlds (what changed was not traced to a particular exporter change; the meshes differ in size by a few percent at most, `halo_scene_d40_09` grew from an empty mesh to 340 triangles).

`build/retail-assets/catalog.json` indexes the local development library. It records every cache and tag, all BSPs, scenario placement tables, animation tracks, and looping sound definitions. The library copies the original caches into `sources/` and exports first-person and static models, full BSP meshes and collision, bitmap PNGs, and sound variants. Bitmap exports include cubemap faces and volume slices; P8 bump images preserve palette indices. WAV assets preserve the original Xbox sample encoding. The catalog records conversion gaps, and the copied caches retain the original data for future work. The campaign app uses its own converted media; the multiplayer caches and the original `ui.map` menu are in the library only (multiplayer and the retail menu are out of scope, NOTICE.md). Rerun only the library with `python3 tools/import_halo_library.py "$maps"`; add `--rebuild` to regenerate converted assets.

The Silent Cartographer uses retail collision across all three sections. To update an existing campaign install, regenerate the maps and script projections, then package the app.

```bash
python3 tools/import_halo_campaign.py "$maps" --mission b30
python3 tools/import_halo_scripts.py "$maps" --mission b30
./scripts/package-macos.sh
```

The original structure collision surfaces handle movement, weapon traces, and bot sight. Follow the path from the beach to the facility, use the Cartographer terminal, and climb back to the surface. Press **E** near the Pelican's rear ramp once it reaches the landing zone. The adapted entry leaves the security door open so the route starts at the descent.
To launch that section directly, run `./scripts/play-macos.sh '-ehalo_section 4 2'`.

### Retail collision for other sections

The engine plays any section on the original structure collision when `packages/maps/<section>.hcol` exists and loads (`Halo: loaded N retail collision surfaces for <section> (M static machines)` in the log); a missing file means stock AssaultCube geometry, and a file that fails validation is reported (`retail collision unavailable`) and also falls back to stock geometry. `tools/import_halo_campaign.py --collision validated|all|none` chooses the sections that get one: `validated` (the default) covers the missions in `VALIDATED_COLLISION` (a10, a30, a50, b30, b40, c10, c20, c40, d20 and d40) except the sections in `STOCK_SECTIONS`, which keep stock geometry (and need no `.hcol` in `package-macos.sh`; the table is empty since the d40 section 3 batch, so the package requires all 55 `.hcol` files, `halo_d40_03.hcol` included, and the QA client's `qa_stock_geometry 1` stands in for a stock section in the tests that drive on stock geometry); `all` writes every section, `none` removes them. For those sections the importer also

- projects each section's BSP with that section's own projection (b30's return reuses section 1's, which is why `halo_b30_03.hcol` is identical to `halo_b30_01.hcol`),
- bakes the scenario machines that never move (bulkhead frames, consoles, a10's jammed bulkheads, which the mission script creates) into the `.hcol` as their real collision polygons, listed in a `DEV1` trailer so the engine stops colliding with their coarse boxes; machines a script switches to a `door_off` permutation (a50's cell doors) keep the engine's removable box; machines that move only in a cinematic (`CINEMATIC_STILL`: c20's `index_platform`) are baked in their first pose,
- treats a closed one-sided door (it opens only for a player behind it) walked into from the front as a door to wait at if a script makes it two-sided or opens it, and as a wall otherwise,
- plans the walkable route, spawns, markers and items on floors with room for the body (walls, shelves, railings and invisible stair ramps included), routes round open door panels, crouches through crawl spaces, jumps low obstacles and gaps, waits at a door only if a script opens it and there is no other way (doors that scripts only close, and those in `NEVER_OPEN`, are walls), walks through the objective volumes the script waits for, or up to a control it waits for the player to use (`SECTION_OBJECTIVES`, which the section's projection also covers), and starts a section past the door its entry script shuts behind the player,
- goes round the vehicles parked in the section when it starts (`VehicleBlocks` in `tools/import_halo_campaign.py`), as the engine's bodies collide with them: the box `source/src/halo_vehicle.cpp` gives the vehicle (the retail model's length, a width centred on its axis and its height above the floor, at the section's scale) at the placement's map cell and heading, widened by the body and the walker's drift, from 0.25 units below the retail point to its top (a body within 0.42 AssaultCube units of the top stands on it). Placements a script creates (not-placed flag 1: most of a50's turrets, d40's trench jeeps, b40's `crev_ent` turrets) are not there when the walk passes and are left out, as are those no difficulty places; turrets, Ghosts, Wraiths, Banshees and Warthogs all count. A jump may not pass through one, and actors, weapons and equipment are not put inside one. The importer lists each walked section's obstacles (`route avoids parked vehicles ...`),
- treats lift platforms (b40's `lift_a` to `lift_d`, c10's `lift_a`, `lift_b` and `lift_d`) as something to stand on but not walk through, and can route a lift ride: a `SECTION_OBJECTIVES` control followed by the retail point where the ride sets the player down,
- treats a closed door that a script destroys (c10's `post_helmet_door_3`, replaced by a bashed-in door after the x50 scene), or whose device group a script opens (c20's floor doors, `device_group_set floor1_door1 door_open`), as a door to wait at, not a wall,
- never jumps through a sealed door or an open door's panels (door panels are not in the BSP, so the jump test missed them); a jump over a scripted door stays free, as a10's cryo jump tutorial needs. The next a50 import changes `halo_a50_02.route`, which jumped through `gravity_door_lr`'s open panels; the other validated routes are unchanged,
- walks off a ledge onto a floor further down than a jump drops only in the sections listed in `LEDGE_DROPS` (d20 sections 2 and 3, up to 3 and 2.6 units), at the cost of a 300-step detour, so other routes are unchanged; a section whose exit volume hangs in a shaft past the last floor (`EXIT_FALLS`: d20 section 1) ends its route with one more step over the shaft,
- in the missions in `UNGROUPED_DOORS_SEALED` (d40), treats a closed unnamed door with neither a power nor a position group as a wall: no script names it and nothing drives it, and a manual or unpowered door does not open on approach. Elsewhere such doors still count as doors to wait at, as the routes were planned (and a10's walked); switching a mission over needs a re-walk,
- reports the scripted doors a route waits at (including those a jump passes over), an objective it cannot reach (`no route to <volume>`) and a route that ends outside its exit volume (`WARNING route ends outside its exit`),
- walks c10's light bridges as floor once extended (`LightBridges` in `tools/halo_collision.py`: flat machine panels that slide out over a pit; the extended pose is the end of the animation whose panels join into one strip). Stepping onto a bridge that starts retracted costs as much as a scripted door, and is allowed only if a script extends it (`flicker_bridge` moves `bridge_c`) or one of its controls is within reach of the step before; `bridge_a`, whose only control is on its far side, cannot be crossed from lift_a's side. A section's bridges in `UNCROSSED_BRIDGES` are no floor at all (c10 section 5's `bridge_c`, which `flicker_bridge` never leaves extended long enough to cross). Bridge decks are kept out of the native cube floor, so actors, items and weapons are not placed on them,
- for the sections in `LIFT_RIDES` (b40 section 6, c10 sections 1 and 4, c20 sections 1 to 3, d40 section 5) writes one more route row on the lift's deck: beside the lift's control when one of the lift's controls is within reach of the deck (the walker uses it there to send the lift), else at the deck's centre (the Library's platforms rise once the player stands on them, d40's elevator once enc5_3 holds every player), with the lift position at which the deck is at that landing. The row is in the route file only; spawns, markers and items stay on retail floor,
- for a damage target no walkable spot sees (`ThrowTarget`: d40's s3 manifold), simulates frag grenade throws on the section's retail collision (`GrenadeArcs` in `tools/halo_collision.py`: the engine's throw velocity, bounce physics, fuse and device boxes, step by step; since the route batch the sphere sweep is `halocollisionsweep`'s, against each polygon's face, edges and corners with the rounded contact normal, where it ignored edges beyond the radius before; a frag all but at rest on a floor goes off where it lies), from the standing cells the leg can walk to: every other cell within `reach` (3.5) units of the target and every fourth out to `far` (7), every fourth throughout on walkways more than 1.5 units below it, from `below` (5) units under the target to `above` (3) over it. From each spot a first look turns up to 60 degrees either side of the target's bearing and pitches -45 to 45 degrees (`pitches`), 15 degrees apart; round each first throw that goes off within half a unit of the frag's outer radius of a target a coarse pass looks 5 degrees apart, then round the 16 best landings from the cells up to two away and within 6 degrees in 2-degree steps. A throw counts when it goes off within the frag's outer blast radius (2.5 units) of the target's box with a clear line to its centre (as `haloscenariotargetsnear` and `Scenario::targetsNear` test) and more than that radius from the thrower's body, feet to eye (so the player who throws it is not caught). The 300 that do the most damage from furthest off are ranked by how many of their eight neighbours a degree away count too, then by damage, then by the thrower's distance; the route walks to the spot whose best aims rank highest and writes a throw row per aim, up to `aims` (5), two degrees apart at least (`WARNING no simulated frag throw lands by ...` when none does, `WARNING only N frag aims` under `least`, 3); the metrics record each planned aim (`throws`: target, yaw, pitch, damage, neighbours, the thrower's distance and the frag's outer radius). Before the route batch: one ring of 3.5 units on the floor above, pitches -30 to 10, no thrower distance, three aims,
- uses a control the script waits for (`UseControl` in `SECTION_OBJECTIVES`: the walker presses use until a named machine stands at a position) and shoots a damage target (`ShootTarget`: the route walks to the nearest standing spot from which a target is in sight, the eye 4.5 units above the feet, through the structure collision (two-sided surfaces included, as shots see it) and the machines in the pose the control left them, and only through the space the opened machine left; that machine's new pose is a wall for the walk there and its old one is not). A target no walkable spot sees is reported (`WARNING no route to sight of ...: the walker cannot shoot ...`) and the walk goes on without it,
- checks that the route passes the volumes a `DOOR_WAITS` door's script waits for before it opens the door (`DOOR_PREREQUISITES`: a10 section 5's `tunnel_trigger_close` before `tunnel_door_2`) ahead of the door's row, records it in the section's metrics (`door_order`) and warns otherwise,
- in the sections in `DOOR_WAITS` (a10 sections 5 and 6) writes a row of its own on the last step before each scripted door the route waits at, so the walker waits there (inside the door's opening radius) instead of backing off and retrying (`tunnel_door_2` in section 5; `cryo_door_2` and `cryo_door_1` in section 6). Only the route files change (a row is added after the step it stands on); the section maps are byte for byte as before. To go back, empty `DOOR_WAITS` and re-run `import_halo_campaign.py "$maps" --mission a10 --part 5` and `--part 6`,
- in the missions in `FLOOR_LEAVES` (c10), walks over a door's leaves folded flat on the floor (`floor_leaf` in `tools/halo_collision.py`: a panel of a door, device kind 0, whose top in that pose is at most 0.25 units above the door's base and which is at most 0.3 thick). c10's interior doors (the BSP 3/4 doorway's `bsp3_device_bsp4_device_1` to `_3`, BSP 4's `bsp4_device_12` and `_17`, BSP 1's and 2's doors of the same model) fold their lower leaves into the doorway when open, 0.05 to 0.23 units proud of the floor, more than a step at the indoor scale; Halo's biped walks over them, and the engine now does too (`Scenario::collideDevices` skips such panels for bodies; shots still hit them). The other missions' routes keep every panel solid, as they were planned and walked; the engine rule applies everywhere, where it only lets bodies through where the routes never go (a static scan of the retail tags finds such panels only on c10's doors: no other mission's door, kind 0 and not a lift, has a panel within those limits in any frame). None of c10's current routes (sections 1, 2, 4 and 7) crosses such a leaf, so their routes and maps are byte for byte as before; the leaves matter to sections 5 and 6, which stay stock for other reasons (below). To put c10 back, empty `FLOOR_LEAVES` and re-run `import_halo_campaign.py "$maps" --mission c10`,
- in the sections in `ASSISTS` (c10 sections 3, 5 and 6), bakes authored walking assists into the `.hcol`: invisible two-sided ramps or planks (convex polygons given in retail units, at most 49 degrees), each standing in for a jump or climb Halo's biped makes and the engine's fixed jump cannot, never for a link Halo does not have. They follow the structure and machine polygons and are listed in an `AST1` trailer after `DEV1` (the surface indices); the engine (`halocollisionmap`) marks them, collides bodies (the player and actors) with them, and lets rays (shots, sight lines, floor probes), grenades, gibs, the camera and vehicles (`halo_vehicle.cpp`'s chassis sweeps and wheel rays) through (`halocollisiontrace` skips them; `retailsweep` and `retailoverlap` in `physics.cpp` pass `retailbody`); the load line counts them (`(M static machines, K walking assists)`). The planner walks them like floor (their own floor is kept through the two-sided-sheet filter, which drops the floors under them) and keeps them out of its sight lines and grenade arcs; each section's metrics count them (`assists`). The figures behind them are in Stock sections (Falls, wedges, lifts and light bridges). To take a section's assists out, delete its `ASSISTS` entry and re-run the section's import (`--mission c10 --part 3`); to put it back on stock geometry as well, add it to `STOCK_SECTIONS`,
- in the sections in `SCENERY_COLLISION` (every section on retail collision since the batch after the c10 section 5 batch, walked, driven and flown; d40 section 3 since the d40 section 3 batch), bakes the scenario's scenery into the `.hcol` (Scenery collision, below),
- in the sections in `SLIDES` (the Crash Site), lets a ledge drop (`LEDGE_DROPS`) go down a slope too steep to stand on, landing up to six cells on, when the body has room along the straight line from the ledge to the landing (instead of falling clear of a vertical face): the engine's body slides down such a face (`retailmove` treats it as a wall and gravity takes it along it), as Halo's biped walks down it (falls under 3 units do no harm, the globals' falling-damage block). The rows have flag 8 as other drops. The other sections' drops are unchanged,
- writes the route (`x y z [flags [device@position [ax ay az]]]`, flag 1 crouch, 2 jump, 4 the first step after a lift ride, 8 a walk off a ledge, 16 a lift's deck row, 32 call the lift from the landing (no script moves it) or use a bridge's control, 64 send the lift from the control on its deck, 128 a step on a light bridge that has to be extended, 256 use the control in reach until the machine named stands at the position, 512 shoot the damage target named down to the health given, at the aim point in the last three columns, 1024 wait at the door named until it is open that far, 2048 throw frags at the aim point in the last three columns, from the eye above the row, until the target named is down to the health given (one row per aim, best first); 256, 512, 1024 and 2048 rows repeat the step row before them; the driven and flown sections' rows add 4096 drive or fly to the point, 8192 board the vehicle type named, 16384 get out, Planned vehicle routes) to `packages/maps/<section>.route` for `tests/test_retail_collision.py` (`tests/test_traversal_routes.py` checks the lift, bridge, action and vehicle rows).

#### Scenery collision

Not verified in the game (written while testing was on hold). Halo collides everything with scenery (rocks, trees, crates, barricades, fallen computer banks, the invisible `door_blast_collision` planes and `a50_nutblocker`s its designers laid over debris and doorways); the engine's world collision is the `.hcol`, so the sections in `SCENERY_COLLISION` (`tools/import_halo_campaign.py`: since the batch after the c10 section 5 batch every section on retail collision, less any in `SCENERY_LEFT_OUT`, which is empty) carry theirs there. For such a section the importer (`scenery_surfaces` in `tools/halo_collision.py`)

- takes the scenario's scenery placements (`scnr` +528, palette +540) whose origin is in the section's BSP (the machines' rule: inside its open space, failing every BSP inside its bounds), or since the d40 section 3 batch in one of the other BSPs the section holds (`EXTRA_BSPS`: c40 section 1's BSP 12, the pulse generator room; a placement in two of them counts once), and in the section's map window (8 units' margin); leaves out those a script creates, destroys or moves (not-placed flag 1, or since the use-aware rule's batch a name a script passes to anything but a read-only use while the section plays, `scenery_scripted` over `object_uses`, Scripted scenery by use, below: the c10 insertion's helmets and props; before, any script string holding the name counted; since the d40 section 3 batch also a name holding the pattern of an `object_create_containing`, `object_create_anew_containing` or `object_destroy_containing` call, `scripted by name pattern` on the import line: Halo matches the pattern anywhere in the name, `strstr` in `hs_library_external.c` of the Halo CE port in `../Halo3DS`, as the engine's interpreter does; `containing_calls` in `tools/import_halo_scripts.py` collects the calls), those some difficulty does not place (flags 2, 4, 8), and those whose tag has no collision model (plants, sparks, decals: `left out` on the import line);
- places each one's `coll` polygons: every node's BSP in its render model's bind pose (a node with several BSPs, one per permutation of its region, as the boulders' five, gives the one at the placement's desired permutation, else the first, which is the permutation `model_meshes` draws), rotated by the placement's yaw, pitch and roll (`euler_rows`: since the final integration review Halo's own `matrix4x3_rotation_from_angles`, read from the Halo CE source port in `../Halo3DS` that object placement uses: yaw about +z, then pitch about the world's y axis, then roll about the world's x axis; the scenery batch's yaw-pitch-roll about the object's own axes agreed only for objects not both turned and tilted, and against the BSP floors of c10, a50 and b40 Halo's composition rests 45 tilted Covenant crates with fewer vertices in the floor, most with none, against 6 for the old one, 25 equal; the scenery sections were re-imported with it, not seen in the game) and moved to its position; a two-sided polygon whose normal points down is faced up (the engine collides alike from either side; the planner reads only upward floors);
- writes them after the static machines and before the walking assists, with an `SCN1` trailer between `DEV1` and `AST1`: per placement its scenario scenery index, its first surface and its surface count. The engine (`halocollisionmap`) checks the ranges (ascending, inside the file, not overlapping), flags the surfaces `haloCollisionScenery` (for QA and the log only: they are ordinary static surfaces, so bodies, shots, sight lines, grenades, the camera and vehicles all collide with them) and logs `Halo: loaded N retail collision surfaces for <map> (M static machines[, K walking assists]), S scenery objects (T surfaces)`; the part before the comma is as it was;
- plans the route, spawns and markers on and round them as for the static machines (their tops are floor, their sides walls, and they block sight lines and grenade arcs), and counts them in the section's metrics (`scenery`);
- draws them: the Crash Site's trees, boulders and lifepod by their mapmodels where the native floor holds one (whose cylinders no longer collide: the tracked `halo_a30.cfg` gives them zero radius and height, `mmcollide` skips them), turned as Halo turns them since the batch after the c10 section 5 batch (`mapmodel_angles`, below), every other baked placement in the section's `.mesh` (`write_world_mesh`'s `scenery`: its render model's first permutation, its model-shader (`soso`) parts and, since the batch after the c10 section 5 batch, its environment-shader (`senv`) parts by their base map, which is how Halo's human props are shaded (barricades, computer banks, gun racks: before, they collided unseen), shaded with the BSP's lightmap colours within 2 units of its origin, opaque but for the parts whose `soso` shader Halo alpha-tests (no "not alpha tested" flag: the pine boughs), which go in cutout batches (`HALO_CUTOUT`, alpha kept; review fix, not seen rendered), as do `senv` parts with the "alpha tested" flag; a batch over the loader's 200,000 vertices is split; the invisible planes draw nothing). A mapmodel whose placement is not baked (another BSP's, or a scripted one) draws without colliding; since the use-aware rule's batch the Crash Site has none (`pod_of_death` is baked) and draws no mapmodel for a placement the mission does not place at its start (not-placed flag 1): its only one, `intro_tree`, is created by no a30 script (its one use is `cutscene_intro`'s `object_destroy`; a30 has no `*_containing` call), so Halo never shows it in play, and it had drawn from the start (`crash_site_scenery`; judgement call: a mapmodel cannot appear later, and nothing needs it to).

Scripted scenery by name pattern (the d40 section 3 batch; judgement calls, read statically from the decompiled scripts, not seen in the game). Of every `*_containing` pattern in the ten scenarios, the ones that match placed scenery (not-placed flag 0) in a section's window are: a50's `box` (`extraction_cleanup`: `box_1` to `box_4`, `c_storage` crates in the hangar, sections 3 and 5) and d40's `bsp_8_` (`endgame_cleaner`) and `bsp_8_1`, `_5`, `_7`, `_8` (`test_for_endgame`: the flames and oxygen tanks along the Warthog run, section 8). Both run only once the mission is over: `game_win_script` calls `extraction_cleanup` behind a fade out just before `cutscene_extraction` and `game_won`, and `test_for_endgame` waits for the player in `grand_finale` (the drive's end), destroys every object, re-creates those and wakes `endgame_cleaner`, which destroys them after a sleep while the ending plays. So they stay baked (`SCENERY_SCRIPTS_AT_END`: a crate or a flame that goes in the mission's last seconds changes nothing the player can do; `bsp_8_1`, `_5`, `_7` and `_8` themselves, which those calls name in full, are flames without collision, baked as nothing since the use-aware rule's batch). d40's `hangar_fire` (`hangar_3` in `x70_finale`, the ending cinematic) matches `hangar_fire_2a` to `_2c`, placed `burning_flame` emitters in the Longsword's hangar (section 8); the rule leaves them out now (`3 scripted by name pattern` on section 8's import line), but they have no collision model, so nothing changes. Every other pattern that matches scenery matches only placements a script creates (flag 1, left out already: a10's `x10_` dressing, b30's `downed_smolder` and `dust`, c10's `blood`, `elite_corpse` and `shotgun`, d40's `banshee_fire`, `display`, `hangar_fire_1`, `hangar_tank` and `nipple`); b40's `_dump`, d20's `0_`, `1_`, `2_` and `2x_` and c40's `object_` patterns match no scenery. One placement the whole-name rule left out is put back: c10's `x50_rock` (a `boulder_moss_large` in the swamp, BSP 0, placed when the mission starts) is destroyed only by x50's `door` script (`object_destroy_containing x50_rock`), which plays in section 3, and the player never comes back to BSP 0, so section 1 bakes it (`SCENERY_KEPT`; 156 placements, the route the same). So the new rule took no collision out, and the full `--metrics-only` run found every section's scenery count, map and route unchanged but those of the re-imported sections (55 sections, 4,113 placements: 4,099, d40 section 3's 13 and `x50_rock`). Not looked at: the whole-name rule counts any script string, so a placed scenery name that is also something else's name (c20's `section2` smoke, also a trigger volume; b40's and d20's `rifle`) or that a script names only in another section may be left out where it stands; the batch's scan lists 65 such placed placements in section windows besides `x50_rock` (a placement counted once for each section that holds it), most of them lights, smoke and flames without collision; they are left as they were. The final integration review's scan (every placed scenery placement of the ten scenarios, any BSP; not seen in the game) split them: none with a collision model is left out by a string that no object-typed script literal shares (c20's `section2`, a script and trigger volume name, is smoke), but seven with collision are left out though no script creates, destroys or moves them while their section plays: a30's `pod_of_death` (the player's crashed `lifepod_crashed` at the Crash Site, 417 polygons, named only as the parent of `setup_dead`'s `objects_attach`: it draws as a mapmodel and does not collide), a50's `field_generator_a` (`c_field_generator`, BSP 0, named only by `objects_predict`), b40's `pelican_crashed` (BSP 0, `objects_predict` only), b30's `shaft_box_1` to `_3` (`c_storage`, BSP 0, destroyed only by `cutscene_extraction_exit`, the mission's last scene) and c10's `crashed_dropship` (`pelican_crashed`, BSP 0, destroyed only by `ini_x50_preclean`, section 3's x50, as `x50_rock` is). Left for a later batch: the fix (only object-typed literals of calls that create, destroy, teleport or attach as the child count, plus `SCENERY_KEPT` and `SCENERY_SCRIPTS_AT_END` rows) re-plans the Crash Site, whose route starts at the lifepod, and other routes. Done in the use-aware rule's batch (next paragraph).

Scripted scenery by use (the use-aware rule's batch; judgement calls, read statically from the decompiled scripts and the Halo CE port in `../Halo3DS`, not seen in the game). The whole-name rule left out any named placement whose name some script string held. Now `object_uses` (`tools/import_halo_scripts.py`, beside `containing_calls`) lists, for each object name, every call that takes it as a literal argument of an object type (the node's constant type: `object_list`, 23, or `object` to `scenery_name`, 37 to 48), with the argument's place and the script, and a global's initial value as `(global)`; `scenery_scripted` (`tools/halo_collision.py`) leaves a placement out only if one of its uses may create, destroy, move or otherwise change it, that is any use but these, checked against `../Halo3DS`: `objects_predict` (`hs_objects_predict` → `object_predict`, which loads the definitions of the object and its children), `object_beautify` (a render flag for cutscenes), `objects_can_see_object`, `objects_can_see_flag`, `volume_test_object`, `volume_test_objects` and `volume_test_objects_all` (tests only), `object_pvs_activate` and `object_pvs_set_object` (they pick the object whose PVS is activated, `object_pvs_set_object`), and the parent, argument 0, of `objects_attach` and `objects_detach` (`objects_scripting_attach` and `objects_scripting_detach` attach or detach the child; the parent stays) (`SCENERY_READ_ONLY`, `SCENERY_PARENT_ARGUMENT`); and not a use in a script of `SCENERY_SCRIPTS_AT_END`, which now counts for full names as well as patterns. Any other function, a special form (`set`, `if`, `begin`) or a global's initial value keeps the placement out, so a function not listed keeps the old behaviour. A name that no object-typed literal shares (c20's `section2`, a trigger volume and script name) is no longer left out; every such placement is smoke, a light or a flame without collision, so nothing changes for them. Placed placements with collision that the rule now bakes, each decision:
- a30 `pod_of_death` (the player's crashed `lifepod_crashed` at the Crash Site, 417 polygons): its only uses are `setup_dead`'s four `objects_attach` calls, as the parent of the dead marines and the pilot; baked by the rule. The scenario's player start is inside the pod (Halo's player stands up in it after `cutscene_intro` and walks out of its open rear end; `mission_a30` waits for the player to leave `lz_lifeboat`). The pod's inside is about 1 retail unit high and the engine's body 1.27 (5.2 cube units at the 4.1 scale), so the planner found no layer there (`no continuous walkable layer`); the Crash Site now starts just outside the pod's rear end, on the ground at (31.35, -100.45, 58.7), 3 units from the player start in the direction the player faces (`SECTION_ANCHORS[CRASH_SITE_KEY]`; judgement call: the first steps Halo's player walks, no new link; `lz_lifeboat`'s wait then ends at once, so `intro_1b` and the mission's encounters start as soon as `intro_1` has played). The projection's window is the same, its origin 0.01 lower (`a30.hscb` regenerated: two bytes). 1,123 steps (was 1,136), entry offset 0.2, exit offset 7.8, ending inside `first_marine_extraction`, the jumps and the ledge drop (`SLIDES`) kept; 264 placements; the pod's mapmodel still draws it (229 of the scenario's 585 placements of the five models are mapmodels now; the pod's mapmodel cube is now capped under the raster stamp: 231 capped, 230 before).
- a50 `field_generator_a` (`c_field_generator`, BSP 0): only `objects_predict` in `mission_area2`; baked by the rule (section 1, 265 placements, the route the same).
- b40 `pelican_crashed` (BSP 0): only `objects_predict` in `ext_a_setup`; baked by the rule, but it stands outside b40 section 2's map window (the only section on BSP 0), so no `.hcol` changes. b40 section 7's `2 scripted` are other names.
- b30 `shaft_box_1` to `_3` (`c_storage`, BSP 0): destroyed only in `cutscene_extraction_exit`, which `obj_shafta_goal` calls once the player boards the extraction Pelican, followed by `game_won`; `SCENERY_SCRIPTS_AT_END['b30']` now holds it (its other uses are the script-created dust, `falling_box_` and Pelican objects), so sections 1 and 3 bake them (373 and 358 placements, the routes the same).
- c10 `crashed_dropship` (`pelican_crashed`, BSP 0): destroyed only by `ini_x50_preclean`, which `mission_control` calls in section 3 at `control_lab_trigger`, as `x50_rock` is: `SCENERY_KEPT[('c10', 0)]` (157 placements, the route the same).
- a10 `field_x30` (`c_field_generator`, BSP 6, a10 section 7): destroyed only by `mission_final` after the player reaches `end_trigger`, the mission's exit, just before `x30` and `game_won`; not one of the critic's seven (the review's scan counted only uses within the section), found by this batch's scan: `SCENERY_KEPT[('a10', 6)]` (33 placements, the route the same).
Still left out, as before: a10's `bridge_barricade_1` (`object_create` and `object_destroy` in `mission_shoot`, during play), d40's `fighter_hangar` (`object_create_anew`, `object_teleport`, `scenery_animation_start`), b30's `rock_kick` and `rock_still` (the ledge scene), and the rest, which have no collision model. A scratch comparison of the two rules over every placed named placement of the ten scenarios found only these changes, c20's `section2` smoke and four of d40's `bsp_8_` flames (no collision model). The full `--metrics-only` run found 4,123 placements (4,113 and 10: the pod, the field generators of a10 and a50, the three crates in each of b30's two sections and the dropship); only `halo_a30`'s route changed. `tests/test_scenery_uses.py` checks the uses, the decisions and the SCN1 trailers (needs the caches; not run).

The scenery batch: the Crash Site, 263 placements (23,935 surfaces; 228 drawn by mapmodels, 35 in the mesh, among them the small granite boulders, which have no mapmodel slot), route 1,136 steps (was 1,115), ending in `first_marine_extraction`; c10 section 3, 23 (1,417 surfaces: the channel's and the lab's crates, field generators, uplinks, hall D's fallen b40 metal and four invisible planes), route 1,359 steps, both assists kept; c10 section 6, 2 (a crate and a field generator in hall G); c10 section 5 (since the c10 section 5 batch), 38 (3,064 surfaces; 35 drawn in the mesh; 36 more have no collision model, emitters and lights among them), among them the west bay's debris pile its route climbs, route 1,101 steps over one assist. d40 section 3 was in the table but on stock geometry (no `.hcol`) until the d40 section 3 batch, which put it on retail collision with its 13 placements (1,121 surfaces; notes on d40).

Every other section (the batch after the c10 section 5 batch). The other 50 sections on retail collision were added mission by mission, a10 to d40, the 45 walked ones first, then, once the vehicle planners were seen to take scenery as they take the static machines (`VehicleGround` builds its floors and walls from every polygon it is given, scenery included; `FlightSpace` tests its body probes and its moves against them with `SightLines`), the 3 driven and 2 flown ones; each was re-imported and its metrics read. All 54 now carry their scenery: 4,099 placements, 402,315 surfaces in all, from 1 (c20 sections 1 to 3) to 370 (b30 section 1: the beach's rocks and palms), the largest `.hcol` d20 section 2's 69,981 surfaces (50,630 of them scenery: its rocks and shards), within the loader's 131,072 and 32 MiB (`write_structure_collision` now refuses to write a file past the loader's limits, 3 to 8 points a polygon included, rather than leave the engine to fall back to stock geometry; every scenery polygon has 3 to 8). b40 sections 4 and 6, c40 section 5 and d40 section 6 have no placement in their window (no SCN1). Results, from the importer's metrics (nothing walked):
- every route still ends in its exit with no stub and no planner warning; none needed an assist. 29 walked routes moved round or over scenery (from one step shorter to 26 steps longer, d40 section 5's 68 aside: a10 sections 1 and 3 to 7, a30 sections 2 and 3, a50 sections 1 to 3 and 5, b30 sections 1 and 2, b40 sections 3, 5 and 7, c10 sections 1, 2, 4 and 7, c40 section 3, d20 sections 2 and 4 to 6, d40 sections 1, 4 and 5; a10 section 2's, b30 section 3's and d40 section 2's maps changed in their entities only) and so did c40 section 1's first foot leg (the walk to the Banshee by `fly_away_1`, same length); the driven routes (d40 sections 6 to 8, the Maw's jumps included) and c40 section 4's flight came out byte for byte the same, and d40 section 5's five frag aims are the ones planned before;
- spawns, route markers, items and actors stand clear of the scenery (a scratch check of every section's map entities against its SCN1 polygons: none inside a closed placement at the feet; a d20 section 2 route marker stands under a rock shard's edge 0.8 above the feet, which the body steps onto, and a few items lie under a shelf or against a crate, as in Halo);
- the planner's native actor spawns moved with the routes: a10 section 7 has 2 (was 5), a50 sections 2 and 5 have 5 (were 6), a50 section 3 7 (was 6), b40 section 3 8 (was 6), d40 section 5 2 (was 1); the mission scripts' squads are unaffected;
- c10 section 2's four plasma pistols are no longer placed: they lie in a side room whose doorway a tilted `c_storage_large` and a `door_blast_collision` plane above it close, so by the retail collision geometry the room is sealed in Halo too (inferred, not checked in Halo); the planner no longer reaches it (its reach 69.4 to 68.6 per cent of the floor), and a weapon with no reachable cell within 4 is left out, as on every section;
- 66 of the baked placements draw nothing, by design: the invisible `door_blast_collision` planes and `a50_nutblocker`s;
- the meshes: the largest is a30 section 3's, 159,270 triangles (127,924 of them its 331 placements: pines, rocks, boulders) in 32 texture batches, 11.5 MB; the most textures b30 section 3's 39, under the writer's 100 and the loader's 128 batches. The ten mission imports took about 11 minutes in all, four sections at a time (d40's 3.5, most of it d40 section 5's frag-throw search).
Not seen in the game: whether the engine's actors (who follow AssaultCube's flood waypoints, laid on the native cube raster, which holds the scenery since the cube-raster stamp, below) snag on scenery they used to walk through, whether the Warthog's chassis sweeps and the Banshee's capsules meet scenery the planners passed (d40 section 8's 55 placements in the pit room, c40's 120 and 122), and how 160,000 triangles of drawn scenery perform.

The Crash Site's mapmodels (the batch after the c10 section 5 batch) are turned as Halo turns their placements: AssaultCube draws a mapmodel with Rz(attr1 / 10 + 180) Ry(-attr5 / 10) Rx(-attr6) (`rendermapmodels`, `md3.h`'s `quat(-yaw - 180, pitch).roll(roll)`, `fromquat`), not Halo's Rx(roll) Ry(-pitch) Rz(yaw), so `mapmodel_angles` (`tools/import_halo_campaign.py`) solves for the three attributes from `euler_rows` seen in the campaign frame (y reflected), taking the solution whose roll fits attr6's signed byte of whole degrees and solving yaw and pitch again for the rounded roll. The batches before copied Halo's angles into the attributes (yaw + 180, pitch, roll), which mirrored every yaw: a scratch comparison of the rebuilt matrices over all 586 of a30's placements of the five models found the copied angles up to 178 degrees from Halo's rotation (405 more than a degree off, median 47; the 181 at zero yaw and no tilt were right), and `mapmodel_angles` within 0.43 degrees (mean 0.02; the roll's whole degrees), and within 0.54 over 20,000 random angles. The script-drawn objects already used the right form (`180 - yaw`). `halo_a30.cgz` was re-imported; its mesh, `.hcol`, route and models came out the same. `tests/test_retail_scenery.py` checks `mapmodel_angles` against a re-implementation of `md3.h`'s path statically. Not seen in the game.

The cube raster (the cube-raster stamp; not verified in the game). Each section's `.cgz` still holds AssaultCube's cube raster, which on retail collision only three things read for play (besides the renderer's occlusion map, light and outside-map test, below): AssaultCube's flood waypoints (`FloodThink` and `CanPlaceNodeHere` in `source/src/bot/bot_waypoint.cpp` put a waypoint only on an open cube, at its floor plus 2, and the actors follow them out of sight of their goal, `halo_ai.cpp`), the blob shadows under models (drawn at their cube's floor) and the stock fallback, on which bodies, vehicles, sight lines (`IsVisible`) and spawns use the cubes. On retail collision the player, actors, shots, grenades, the camera, vehicles and the waypoints' links and sight checks (`TraceLine` goes through `haloscenariotrace`) already use the `.hcol`, scenery included; but the raster came from the BSP alone, so waypoints stood inside crates, rocks and trunks, and from inside a closed placement the one-sided retail trace sees out, so their links passed through it. `scenery_raster` (`tools/halo_collision.py`) now stamps every baked placement into the raster after the route is planned: per cube (the square [x, x+1] x [y, y+1] whose floor AssaultCube samples at its corner), each placement counts as solid between the lowest and the highest point of its polygons inside the square; from the cube's floor up, a placement at or below the floor (within 0.3) or wholly 5.2 or more above it (a crown, an arch) is passed over, a low one up to the engine's step (1.26) whose walkable top (normal z 0.65 or more) covers the sample point raises the floor onto that top (and the search goes on from there), a low one that does not is stepped over, and anything else makes the cube solid, so a rock's side or an invisible wall plane makes every cube it crosses solid. The planner's `CollisionTree`, the `.hcol` and every route are untouched (the full `--metrics-only` run found every route the same). The cubes the route walks and the vehicles stand in keep their floor, and so do the Crash Site's mapmodel cubes, which the engine draws at their cube's floor: those inside their own placement (a pine's trunk) are capped instead, open space one unit high, so neither a waypoint (the flood needs the body's radius between floor and ceiling) nor a body on stock geometry fits. Actors, weapons and equipment are placed off the solid cubes (they move to the next cell beside it), and the route's and every placed entity's cubes are now written as open space even where a corner of the square has no floor (beside a wall, on another storey): before the stamp 8,383 of the 62,643 route rows and 1,020 entities (755 route markers, 89 equipment, 58 vehicles, 34 lights, 29 weapons, 26 actors, 13 starts and 16 stand-in boxes) stood in solid cubes, which on the stock fallback rejects a spawn and leaves an item drawn and picked up at the solid cube's default floor. An actor, weapon or equipment piece with nothing but scenery cubes in reach keeps its cell and its cube is given back (`scenery_reopened`: two of b40 section 3's script-created items under a snow pine's low boughs, which start 4.2 to 5 units over the floor, under the body's 5.2, so the engine's bodies cannot walk under them either). Solid scenery cubes carry vdelta 83 (`RASTER_SCENERY_VDELTA`; `haloSceneryVdelta` in `source/src/halo_campaign.h`), which no other solid cube of the section maps has; `load_world` (`worldio.cpp`) sets `haloscenerycubes` for a campaign section map and `lightray` (`worldlight.cpp`) lets light through such cubes, so the cube light that tints models (the player's weapon and body, actors, items) is what it was before the stamp; any other map's solid cubes stop light as always. The 2D occlusion map that culls models (`computeraytable` and `isoccluded`, `worldocull.cpp`) passes them too (`haloscenerycube`): it treats a solid cube as a full-height wall, so without that an actor, item or Crash Site tree behind a waist-high crate or rock would not be drawn, and every model beyond two units would vanish while the camera stood on a tall crate or rock (its cube solid). What else changes on screen: a model standing on a raised cube gets its blob shadow on the low top (before, under it inside the prop's mesh), one on a solid scenery cube (on top of a tall crate) at the cube's default floor (hidden in the prop or the ground below, as the old one under the prop was), the stock minimap (`showmap`, not shown in the campaign) shows the scenery, and the colour buffer is cleared when the camera is inside a scenery cube (the mesh draws over it). The importer prints a `cube raster:` line per section and writes the metrics' `raster` record (`scenery_solid`, `scenery_raised`, `scenery_kept`, `scenery_capped`, and the route rows and entities still in a solid cube, by reason and kind). Results (the full re-import, mission by mission in one run, then a full `--metrics-only`: all 55 sections current, map, route and `.cfg`, no stub, no warning): 137,886 scenery cubes in 44 of the 51 sections with SCN1 placements (the other seven's placements, a50 section 4's, b40 section 2's, c10 section 6's two in hall G and c20's, stand off the native floor the route's flood reached; from 18 in d40 section 8 to 23,798 in a30 section 3; the Crash Site 22,844, about a tenth of its floor, its pines' boughs and boulders), 499 floors raised onto low tops (298 of them by a whole unit or more, which is what the cube floor stores), 310 cubes of the route and the vehicles left as they were and 230 Crash Site mapmodel cubes capped; every route byte for byte the same; 14 equipment pieces, 6 weapons and 5 actors moved off scenery cubes (b40 section 3 most: 2 actors, a weapon and 6 items), every count of actors, weapons, equipment and vehicles the same. Left in solid cubes, all with no native floor at their cube: 753 route rows (c40 section 1's 381 and section 4's 53 flight and leg rows, d40 section 8's 307 and section 6's 6 drive rows, a lift deck or exit fall row in b40 section 6, c20 sections 1 to 3, d20 section 1 and d40 section 7), 54 vehicles at their retail points, and the starts, markers and lights on the flown and driven sections' vehicle routes (68 markers, 5 lights, 1 start); none in a scenery cube. A scratch sampling of every SCN1 wall polygon (its centroid and its vertices pulled a fifth of the way in) found at most 0.12% of a section's samples in an open cube within the body's column (c40 section 1: 37 of 29,930; most of them the cube floor's rounding to whole units). `tests/test_scenery_raster.py` checks the `.cgz` against the metrics and the `.hcol`'s SCN1 statically and the flood waypoints in the game (`qa_raster_waypoints`). Not seen in the game: whether the actors now walk round the scenery, and whether the flood still links every part of a section (a corridor half filled by crates may now be one cube wide; the flood needs open cubes round a waypoint).

Limits: the mesh's scenery is lit by one colour a placement; the Crash Site's mapmodels stand on whole cells and the native floor, up to half a cell from their collision (their turn now matches it). To take a section's scenery out, add it to `SCENERY_LEFT_OUT` and re-run its import; for the Crash Site also put `halo_a30.cfg`'s mapmodel lines back to `CRASH_SITE_MAPMODELS`' sizes (the route then goes round their cylinders again, `SceneryBlocks`). `tests/test_retail_scenery.py` checks the trailers against the metrics without the game and, in it, that each placement stops a ray, a grenade's sweep and a body from above (`qa_retail_scenery`).

A scenario may give several trigger volumes one name (b40 has a `bsp 11,2` and a `bsp 2,11` at each of the c1 tower's two doorways). `SECTION_ANCHORS`, `TRANSITIONS` and `SECTION_EXITS` name the n-th of them `<name>#<n>`, in scenario order; a bare name is the last for anchors and the first for exit volumes. In the engine a BSP switch volume that holds the player when a section starts fires only once the player has left it, and is ignored for the whole section when another volume switches to the same BSP (the doorway the section was entered by).

Parked vehicles changed four routes, each of which walked through or brushed a parked vehicle before (checked statically against the planner's box, and against the engine's box at the map's placement with the body's 1.1-unit radius: the old routes had rows inside, the new ones none): `halo_b40_02.route` crossed `ext_a_bridge_turret_a` and `ext_a_bridge_turret_c` on the a2 tower's bridge (18 rows inside the planner's box, 16 inside the engine's; now 366 rows, was 334), `halo_b40_05.route` crossed `ext_c_banshee_a` and `ext_c_banshee_b` (27 and 26 rows; now 317, was 305), `halo_b40_07.route` brushed `zig_top_turret_a` (7 rows inside the planner's margin, none inside the engine's box; 817 rows as before, 20 of them moved) and `halo_c40_03.route` crossed the unnamed Ghost at (157, -336) in the canyon (6 and 5 rows; 1606 rows as before, 90 moved). Every route still ends where it did. Their section maps changed with them (spawns, markers and the stand-in items follow the route; the cube geometry is unchanged), and `halo_a50_01.cgz` moved one actor spawn out of a turret's box; every other route and section map is byte for byte as it was. None of these has been walked. Re-run `import_halo_campaign.py "$maps" --mission b40` and `--mission c40` (after `tools/halo_vehicles.py`, which writes the boxes' sizes; without `vehicles.txt` the planner ignores vehicles) to regenerate them.

Movement on retail collision uses the stock physics unchanged (ground speed 16, acceleration, friction, jump). The collision layer slides along walls, corners, door panels and other bodies, and climbs device and mapmodel tops below the step height, so nothing the stock geometry slid along snags; allies the player walks into step aside. The test measures this against the stock geometry of each section.

### Falls, wedges, lifts and light bridges

None of this has been verified in the game yet: it was written while testing was on hold. `tests/test_retail_recovery.py`, `tests/test_traversal_routes.py` and the lift and bridge walks of `tests/test_retail_collision.py` (`PENDING_WALK`) check it once testing resumes.

- Falls out of the world. On retail collision a body can fall through a hole or seam in the structure, which the stock cube grid never had. The engine (`updateRecovery` in `source/src/halo.cpp`) keeps the last eight retail floor spots the player stood on, at least 2 units apart (only real structure floor counts: not a lift deck or a light bridge, which move or vanish). A player whose feet are more than 8 units below the section's mesh, or who has been in the air for two seconds with neither structure nor device below down to that depth, is put back on the newest of those spots that is still clear (`Halo recovery: fell below the retail collision, player put back at x,y,z`, or `fell out of the world`). A second recovery within five seconds goes one spot further back. If none is clear, the player dies and reverts to the checkpoint as usual. Actors (enemies and allies) whose feet fall below that depth die, as in Halo. Ordinary falls, into a pit with a floor, or d20's jump into the shaft its exit volume hangs in, never reach that depth or that airtime over nothing, and the checks do nothing on stock geometry, during cinematics or once a section is complete.
- Wedges. A player whose body overlaps the structure by more than 0.1 units or is inside a device's box (a door or a lift deck closing on them) for 0.75 s, or who has been in the air without moving at all for 1.5 s (caught in a crease too steep to stand on), is moved to the nearest clear retail floor within 3 units (`wedged in the collision`, `stuck in mid-air`), or failing that to a safe spot as above. A player wedged before standing on any retail floor since the last move that was not walking (a spawn, a revert to a checkpoint on a lift deck, a teleport) is not reverted, which would put them back in the same place and loop: they are left where they are (`no safe floor since the checkpoint, player left in place`) and checked again five seconds later. A spot inside a vehicle's footprint (one parked there since) is neither recorded nor used. None of these checks run while the player is in a vehicle seat; the vehicle has its own stuck recovery (see Vehicles). A player thrown out of a vehicle onto it, with no room for the body beside it, whom these checks find wedged or falling within 15 s, without having taken a seat since, is put back in that seat instead (not after the vehicle itself fell out of the world), the vehicle righted where it lies or moved to where it fits (`Halo recovery`'s reason, then `player put back in the Warthog at x,y,z`): in the Maw's run the driver has never stood on retail floor, and this used to log `player left in place` every five seconds. Moves that are not walking reset this recovery (see the next item).
- Moves that are not walking. Every move of the player other than walking goes through one helper, `haloplayermoved` in `source/src/halo.cpp`: a spawn or revert, Continue, a section start (BSP switches end a section, so this covers them), the end of a cutscene (the Cartographer's handoff moves the player; every scene's end is treated the same), getting into a vehicle seat (the scripts' `vehicle_load_magic` and `unit_enter_vehicle`, a revert or the Maw's escape seating the player, a reclaim) and out of one (beside it, or thrown onto it), and every QA hook that teleports the player (`tests/native/*.inc`, except `qa_retail_recover`, which stands in for a fall or a wedge). It drops the safe spots recorded before the move, so the recovery never puts the player back on a floor from before a teleport, restarts the wedge and stall timers, and starts a one-second grace in which the wedge and stall checks do not count (the falls are still checked): a wedge right after a move is nudged after 1.75 s instead of 0.75 s. Getting into a seat keeps one safe spot, the retail floor the player got in from: when the vehicle falls out of the world with no safe spot of its own, the driver goes there (`halorecoverplayer`) rather than dying. With `halo_scripttrace 1` each move is logged (`Halo moved: <why>, N safe floors dropped`). The mission scripts' player teleports (`object_teleport` of the player, as in d20's captain scene and d40's bridge) are still not performed, so they do not move the player. Not verified in the game: `tests/test_retail_recovery.py` checks it (`qa_halo_moved`).
