- Lifts. Everyone standing on a lift's deck is carried with it: the player, allies and enemies, on retail collision as on stock geometry. On a rising deck a body counts once any part of it is over the deck, as the device collision stands it there, so the deck no longer lifts into someone on its edge and wedges them (only the player whose centre was over the deck used to be carried). A descending deck carries only the bodies whose centre is over it, so someone standing on the landing beside a flush deck is not dragged down through the landing's floor. A lift's deck at rest may stand higher above its landing than a step: the Library's platforms are 0.38 units up at the bottom of their shafts, 2.6 units at floor 1's scale, against a 1.26-unit step. A grounded body may step onto a lift deck from up to 0.45 retail units below its top (`Scenario::liftBoard`); nothing else changes the step height. The device's collision is its boxes in the current animation pose, so it moves with the lift.
- Light bridges. A light bridge is a machine whose panels slide out over a pit; the engine collides with each panel's box in its current pose, so an extended bridge is floor and a retracted one leaves the pit open. A bridge that retracts under the player (c10's flickering `bridge_c`) drops them into the pit; if the pit has no floor, the fall recovery puts them back on the last floor they stood on. The route planner models them as above (`LightBridges`); c10 section 5's route keeps off `bridge_c` (`UNCROSSED_BRIDGES`).
- The QA walker (`tests/native/retail_qa.inc`) boards, calls and sends lifts at deck rows, waits two rows short of a light bridge until it is extended, and after a recovery carries on from the nearest route row behind it. See `tests/README.md`.
- Stock sections. With this, c10 section 2 moved to retail collision (below), in batch 6 c10 section 3 (over two walking assists) and the Crash Site (imported from the retail BSP), in the scenery batch c10 section 6 (over two walking assists up fallen debris, found when its scenery was looked at again), and in the c10 section 5 batch c10 section 5 (over one walking assist onto the debris pile in its west bay, measured with Halo's placement rotation). d40 section 3 has no way between its floors that the engine's movement can take and none that an assist may stand in for (below); since the d40 section 3 batch it is on retail collision all the same, starting on the upper level where its stock route started, so `STOCK_SECTIONS` is empty. What Halo's biped can do, read from the retail tags (batch 6; not checked in Halo): the cyborg biped's jump velocity is 0.07 world units a tick (`bipd` +948), and the globals' falling-damage block was built with a gravity of 0.003565 units a tick squared (its harmful 3- and 6-unit falls are stored as 0.1463 and 0.2068 units a tick, v²/2d, and its 16.5-unit limit as 0.343), so a standing jump lifts the feet v²/2g = 0.69 units in 0.65 s; crouching in the air raises the feet by the difference between the standing and crouching collision heights (0.7 and 0.5, `bipd` +1060 and +1064), 0.89 in all, and the collision sphere's roll over an edge adds a few hundredths; running (2.25 units a second forward, the globals' player information) a level jump covers about 2.9 units. The engine's jump at the indoor scale (8) lifts the feet 0.36 units and a running jump covers about 1.3; the planner's jumps (2 to 4 cells, `JUMP_CLIMB`) model less than that. The movement constants are fixed, so a link inside Halo's envelope and outside the engine's gets a walking assist (`ASSISTS`), and one outside Halo's envelope stays out. d40 sections 6 to 8, the Warthog run, and c40 sections 1 and 4, which only a Banshee leaves, are `DRIVEN_SECTIONS`: they get an `.hcol` for the vehicle and, since batch 7, a planned vehicle route instead of a walking one (Planned vehicle routes, below; Vehicles; Enemy and flying vehicles).

Notes on a10 (The Pillar of Autumn), from `python3 tests/test_retail_collision.py a10` (one playthrough from section 1): sections 1 to 4 are walked start to exit on retail collision, with spawns, markers and items on retail floor and movement within 10% of stock (ground speed 15.9 to 16.0 u/s in every section, the same acceleration and jump). Section 1 includes the jump and crouch tutorials; section 2 Keyes's bridge scene (the route walks through `bridge_trigger_3`) and the cafeteria fight; section 4 a jammed bulkhead to crouch under. Section 7 reaches its exit when started on its own. Open items:

- Section 5 waits at `tunnel_door_2`, now on a door row of its own (`DOOR_WAITS`). The door has no control panel (no control shares its group); it is an automatic door, initially off, that `mission_tunnel` powers (`device_set_power tunnel_door_2 1`) after it has seen the player in `tunnel_trigger_close` (all players), closed `tunnel_door_3` behind them, waited 45 ticks and for that door to stand more than 0.25 open, for `game_all_quiet` and for the `tunnel_2` conversation (each wait at most `delay_dawdle`, 10 s) and 4 s more. Powered, the door opens for a body within 0.6 units of its origin, where the walker waits (0.08 units away on this route). A static trace (batch 5) settles the `tunnel_door_3` wait: the door is a blast door (`door blast`, machine kind 1, which the engine, like Halo, never opens on approach) that `device_set_position` closes at its powered rate, 0.0024 a tick (about 14 s from open to shut), so 45 ticks after the script starts closing it, it stands near 0.89 and the wait passes at once. No reopening is needed, and the route needs no detour back to it. The route enters `tunnel_trigger_close` 123 steps before the door row and stays in it to the door (the importer now checks this, `DOOR_PREREQUISITES`, and records it in the section's metrics), so on a walk carried in from section 1 `tunnel_door_2` should be powered about 25 s after the route enters the volume. The engine's automatic-door rule (`Scenario::updateDevices`) already asks a kind-0 door to open every fourth tick while the player is inside its radius, powered or not; unpowered it cannot move, and it starts to open on the tick the power arrives, so the walker standing at the door needs nothing more (read from the code, not seen). The last walk stopped there because it started the script at section 3, where `mission_a10` waits for the bridge scene (section 2) and never wakes `mission_tunnel`; a walk from section 1 (or 2) does. The walker logs the door's power and position every 20 s with `halo_script_status`. Expected to pass on a walk from section 1, pending that walk; still in `KNOWN_BLOCKED`. A section 5 started on its own used to stay there (no script powered the door); since gap 6 of batch 5's critic its fresh start brings the script forward (`freshScripts` in `source/src/halo_script.cpp`, below), so it is expected to pass that way too, pending a walk.
- Section 6 is reached only through section 5, so it has been walked only on its own, where `cryo_search` (woken by the mission script after the bridge) never opens `cryo_door_2`. `cryo_search` opens it at once (`device_set_position_immediate cryo_door_2 1`) when it is woken, which a walk carried in from section 1 does, and device states carry across sections, so on that walk the door is open; the route waits there on a door row of its own. Expected to pass, pending a walk carried in from section 5, and (gap 6) on its own: its fresh start wakes `cryo_search` too.
- Sections started on their own (section select) begin the mission script afresh, so doors an earlier section's script opens stay shut. Every a10 section after the first is the exception (judgement calls, read statically from the decompiled scripts, not checked in Halo): sections 5 and 6 since gap 6 of batch 5's critic, sections 2 to 4 since the fresh-start batch (gap 2 of batch 6's critic), section 7 since that batch's final integration review. `freshScripts` in `source/src/halo_script.cpp`, next to the startup and the counterpart of `FreshProfile`, passes over the cryo exit (`fast_setup` and `tutorial_setup`, which would otherwise seat the player in section 1's `cryotube_1` far off the section's map, on Normal for the tutorial's minutes) and sets `mark_fast_setup`, which `mission_a10` waits for before it wakes the threads of sections 1 and 2 (`main_setup`, the cryo and containment threads, `mission_crossfire`, `mission_bridge`). Section 2 needs no more: `mission_bridge` plays the bridge scene and sets its flags in that section. Sections 3 to 7 also set the flags `mission_a10` waits for before it wakes their threads (the bridge scene's `mark_bridge_cutscene_start` and `mark_bridge_cutscene`, `mission_shoot`'s `mark_weapon` and `mark_shoot`), so `mission_a10` wakes `mission_airlock_1`, `mission_flank`, `mission_loop` and `mission_airlock_2` (section 3), `mission_knot` (section 4), `mission_final` (section 7: its encounters, `final_door_1`, and `x30` and `game_won` at `end_trigger`; the section's exit plays `x30` without it too, `finishSection`'s exit scene) and the rest at once, as it would after the bridge; sections 5 and 6 also wake `mission_tunnel` and `cryo_search` at once. Logs: `HaloScript: a10 section 2 started afresh: the script brought forward (1 globals set, 2 threads passed over, 0 woken)`, sections 3, 4 and 7 `(5 globals set, 2 threads passed over, 0 woken)`, sections 5 and 6 `(5 globals set, 2 threads passed over, 2 woken)`. **The tutorial on Normal (the fresh-start batch):** a walk from section 1 on Normal runs `tutorial_setup` on past the cryo lessons: after waking section 1's `tutorial_first_contact` it waits for `mark_bridge_cutscene` and wakes `tutorial_weapon` (section 2's firing prompt), sets `test_light` (`tutorial_light`'s flashlight prompt in section 5) and wakes `tutorial_motiontracker`, `tutorial_melee` (both in section 6) and `tutorial_grenade`. A fresh start on Normal (only: the other difficulties never wake `tutorial_setup`) resumes it there instead of passing it over (`resumeAfterWake`: its thread starts at the statement after its own top-level `wake`): section 2 after `(wake tutorial_first_contact)`, sections 3 to 7 after `(wake tutorial_weapon)`, so section 2's lesson stays in section 2 (log: `HaloScript: a10 section 3 started afresh on difficulty 1: tutorial_setup resumed after its statement 65 (wake tutorial_weapon)`; statement 63 for section 2). This changes sections 5 and 6 on Normal too, which gap 6 had passed over the whole tutorial: `tutorial_motiontracker` powers off `motiontracker_door_2` and `_3` and `tutorial_melee` swaps the open `melee_door_1` for `melee_door_2`, so on Normal the way through section 6 is by `motiontracker_door_1` and the melee door, as on a Normal walk from section 1, not by `motiontracker_door_2`, which section 6's route passes (0.08 units from its row 93). The route walker walks on Heroic by default (`tests/test_retail_collision.py --difficulty`), where none of this runs. **The melee door (the fresh-start batch):** `melee_door_2` is placed "opened by melee attack" (scenario machine flag bit 3, the `.hscb` device attributes' bit 8) and initially off, and it stands at 0.8325 (`device_set_position_immediate`), which in its 120-frame animation is still shut (its leaves swing between frames 99 and 119); `tutorial_melee` waits for it to reach 1.0. In Halo a melee whose nearest object is such a machine puts it fully open at once, powered or not (`machine_try_to_open_with_damage`, `device_set_actual_position 1.0`, read from the Halo CE source port in `../Halo3DS`: `units/units.c`, `devices/device_machines.c`). The engine did nothing with a melee on a machine, so a Normal game would have stopped there. Now the player's melee, when it meets no actor, traces its reach (4 world units from the eye along the aim) through the machines; the nearest one hit, if it is placed so and no structure stands nearer, is put fully open (`Scenario::meleeDevice` via `haloscenariomeleedevice`, called from `halomelee` in `source/src/weapon.cpp`; log `Halo: a melee opened melee_door_2`). `melee_door_2` is the only machine so placed in the ten missions (read from the `.hscb` device attributes). Not verified in the game; `tests/test_starting_profiles.py` items 7 and 8 check the fresh starts and the melee door (not run). a10 has no `player_location_index` (that is a50's). The other missions keep the plain fresh start; a10 section 1 is untouched. A saved section continued from the title menu now resumes the script that was saved with it (progress file version 3, see Title menu and saved games), including the script carried in from earlier sections. This predates retail collision: stock geometry collides with the same doors. Playing on from section 1 carries the script state across, as the test does.
- `halo_b30_01` and `halo_b30_02` kept the render-raster route of the Silent Cartographer work (`RASTER_ROUTES`) until the route batch; they are planned walks now, on the same layout (notes on b30, below), so every retail-collision section has a planned route and `RASTER_ROUTES` is empty. `halo_b30_03`, the return, is walked since batch 5.

d40's `elevator` is a car: a 0.2-unit floor slab in a frame that reaches 2.4 units above the deck and 2.7 below it, and it rises 101 units up the engine room shaft. As one box it filled the car, so neither the player nor the route could stand in it (enc5_3, which raises it, waits for every player to be inside). `tools/halo_devices.py` now keeps only the slab of a machine that travels more than 4 units with a deck that has a slab under it (a downward face as large, at most 0.55 below) and a frame more than a unit above it; no other retail machine matches, so only `d40.hscb` changes (regenerate it with `import_halo_scripts.py "$maps" --mission d40`). The route planner then counts the elevator as a lift platform. A lift collides as one box per platform. c10's lifts have a rim 0.2 units above their deck, which put the box top 0.46 units above the landings, more than a step or a jump; `tools/halo_devices.py` now tops a lift's box at its deck (the upward face with the most area) when the two differ by more than 0.1 units. b40's lifts (0.03) are unchanged. The Library's (c20) platforms are two boxes, a 0.4-unit deck over a 1.13-unit turntable, so the rule leaves them as they are; the importer now counts a lift as any panel set under 1.2 units thick that travels more than 4.

A BSP switch half way along a lift shaft ends the section there, and the next section puts the player at its start on the landing, so the rest of the ride never happens. The engine counts the volumes on the vertical line from the switch point to the start's height as entered by the player for the first second of the next section (`Scenario::noteRide`, logged as `Halo trigger: <volume> passed on the ride`); c20's `section7_trigger`, above `bsp 2,3` in the platform3 shaft, which `section6` waits for before floor 4's encounters and doors, is the case that needs it.

Notes on b30 (The Silent Cartographer): section 3, the return (BSP 0 again, on section 1's projection, so its `.hcol` is still section 1's and the script projections are unchanged), is a planned retail route since batch 5, no longer the authored raster layout, which ended 37.6 units from its end anchor after 53 steps. It starts where the player comes out of the shaft (`bsp 0,1`, the anchor it had) and walks 170 steps, through no scripted door, to the ledge beside the extraction Pelican's rear ramp (`SECTION_EXITS`: a retail point, 1.5 units' reach), which is its end marker: the section ends when the player boards the Pelican there with E once it hovers (`halopelicanready`), as before. Judgement calls: the gap's plan named `player0_map_done` as the start, but that flag is the Cartographer room, in BSP 1 (section 2), where the scene leaves the player; section 3 begins where section 2's walk out of the facility arrives. It named `beach_lz` as the end, but that is the insertion beach 100 units west (the mission table's end volume); the Pelican hovers at the ledge, so the route ends there. No script waits for a volume on the way (the engine brings the Pelican, `EngineApproach`), and the route passes neither `shafta_lock_door` nor another scripted door, so it has no `SECTION_OBJECTIVES`. The walker presses use at the route's end until the section completes (`RETAIL_QA interact`). Not walked; to put the return back on the raster layout, add `('b30', 2)` to `RASTER_ROUTES` and re-run `python3 tools/import_halo_campaign.py "$maps" --mission b30 --part 3`.

Notes on b30, sections 1 and 2 (the route batch, gap 7 of batch 5's critic; not walked, unverified): both are planned walks on retail collision now, no longer the authored raster route (`RASTER_ROUTES` is empty), on their old layouts: no anchor or objective moves either section's map window or origin, so their `.hcol` and `.mesh` files, `b30.hscb` (re-imported, byte for byte the same) and section 3's projection (section 1's) are unchanged, and `tests/test_b30_retail_collision.py`, which walks its own waypoints on section 2's layout (`tests/data/b30-collision-qa-route.txt`), is the regression check of the layout the planned route shares. Section 1 (BSP 0) walks 1,010 steps from the landing zone to the shaft A entrance (`bsp 0,1`, 1.1 units from its middle; it was 981 raster steps ending 7 units off). Its route passes no scripted door: `shafta_lock_door` is beyond `bsp 0,1`, in BSP 1, and nothing on the beach waits for a volume before the switch. Section 2 (BSP 1) is entered and left through `bsp 1,0` at the top of shaft A, which the engine holds back until the map is done (`Scenario::switchBsp`, `cartographerMapped`): it walks 3,301 steps from `bsp 1,0` past `shaftA_window` and `shaftA_slam`, through `shafta_lock_door` on a door row of its own (1024, `DOOR_WAITS`; the door starts open and only scripts close it, a DoorBlocks closable door, so it gets a row only because the section is in `DOOR_WAITS_CLOSABLE`; the engine opens it at the section's start and holds `mission_shafta_lock`, `Scenario::startCartographer`, so the row should pass at once), down through `shaftA_unlocked`, `_beam`, `_u`, `_jig`, `_mind`, `_ramp` and `_control` to `shaftA_switch`, where it uses `shafta_map_control` (a 256 row naming `holohalo_1`, the holo ring that `cutscene_map` sets to 1, so the row counts as done once the scene has played; `SECTION_OBJECTIVES`), and from `player0_map_done`, where the scene leaves the player, back up the same way to `bsp 1,0` (1.2 units from its middle). It passes neither `shafta_killer_trigger` (whose `damage_object` kills the player) nor `shaftA_ledge_trigger` (the ledge scene's volume). A door without a script name on the way (on the beam level) opens on approach and gets no row: the walker reads a door's position by its name (judgement call: such doors never get a door row, in any section). The plan's "shaft A doors" are that one door: the adapted approach skips shaft B's security detour (`cutscene_shaftb_goal`, which would unlock it), so no other door gates the descent. To put a section back on its raster route, add it to `RASTER_ROUTES` (and for section 2 take it out of `DOOR_WAITS`, `DOOR_WAITS_CLOSABLE` and `SECTION_OBJECTIVES`) and re-run `python3 tools/import_halo_campaign.py "$maps" --mission b30 --part N`.

Notes on a30 (Halo): the first section, the Crash Site (`halo_a30`, the engine's first a30 section), is imported from the retail BSP since batch 6 (`CRASH_SITE` in `tools/import_halo_campaign.py`), like the other sections: BSP 0 on a uniform projection of its own (its `section_layout`: the outdoor scale, 4.1, for a window 65 units round the lifepod and the end), with an `.hcol`, a walked route and metrics (`python3 tools/import_halo_campaign.py "$maps" --mission a30 --part 0`; a full import includes it, before a30's other sections). Its tables use `CRASH_SITE_KEY`, since `('a30', 0)` is `halo_a30_01`. It starts at the scenario's player start, the lifepod (since the use-aware rule's batch just outside the pod's open rear end, `SECTION_ANCHORS`, as the pod now collides and the engine's body does not fit inside: Scenery collision, Scripted scenery by use), and ends in `first_marine_extraction`, where the authored layout ended too (the end marker; `halo_a30_01` then starts in front of the first structure). The walk climbs the lifepod's valley through `lz_pass` and `pass_mouth` and reaches the marines' basin only down two rocky slopes too steep to stand on, 2.1 units down over 1.5 at the valley's north end and 1.5 down into the basin, which Halo's player walks down (harmless falls) and the engine's body slides down: the importer's `SLIDES` lets those ledge drops (`LEDGE_DROPS`, 3 units) go down a slope instead of a vertical face. 1,115 steps, ending inside the volume (7.8 units from its centre). No walking assist is needed. The authored layout ran on a non-uniform projection (2.75 across, 2.0 up), which kept it on stock geometry with no route metrics. Judgement calls: the authored map's actors (the_last_stand's wolf squad, lz_search's pass squads) are left to the mission script, as in the other imported sections (the map has no actor spawns), and its stand-in boxes give way to the scenario's equipment; the scenery (pines, boulders and the crashed lifepod, 230 of the scenario's 586 placements, those over the route's floor; 229 of 585 since the use-aware rule's batch left out `intro_tree`) stays as mapmodels, exported at the new scale (`write_scenery`; a model too big for an MD3 at that scale gets a larger `mdlscale`, which leaves its size unchanged), standing on the native cube floor under it with a zero z offset. Since the scenery batch the scenery collides as its real shapes (`SCENERY_COLLISION`; Scenery collision, above): 263 placements of BSP 0 in the map window are in the `.hcol`, the 35 of them no mapmodel draws (no cube floor under them, or no mapmodel slot: the small granite boulders) are drawn in the mesh, and the mapmodels only draw. The tracked `packages/maps/halo_a30.cfg` keeps its texture and sky slots; its mapmodel lines now have zero radius and height (no collision cylinder), which the importer checks before it writes the map; the route goes round and over the real polygons (1,136 steps, one slide, ending inside `first_marine_extraction` 7.8 units from its centre). Without scenery collision the lines carry `CRASH_SITE_MAPMODELS`' sizes (the authored sizes times 1.5 across and 2.05 up) and the route goes round the cylinders (`SceneryBlocks`). `tools/import_halo_a30.py` now writes only the Crash Site's textures and sky; its map, mesh and scenery models come from the campaign importer. Regenerate `a30.hscb` with the map (`python3 tools/import_halo_scripts.py "$maps" --mission a30`): its Crash Site projection follows the layout. Not walked or played in the game yet (`PENDING_WALK`; `tests/test_retail_collision.py a30` now starts at the Crash Site, `--section a30:0`). To go back to the authored map: set `CRASH_SITE = False`, restore `halo_a30.cfg`'s mapmodel lines from git (z offset -64, the 2.75-scale sizes), delete `packages/maps/halo_a30.hcol`, `.route` and `.metrics.json`, and re-run `python3 tools/import_halo_a30.py "$maps/a30.map"` and `python3 tools/import_halo_scripts.py "$maps" --mission a30`; `package-macos.sh`, `ci-macos.sh` and the route gate follow the switch. The two imported sections, `halo_a30_01` and `halo_a30_02` (engine sections 2 and 3), get a `.hcol` and a route but have not been walked in the game yet. `tests/test_retail_collision.py a30` starts at the Crash Site since batch 6 and walks on into `halo_a30_01`. Batch 5 re-planned `halo_a30_01` (BSP 0): its start, the centre of `first_structure_front`, is 7.5 units above the floor, and the floor node nearest it was a 133-cell fragment (a rock top), so the route stopped after 22 steps (reach 0.01%). The section now starts on the valley floor in front of the first structure, (2.79, 48.89, 63.84) (`SECTION_ANCHORS`), and walks 1,281 steps down the valley. Its BSP switch, `bsp 0,1` at x -338, is 165 units beyond the section's map window, and the cave floor round `cave_floor_entrance` (z 35) is cut off from the valley by a 5-unit gap over a 4-unit trench, beyond any jump or ledge drop the planner allows (checked up to 4-unit drops), so the route ends on the ledge above it, 16.9 units from the volume's centre (`SECTION_EXITS`, a retail point with 2.5 units' reach), where the section ends at its end marker as it did before (halo.cpp's stage machine; the stock layout ended at the cave floor). Judgement call: BSP 0 between the cave and `bsp 0,1` stays skipped, as it was; taking it in would mean a map window twice as wide at half the scale. Its origin is now the middle of its height range (`MIDDLE_ORIGIN`: the 28-unit drop to the ledge is at the edge of the native cube heights about the start), so its projection changed: regenerate `a30.hscb` (`python3 tools/import_halo_scripts.py "$maps" --mission a30`) with the map.

Notes on c10 (343 Guilty Spark): all seven sections get a `.hcol` and a route (section 5 since the c10 section 5 batch); none has been walked in the game yet. Regenerate the script projections after the maps (`import_halo_scripts.py "$maps" --mission c10`): sections 2, 3, 5 and 6 changed their projection (section 3 in batch 6, section 6 in the scenery batch, section 5 in the c10 section 5 batch). The interior has two storeys (z 0 and 1.9 in interior A) and crosses pits on light bridges that the player extends from control panels (modelled now, see above). Since the c10 section 5 batch every c10 section has an `.hcol` and a route, so a playthrough from section 1 walks on through section 5 (it used to stop there, on stock geometry); sections 6 and 7 can still be walked on their own with `--section c10:6` and `--section c10:7`, which begin the mission script afresh (section 6's `mission_int_b` is then not at hall G; the route needs none of its waits). Section 5's projection changed with its new start, so `c10.hscb` was regenerated. Sections 3, 5 and 6 carry their scenery's collision (`SCENERY_COLLISION`):

- Section 1 (BSP 0, the first swamp) walks through `enc_swamp2`, which `mission_swamp_a` waits for before it calls `lift_a` up, to the top of the lift beside `lift_a_cont_a`, and ends with a deck row at that control, which stands on the deck's corner: the walker boards, uses it, and rides `lift_a` down (no call: the script brings the lift). `bsp 0,1` is half way down the shaft. Still in `KNOWN_BLOCKED`; expected to pass, pending a walk.
- Section 2 (BSP 1, from the bottom of `lift_a`) is new on retail collision, not yet walked. It starts on the landing east of the lift's deck (the section used to start at `bsp 1,0`, half way up the shaft, and its route began on the top landing), walks the upper hall and drops 1.9 units into the lower hall beside `bridge_a` (`LEDGE_DROPS`; `bridge_a` is retracted and extended only from its far side), and walks the lower storey to `bsp 1,2`. The route passes `post_lifta_bashed_2`, the bashed door the script creates there only after x50; in this section the open `post_lifta_door_2` stands there. To put the section back on stock geometry, add `('c10', 1)` to `STOCK_SECTIONS` and run `python3 tools/import_halo_campaign.py "$maps" --mission c10 --part 2` (it deletes the `.hcol` and `.route`).
- Section 3 (BSP 2, the control room and the x50 scene) is on retail collision since batch 6, over two walking assists (`ASSISTS`); not walked yet. Two volumes are named `bsp 1,2` (and `bsp 2,1`): the lower doorway (z -0.5) and the upper one (z 1.9). Before x50 the way in is the lower one (`post_helmet_door_4`, the upper doorway's door, is off until `ini_post_helmet_replace` powers it after x50), so the section starts in `bsp 2,1#0` and ends in `bsp 2,1#1` (`SECTION_ANCHORS`, `SECTION_EXITS`). `mission_control` waits for `bayb_trigger` and then `control_lab_trigger`, where x50 plays and leaves the player at `player0_playon_c10` (`SECTION_OBJECTIVES`). BSP 2's floors fall into three parts: hall C (the lower doorway and its sunken channel), hall D's lower floor, and everything else (the upper storey, bay B, the lab). Hall C's way on is the platform over the channel's south end in front of `post_helmet_door_2` (x -18.65 to -17.4, y -34.2 to -35.6, at the walkways' height and 1.0 above the channel floor and its central trench); the walkways either side are 1.6 units from it across the channel's slope, a level running jump for Halo's player (2.9) and beyond the engine's (1.3), and the slope's struts stand between them except just north of the platform. Beyond the door, hall D's lower floor meets the raised floor that leads up the V-shaped ramp to bay B only at a bench whose tops stand 0.70 and 0.80 above that floor (the ramp lands on the 0.80 one), a crouch jump in Halo (0.89; a standing jump's 0.69 falls just short of either) and beyond the engine's climb. The assists: a level plank from the west walkway across the channel to the platform's north-west corner (x -20.39 to -18.32, y -33.14 to -34.08, z 0.008, just under the floors' 0.013) and a 32-degree ramp from hall D's floor onto the bench (x -13.95 to -12.95, y -54.33 to -55.59, z 0.006 to 0.789). The route (1,359 steps, 93% of the floor reached, ending in the upper doorway 2.1 units from its centre) crosses both, passes post_helmet_door_2 (an automatic door, which the planner reports as the bashed `post_helmet_bashed_2` the script puts there after x50, as section 2's does), reaches bay B and the lab, and after x50 walks out of the control room past `control_door_bashed_a` (over its threshold, a two-cell jump) to the upper doorway, where it waits for `post_helmet_door_4`. Judgement calls: the channel's crates (`c_uplink`, a tilted `c_storage`) give Halo's player a second way onto the platform; the plank stands for the running jump. Since the scenery batch the section's 23 scenery placements collide (`SCENERY_COLLISION`) and the route was re-planned with them (1,359 steps again, ending in the upper doorway 2.1 units from its centre; route, map, mesh and `.hcol` regenerated). With them, the tilted `c_storage` leaning into the channel beside the platform's east edge makes a walkable slope from the channel floor up to an exposed strip of its top 0.27 wide at z -0.46, 0.47 below the platform (a standing jump in Halo), and hall D's fallen b40 metal makes a first step 0.29 up, from which the bench is 0.41 higher: Halo links through real scenery, but each still leaves a climb above the engine's 0.36, so an assist is needed either way. Both assists stay as they were (judgement call: the plank and the ramp stand on retail floors and do not depend on the tilted scenery's pose); without them there is no route. Revert switch: delete `ASSISTS[('c10', 2)]`, add `('c10', 2)` to `STOCK_SECTIONS` and re-run `import_halo_campaign.py "$maps" --mission c10 --part 3` and `import_halo_scripts.py "$maps" --mission c10`.
- Section 4 (BSP 1, back from the control room) walks through the doors `ini_post_helmet_replace` powers after the x50 scene (`post_lifta_door_1`, `post_helmet_door_5`, the bashed `post_helmet_door_3`) to the landing beside `lift_b_cont_a` and ends with a deck row at that control; `bsp 1,3` is half way down the shaft. `KNOWN_BLOCKED`; expected to pass, pending a walk.
- Section 5 (BSP 3, from the bottom of `lift_b`) is on retail collision since the c10 section 5 batch (after the final integration review), over one walking assist (`ASSISTS`); not walked yet. It starts on the landing at the bottom of `lift_b` (`SECTION_ANCHORS`: `(-18.0, 23.4, -37.9)`; the default start, `bsp 3,1`, is near the top of the shaft) and ends in the lower BSP 3/4 doorway (`TRANSITIONS`: `bsp 3,4` #0, z -40.85 to -38.13; #1 is the upper doorway's, z -38.1 to -37.1, whose `bsp3_device_15` and `_16` are off). The lower storey's own way east, `bsp3_device_13` and `_14`, is off too (placement flag 2) and nothing powers them, so the way from the landing to the lower doorway is over the upper storey beyond `bridge_c`. The route does not cross `bridge_c` (`UNCROSSED_BRIDGES`). Decompiled (batch 6), `flicker_bridge` is a continuous script that is never stopped: `(sleep_until (= (structure_bsp_index) 3) 1)`, `(device_set_position bridge_c 0)`, `(sleep (random_range 15 75))`, `(device_set_position bridge_c (real_random_range 0.5 1))`, `(sleep (random_range 15 75))`. It moves the bridge's position, not its power or permutation (no script names `bridge_c` otherwise; its control, `bridge_c_cont_b`, is unusable, flag 16, and unpowered, `bridge_c_power` 0), and the deck is the machine's four panels, whose collision boxes follow the animation (the tag is "bridge fast", 0.0667 a tick): two halves that slide in from each end, leaving a gap of 8.5 (1 - position) units. From the start of an extension to the next retraction is at most 90 ticks (3 s); the 8.5-unit crossing takes 3.8 s at Halo's run (2.25 units a second), so Halo's player could only make it riding the panels as they move, which is not confirmed, and an assist there would be a permanent bridge, a link Halo does not have (the batch 6 finding, which kept the section stock until now; with the bridge the planner found a 490-step route). Instead the route goes round by the debris pile in the lower storey's west bay (x -47 to -44, y 35 to 41), which the section's scenery collision (`SCENERY_COLLISION`, 38 placements) completes now that `euler_rows` is Halo's own placement rotation (the final integration review). The pile is the BSP's own rubble (a block whose top is at z -39.20, 0.80 above the lower floor's -40.00, with a chamfer from -39.30 round it and a tier at -39.10), a fallen b40 metal sheet (-39.09 to -38.97), tilted `c_storage` crates (one tilted 40 degrees, its top -38.73 to -38.92), `c_uplink`s and a `c_storage_large`, and three invisible two-sided `door_blast_collision` planes: one a 28-degree ramp (normal z 0.88) from the metal sheet's height (-38.98) up to the upper storey's balcony (-38.29; the balcony floor is -38.31, y 34.2 to 35.7), one a 46-degree plane (normal z 0.70, walkable, unused) over the pile's west side up to the west wall (-39.30 to -38.22), one too steep to stand on (a guard). So from the rubble's top the pile is a single walkable slope up to the balcony (the planner's floors join it in steps of at most 0.16), and the only climb beyond the engine's jump is the first, from the lower floor: 0.70 to 0.72 onto the rubble's north chamfer, or 0.46 onto the `c_storage` lying on its side at the pile's foot (scenario scenery 1495, yaw 90, pitch -72.9, roll 90: a flat top at -39.53 to -39.54, x -46.43 to -45.63, y 39.62 to 40.28, bevelled out to x -45.47 at -39.60), from which the chamfer is 0.28 higher and 0.375 across. 0.46 is a standing jump in Halo (0.69; the crouch jump's 0.89 is not needed), and 0.28 is within the engine's own jump (0.36 at the indoor scale; the planner's limit, `JUMP_CLIMB`, is 0.28 here). The assist: a 28-degree ramp from the floor east of the crate onto its top (x -44.85 to -45.72, y 39.70 to 40.10, z -39.994 to -39.536; its upper corners on the crate's flat top and east bevel). The scenery batch's figures for the stack (floor to crate top 0.72 to 0.84, then 0.38 to 0.50 to the balcony) were measured with the old rotation, which left the crates and the planes in the wrong pose; with Halo's rotation the tilted pieces rest on the rubble and the planes meet the sheet and the balcony. The route (1,101 steps; 84.6% of the floor reached): from the landing, a 1.9-unit drop to the lower storey (`LEDGE_DROPS` 2.1), west along it to the bay, up the ramp onto the crate, the 0.28 hop onto the rubble (route flag 2), south over the rubble's top and the metal sheet and up the invisible ramp onto the balcony, east along the upper storey past the far end of `bridge_c` (its automatic doors open on approach; the route waits at no scripted door), a 1.9-unit drop to the lower storey beside the lower doorway, and into `bsp 3,4` #0, ending 1.9 units from its centre. Judgement calls: the debris pile, with an invisible ramp laid up its side, reads as Bungie's designed way up for a player who finds `bridge_c` flickering; the climb onto the crate is read from the scenery placement and the biped's jump, not seen in Halo; the ramp stands for the smallest Halo jump that reaches the pile (0.46, a standing jump), not for the 0.72 climb onto the rubble. `c10.hscb` was regenerated (the section's projection follows its new start). Revert switch: add `('c10', 4)` to `STOCK_SECTIONS` (and, to plan across the bridge again, delete its `UNCROSSED_BRIDGES` entry) and re-run `import_halo_campaign.py "$maps" --mission c10 --part 5` (on stock geometry it deletes the `.hcol` and `.route`) and `import_halo_scripts.py "$maps" --mission c10`.
- Section 6 (BSP 4, from the lower BSP 4/3 doorway to `lift_d`) is on retail collision since the scenery batch, over two walking assists (`ASSISTS`); not walked yet. It starts in the lower doorway (`TRANSITIONS`: `bsp 4,3#0`; the upper doorway's doors, `bsp3_device_15` and `_16`, `bsp4_device_13` and `_15`, are off and nothing powers them), walks the lower storey north past `last_door_a` (an automatic door, which `mission_int_b` replaces with the half-open `last_bashed_a` once the player reaches hall G: the route passes it before) and climbs to the upper storey up the fallen debris under the upper floor's broken edge (x 6.4 to 9, y 79 to 85.8). The debris top (z -39.20, x 7.1 to 9.0, y 80.6 to 83.5) is 0.80 above the lower floor (-40.00), and the broken edge has a notch (z -38.50, x 5.8 to 6.4, y 82.3 to 82.9) 0.70 above the debris and 0.30 below the upper floor (-38.20): two crouch jumps in Halo (0.89; standing, 0.69, falls short of both), beyond the engine's 0.36. The assists: a 36-degree ramp from the floor south of the debris onto its top (x 8.2 to 8.8, y 79.6 to 80.75) and a 35-degree ramp from the debris top west into the notch (x 7.35 to 6.35, y 82.42 to 82.85); the 0.30 step from the notch is the engine's own jump. The route then crosses `bridge_e` (extended from `brdige_e_cont_a`, at the hall G end: route flag 32) to the `lift_d` room and ends on the lift's deck at `lift_d_cont_a` (`SECTION_EXITS`, `LIFT_RIDES`): the walker calls the lift from the landing (no script moves it), boards, uses the control and rides up; `bsp 4,5` is half way up the shaft (889 steps, ending 1.4 units from the control). The direct climbs batch 6 measured (1.31 with 0.4 across, 1.0 with 1.9 across) stay out of reach; the debris climb was found when the section was looked at again with its scenery (its two placements, a crate and a field generator in hall G, are not on the way). Judgement calls: the debris climb is read from the BSP, not seen in Halo; a Flood-broken floor with debris piled under it, behind a door the script bashes in, reads as the designed way up. `c10.hscb` was regenerated (the section's projection follows its new start and end). Revert switch: add `('c10', 5)` to `STOCK_SECTIONS` and re-run `import_halo_campaign.py "$maps" --mission c10 --part 6` and `import_halo_scripts.py "$maps" --mission c10`.
- Section 7 (BSP 5, the second swamp) now starts on the landing at the top of `lift_d` (it used to start half way up the shaft, in `bsp 5,4`, and failed to import) and ends in `monitor_trigger`, where `mission_swamp_b`'s last fight and the extraction play. The mission's end used to be `enc_swamp2`, a volume in the first swamp; the section's projection changed.

Notes on c20 (The Library): all four sections get a `.hcol` and a route from the importer; none has been walked in the game yet. Regenerate the script projections after the maps (`import_halo_scripts.py "$maps" --mission c20`): every section's projection changed. The Library climbs four floors (-4.6, 11.4, 27.4 and 43.4) on `platform1` to `platform3`, which the `platforms` script raises while the player stands on one. The floor doors open through their device groups once the Monitor's command lists and the fights are done, and the routes walk the volumes the scripts wait for, in order (`SECTION_OBJECTIVES`):

- Section 1 (BSP 0, floor 1) walks the Monitor's chain (`monitor1_2`, `enc3_trigger`, `monitor1_4` to `monitor1_6`, `floor1_door1_trigger`), through `floor1_door1` and `section2`, and ends beside `platform1` (within 14.5 units of its centre: the corridors meet the round shaft room on the diagonals, where the platform's square box reaches 14 units out). `bsp 0,1` is half way up the shaft.
- Section 2 (BSP 1, floor 2) starts in `section3_trigger` on the landing, passes `enc3_4_trigger` (`floor2_door1` opens 48 s later), `enc3_6_trigger` (`monitor2_5` opens `floor2_door2` after the Monitor's lists and a Flood wave) and `section4_trigger`, and loops east through `monitor2_10` and `monitor2_11` to `platform2`. Without those two the loop, past x 70, was outside the projection and the route stopped 25 units short.
- Section 3 (BSP 2, floor 3) starts in `section5_trigger` and passes `enc5_2_trigger`, `enc5_5_trigger` (`floor3_door2` opens after its counter, 40 s in the volume), `enc5_6_trigger` (`floor3_door3`), `section6_trigger`, `enc6_3_trigger` (`floor3_door4` to 0.6) and `enc6_4_trigger` (`floor3_door4` shuts behind, `floor3_door5` opens after a wave) to `platform3`. `floor3_door1` never opens.
- Section 4 (BSP 3, floor 4) starts on the landing west of the top of `platform3` (`section7_trigger` is passed on the ride, see above), passes `enc7_4_trigger`, `enc7_5_trigger` (`floor4_door2`'s counter) and `enc7_9_trigger` (`floor4_door3`) and ends in `finale`, on the `index_platform`.
- Sections 1 to 3 end beside a platform, with a deck row at its centre: the walker steps onto the deck and stands there until the platform rises through the BSP switch. At its lowest position a platform's deck box is 0.38 units above the landing (it is flush at the top), which at these sections' scales (6.9, 5.1 and 5.3) is more than the step height and close to the jump height; the engine now lets a grounded body step onto a lift deck from up to 0.45 units below it. `tests/test_retail_collision.py` still lists them in `KNOWN_BLOCKED`; expected to pass, pending a walk.

Notes on c40 (Two Betrayals): sections 2, 3 and 5 get a `.hcol` and a route from the importer; none has been walked in the game yet. Sections 1 and 4 get a `.hcol` and are flown (below). Regenerate the script projections after the maps (`import_halo_scripts.py "$maps" --mission c40`): sections 1, 3 and 5 changed their projection. The mission flies a Banshee between the pulse generators; the sections that only a flight leaves are `DRIVEN_SECTIONS` and `FLOWN_SECTIONS` in `tools/import_halo_campaign.py` (an `.hcol`, no walking route, the raster's spawns and markers as on stock geometry). `tests/test_retail_collision.py` skips them, as it does the Maw's run, so a playthrough from section 1 starts its walks at section 2; walk the others with `--section c40:2`, `--section c40:3` and `--section c40:5`, which begin the mission script afresh. **Not flown in the game yet.** To put sections 1 and 4 back on stock geometry, add `('c40', 0)` and `('c40', 3)` to `STOCK_SECTIONS` (it wins over `DRIVEN_SECTIONS`, and section 1's map is then laid out without the generator, `FLOWN_EXTENTS`) and re-run `python3 tools/import_halo_campaign.py "$maps" --mission c40` and `python3 tools/import_halo_scripts.py "$maps" --mission c40`; the engine then stops treating them as flown (`flownSection` asks for retail collision):

- Section 1 (BSP 2, the control room to the c bridge) is flown. Its exit, `door_c1` into BSP 10, is on the c bridge at z 70, and the walk down from the control room reaches only the canyon floor (z 62 at its highest near the bridge). The way up is `fly_away_1` to pulse generator 1 (BSP 12, z 104) or `lift_d` in the c1 tower (BSP 11), and neither BSP is a campaign section. `door_c1` is also unpowered from the start (`e1_a`) until `pulse_1_trigger` (`e7_c`). The section's map now reaches 20 units past `pulse_1_trigger`, `e7_trigger_b` and `e8_trigger` (8 units beyond BSP 2's edge, `FLOWN_EXTENTS`), so the player walks down to `fly_away_1` in the canyon, flies up into `pulse_1_trigger` (BSP 12 has no collision here: the generator's platform is not there, the volume is entered in the air, and `e7_c` powers `door_c1`), lands on the c bridge and walks through `door_c1` into the BSP switch to section 2. The raster's route (142 steps) still ends 90 units from that exit, as the stock layout's did (43 steps, 87 units): its end marker is not where the section ends. (That describes the old raster layout; the planned route is under Planned vehicle routes. Scenery: since the d40 section 3 batch the importer also scans BSP 12, the generator room it holds (`EXTRA_BSPS`), for scenery placements; it has one in the map window, a `b40_doorblinker` light with no collision model, so nothing is baked or drawn from it and the `.hcol`, mesh, map and route came out byte for byte the same: 120 placements, all from BSP 2. Not seen in the game.)
- Section 2 (BSP 10) walks from `bsp 10,2`, past `door_c1` (which `e20_a` powers off behind the player), to `bsp 10,1`.
- Section 3 (BSP 1) starts at the bottom of `lift_c` (BSP 8, not a campaign section), as before, so the upper storey of BSP 1 and its encounters (`e30_a` to `e43_c`) are skipped. It walks the canyon floor, past the bottom base (`e50_a_trigger`) and through the tunnel (`tunnel_trigger`, `waypoint5_trigger`), to `bsp 1,0`. It used to end at `waypoint5_trigger`, which left `bsp 1,0` outside its projection: the route could not reach the transition. Pulse generator 2 (BSP 6, z 87) is reached only by Banshee and is skipped, as it was on stock geometry.
- Section 4 (BSP 0, the canyon to the third generator) is flown. `bsp 0,5` is on the landing pad at z 33.6, which the canyon floor (z -11 to 1) reaches only by `c_banshee_1` or by `lift_a` in BSP 4 (the a1 tower) and `lift_b` down from its z 48 walkway. `tun_garage_2` and `tun_garage_3` on the way open only from their control panels, which the route planner does not model. `c_banshee_1` is the script's (an Elite flies it, `unit_set_enterable_by_player false`) and `e62_a_ban_1` and `_2` appear only at the main base, so the engine puts a Banshee of its own beside the player 2.5 s into the section (and again whenever no free Banshee stands in it); the player flies it up to the landing pad. Its layout is unchanged (706 raster steps).
- Section 5 (BSP 5) now starts in `bsp 5,0`, inside the landing pad door, where a player arriving from section 4 is, and ends in `pulse_3_trigger`. It used to start beside the generator, and its 27-step route stopped 6 units short of the volume. The `end` script that waits for `pulse_3_trigger` is woken by `e62_a` in section 4.

Notes on a50 (The Truth and Reconciliation): all five sections get a `.hcol` and a route from the importer; none has been walked in the game yet. Regenerate both the maps and the script projections after changing the a50 layout (`import_halo_campaign.py "$maps" --mission a50`, then `import_halo_scripts.py "$maps" --mission a50`). The layout changed with retail collision:

- Section 2 now starts on the gravity room pad (`gravity_teleport0_flag`, where `mission_area5` puts the player after the lift) instead of at `entr_safe` beside the hangar exit, so it covers the gravity room fight, the hall behind `grav_mus_hall_door`, the muster bay (through `muster_ledge_door`, then out by `muster_door_l1` once the wave is beaten) and the hangar hall. Its projection changed and now holds the gravity room.
- Section 3 starts in `bsp 1,2`, where section 2 leaves the player, and crosses the hangar to the control room door (`hangar_exit` and `hangar_lefthall_door` wait for `ini_hangar_hall_open`).
- Section 4 (the prison) is entered and left through one doorway. It starts in `bsp 2,3`, on the prison side (starting in `bsp 3,2` would switch straight back to BSP 2), walks through `control_door_d` to cell block D and up to `prison_d_switch`, which `mission_prison` waits for before the rescue scene, and walks back out to `bsp 3,2`. The engine does not perform the scene's player teleport (`ini_post_rescue`), so the return starts at the switch.
- Section 5 starts in `bsp 3,2` and ends at `shuttle_door_switch_trigger` beside the hangar door controls.

Notes on b40 (Assault on the Control Room): all seven sections get a `.hcol` and a route from the importer; none has been walked in the game yet. Regenerate the script projections after the maps (`import_halo_scripts.py "$maps" --mission b40`). The lifts between the mission's storeys are in BSPs that are not campaign sections (BSP 4 for `lift_a`, BSP 8 for `lift_c`), so the sections either side of them meet at the lift, except the c1 tower (BSP 11), which is one:

- Section 2 (BSP 0) now ends at the top of the a2 tower, past the first bridge, instead of aiming for `bsp 0,1` on the canyon floor. `lift_a` down the tower and the canyon floor beyond it (ext_a, the crevice and its garage doors) are in BSP 4 or behind it, so the route never got further than the bridge; it used to stop half way across. The section's map is smaller (scale 4.1, was 3.1) for both retail and stock geometry. Section 3 still starts in BSP 1 past `garagedoor_c`.
- Section 3 (BSP 1) ends at the bottom of the b3 lift and section 4 (BSP 10) starts at its top, as before. The route now ends inside the tower's bottom doorway, the first `bsp 1,8` (`SECTION_EXITS`), on BSP 1's side of `b3_bot_ent_door`; it used to aim for `bsp 1,10`, 48 units up at the top of the lift, and stopped beside its anchor, outside every exit volume (a stub). The b3 lift is `lift_c`, in BSP 8, which is not a campaign section: its collision is not in this section's `.hcol` and the engine does not run the lift here, so a `LIFT_RIDES` ride is not possible without making BSP 8 part of the section (judgement call: the exit fix). The engine ignores the switch to BSP 8 (it is not the next section's) and ends the section at its end marker, as before. Only the route and the end marker changed (the projection did not; 1,625 steps). Not walked yet.
- Section 6 (BSP 11, the c1 tower) is entered by the top doorway (`bsp 11,2#0`) and left by the bottom one (`bsp 11,2#1`); it used to start and end at the bottom one and failed to import. The route walks to `lift_d_control_a` at the top, where the player calls `lift_d` up and rides it down, and goes on from the bottom landing (route flag 4 on the first step after the ride). The deck row before that step is at the control, which stands on the deck; the engine puts the lift at the top at position 0, so the walker boards there, uses the control and rides down (it would call the lift first if it were elsewhere: no script moves it). The section's projection origin is the middle of its height range (`MIDDLE_ORIGIN`), since the 16-unit drop at the indoor scale is more than native cube heights hold below the start. `tests/test_retail_collision.py` still lists it in `KNOWN_BLOCKED`; expected to pass, pending a walk.
- Section 7 (BSP 2) starts at the bottom doorway (`bsp 2,11#1`), passes `ext_c_trigger_b` (which wakes `mission_ext_c` and powers `ext_c_ent_door`) and ends in `control_trigger`.
- The garage doors and the control room doors open from control panels that share their position group, which the route planner does not model (they count as sealed); no b40 route needs them.

Notes on d20 (Keyes): all six sections get a `.hcol` and a route from the importer; none has been walked in the game yet. Regenerate the script projections after the maps (`import_halo_scripts.py "$maps" --mission d20`): sections 2 and 6 changed their projection. BSPs 0, 3 and 4 overlap (the control room is in BSP 0 at the start and in BSP 4 for the captain scene), which is harmless since each section projects only its own BSP. The encounter scripts each wait for their volume and wake the next, so the routes walk those volumes in order (`SECTION_OBJECTIVES`):

- Section 1 (BSP 0) walks from the insertion to the hole Cortana tells the player to jump into. `0_to_1_transition_trigger` hangs in the shaft, 1 to 4.5 units below the floor, so the route ends at the edge and its last row (flag 8) is the volume's centre: the walker walks off and falls into it (`EXIT_FALLS`). `section1` then plays `cinematic_drop`.
- Section 2 (BSP 1, the canyon under the Truth and Reconciliation) now starts where `cinematic_drop` leaves the player, `player0_afterfall_base` in the coolant pool. It used to start in `grav_lift`, the volume round the gravity lift at the section's far end, and its 117-step route skipped the canyon. It walks `enc3_1` to `enc3_8b` (`enc3_2` is a detour east) and ends in `1_to_2_transition_trigger`, where `enc3_8` waits for the player to stand three seconds with no Covenant inside before `cinematic_lift`. The canyon floors step down 2.9 and 2.3 units on the way (two ledge drops).
- Section 3 (BSP 2) walks `section4`, `enc4_2`, `enc4_3`, `enc4_3c` (`enc4_3_door` opens once that fight is done), `enc4_5`, `enc4_6`, `enc4_8` and `enc4_8_spawner` (which opens `enc4_8_door`) to `bsp 2,3`. From the `enc4_6` walkway the only way down to the `enc4_7`/`enc4_8` corridor is a 2.5-unit drop; the route used to stop at the walkway, 15 units short of `bsp 2,3`.
- Section 4 (BSP 3) is unchanged: `bsp 3,2` to `bsp 3,4`.
- Section 5 (BSP 4, the control room) starts and ends in `bsp 4,3` and failed to import (its start was its exit). It walks `section6`, `enc6_2` and `enc6_3`, where `cinematic_captain` plays once no Flood is in `enc6_3_safe`, and back to `bsp 4,3` (armed once the player has left it). The scene's teleport to `player0_capt_done`, 1.6 units away, is a player `object_teleport`, which the engine does not perform.
- Section 6 (BSP 3, back through the hangar) walks `section7`, `enc7_1`, `enc7_2` (which brings in the Banshees) and `enc7_6` and ends in `enc7_6c` on the hangar floor, where the Banshees wait. The mission end used to be `hangar_exit_trigger`, a volume d20 does not have, so the route ended at the farthest actor; the section's projection changed.

Notes on d40 (The Maw): sections 1 to 5 get a `.hcol` and a route from the importer (section 3 since the d40 section 3 batch); none has been walked in the game yet. Sections 1, 2, 4 and 5 keep their projections; section 3's origin moved to its new start's height (2.02, from `bsp 2,1`'s 0.71: its window is clipped by the BSP's bounds either way), so `d40.hscb` was regenerated (`import_halo_scripts.py "$maps" --mission d40`; only that section's projection, four bytes, changed). A playthrough from section 1 no longer stops at section 3. Sections 6, 7 and 8 are driven (below; `tests/test_retail_collision.py` skips them):

- Section 1 (BSP 0) starts on the landing deck where the Banshee sets the player down (z 2) and walks through `section1` and `enc1_4`, which opens `enc1_4_door`, to `bsp 0,1`. The only way down to the bridge (z 0) is a 2-unit drop through the gap in the deck's walkway at x -40 (`LEDGE_DROPS`); without it the route stopped on the deck, 17.6 units short of the exit. The route used to jump through the locked hatch on the deck at (-49, 35.5), an unnamed manual door with no group, which is now a wall (`UNGROUPED_DOORS_SEALED`); it walks round by the open door at (-42.3, 34).
- Section 2 (BSP 1) walks `enc2_1`, `enc2_2` (which opens the Hunters' `enc2_1_door2`, on the way), `enc2_6`, `cinematic_bridge` (the x70 bridge scene, which `enc2_5` waits for once its Covenant are dead) and `enc2_6` again, which opens `enc2_6_door`, to `bsp 1,2`. The scene's teleport to `player0_playon_base`, 1.6 units from the volume, is a player `object_teleport`, which the engine does not perform.
- Section 3 (BSP 2) is on retail collision since the d40 section 3 batch (it was the last section on stock geometry; not walked yet). It starts on the upper level, on the walkway's floor at retail (-45.97, -16.31, 2.02) (`SECTION_ANCHORS`, exit `bsp 2,3`): the first row of the route it had on stock geometry, 9.75 units from `bsp 2,1`, so a player starts where they did before and no link is invented. Its walk is 169 plain rows on that level to `bsp 2,3` (no jump, crouch or door rows; ends in its exit, entry offset 0, exit offset 1.0), with its 13 scenery placements (1,121 surfaces). The lower floor round `bsp 2,1` stays unreachable, as it was on stock geometry (judgement call: a player coming from section 2 is put at the section's start, on the upper level, as before). Why the floors stay apart: the lower floor, entered at `bsp 2,1` (z 0), and the upper level (the grate deck at z 1.49 round `enc3_3b` and `enc3_4`, the walkway at x -49 rising to z 2, and the room with `enc3_5b`, `enc3_6`, `section4` and `bsp 2,3` at z 2) have no walkable connection in the structure collision or the render geometry. The grate deck does join the upper room, by ramps at x -49 north (y 5.5 to 7.6) and south (y -5.7 to -15), so the only gap is between the floors. The only floor in between is a burning beam at x -49 (0.24 units up in its middle, 0.79 at its ends, y -2.2 to 2.2). It was thought to end a crouch jump below the deck's edge (0.75); the structure BSP (batch 6) walls its ends in: at y 2.2 to 2.5 a parapet stands from the floor to z 2.79 along the north deck's edge, and along the east deck's edge (x -48.27 to -48.12) a curb and a railing rise to 1.74 and 2.49, open only at y 0 to 0.69 above the curb, 1.4 above the beam there. No jump Halo's biped makes (0.89 crouched) gets from the beam onto the deck, so the beam is not the link and gets no assist. Looked at again in the scenery batch, with its 13 scenery placements' collision (the section is in `SCENERY_COLLISION` and was planned off `STOCK_SECTIONS`): the fallen `h computer bank`s lie on the lower floor with their tops 0.30 to 0.72 up, and from each the nearest upper floor is 1.31 to 1.50 higher, beyond Halo's 0.89, so the scenery is not the link (the burnt banks and oxygen tanks stand on the upper floor). `enc3_3_door1` and `_2` are not manual: they are automatic, powered doors (attributes 0, placement flags 0) that open as the player comes near (`enc3_3` waits for either to stand at 0.9 and then places the infection forms behind it); they are not controls (no use rows), the planner already walks through them, and they join the lower floor only to itself. The one climb in Halo's envelope a static search finds is from the 45-degree chamfers at the foot of the ribbed walls either side of the grate deck (y about ±4.6 to ±5.5, the chamfers 0.3 wide on 0.4 centres, their tops 0.8 up) 0.81 up onto the deck's edge (z 1.39) 1.0 across: a crouch jump, if Halo's biped can stand on those chamfers, which is not settled statically, so no assist (judgement call: a link Halo may not have). What Halo's player does use stays open. No device links the floors: BSP 2 has no lift, and its machines are doors and a hatch panel (device 15, position group `0_3`) that sinks into the floor. To put it back on stock geometry, add `('d40', 2)` to `STOCK_SECTIONS` and re-run `import_halo_campaign.py "$maps" --mission d40 --part 3` and `import_halo_scripts.py "$maps" --mission d40`.
- Section 4 (BSP 3) is unchanged: `bsp 3,2` to `bsp 3,4`, past 47 weapon placements in the armory (the stock raster placed none).
- Section 5 (BSP 4, the engine room) now ends on the elevator, in `enc5_3` (`SECTION_EXITS`): `bsp 4,5` is 65 units up the shaft, and the route used to stop at the bottom 66 units from it. It walks `section5` and `enc5_1_main`, then for each manifold in turn (s1, n1, n3, s3) uses its control on the top floor (`enc5_1_controls1`, `_controln1`, `_controln3`, `_controls3`; route flag 256: the walker presses use until the manifold's piston, `enc5_1_pistons1` and so on, stands open at 1) and walks down to floor 2, to the spot that sees one of the manifold's two detectors along the open channel (`enc5_1_detector_s1b` and so on, about 10 units away; route flag 512 with the aim point), where the walker shoots it down to the 0.1 of its health at which `enc5_1_destroyed_poll` destroys the manifold. Each control's piston slides out of its channel along y when opened, into floor 2's walkway (a wall for the walk to the stand; where it stood is not), and opening one closes the others. Then the route goes through `enc5_2`, which opens `enc5_2_door` once all four manifolds are destroyed. Standing in `prelift_SLAYER` when `elevator_door` shuts kills the player; the route ends past it, on the car, with a deck row at the car's centre (inside `enc5_3`, clear of `prelift_SLAYER`), where the walker waits for the ride. The route is 6,531 steps since the route batch (5,107 with batch 5's top-floor throws; it was 1,614, and 5,077 before the throws); the walker and the test allow long routes more time. The fourth manifold, s3, cannot be shot from the route: from no floor the walk can reach is either of its detectors in sight through its channel. Its stands are on floor 2's south-west bay, which joins the rest of floor 2 only across the channel mouths of s2 and s3, where the closed pistons stand (s2 opens only once all four are destroyed), and neither the top floor nor the oven channels give a sight line (the n3 channel does not see s3b through the core either). Since batch 5 the route throws frags at it instead (`ThrowTarget`, route flag 2048, above). The detectors sit at the inner end of their channel, a tube at z 5.5 to 6.8 that the top floor's walkway (z 7.4) covers from y 3.5 to 6.5; every point that sees a detector's centre is inside the tube (y up to 4.6) or just above its open stretch, and the tube's floor ends at y 3.5, so a frag has to drop into its mouth past the walkway's south edge. Batch 5's search, from the top floor within 3.5 units at pitches -30 to 10, found such throws only at the walkway's south edge: from (-92.2, 3.3, 7.4), three aims, two landing in the tube's mouth with 6 of their 8 neighbours and the third with 3, each doing 102 to 106 damage against the 112.5 a detector needs (0.9 of its 125), and the thrower only about 2 units from every blast, inside the frag's own 2.5-unit outer radius. The route batch (gap 9 of batch 5's critic) widened it: spots every other cell within 3.5 units and every fourth out to 7 (every fourth throughout on walkways more than 1.5 units below the target), from 5 units below the target to 3 above, so floor 2's walkway (z 2.0) below the channel counts; pitches -45 to 45; a first look 15 degrees apart from each spot, then 5 degrees apart round each first throw that goes off within half a unit of the outer radius of a detector, then 2 degrees apart round the 16 best landings; a throw counts only if it goes off more than the frag's outer radius from the thrower's body (feet to eye); and `GrenadeArcs` now sweeps the grenade as the engine does, against polygon edges and corners too (above). The best spot is now on floor 2 at (-92.3, 7.19, 2.03): five aims at pitch 48, yaw 186 to 194 two degrees apart, lobs up through the channel's open stretch that go off by `enc5_1_detector_s3` inside the inner radius (120 damage, so one frag does it), every one of their 8 neighbours a degree away landing too, 7.4 units from the thrower (the importer's metrics record them, `throws`). Of 119,739 simulated throws from 563 spots, 7,665 went off by a detector and 7 of those too close to the thrower. The route is 6,531 steps (5,107 before; the walk down to floor 2 and back). The batch 5 top-floor aims fail the new thrower-distance rule. The search takes about two and a half minutes of the section's import. Other ways that were checked and rejected: a `LEDGE_DROPS`-style drop from the top floor into the channel's mouth does reach a stand that sees s3 (25 steps), but nothing leads out again (the tube's walls, a 3.5-unit drop to floor 0, and the piston closing back into it, which in Halo burns a player in `enc5_1_oven_s3`), so the route would end there; drops allowed for the whole section trap earlier legs in pits. The throws are simulated, not seen: the sphere sweep ignores polygon edges beyond the grenade's radius, and the landings are narrow, so a walk may find them falling short. The walker throws four frags an aim, the second and third half a degree either side, and logs where each lay (`GRENADE throw`). The section stays in `KNOWN_BLOCKED` until a walk passes (`PENDING_WALK` says what should pass). To go back to the old route, replace s3's `ThrowTarget` with `ShootTarget` in `SECTION_OBJECTIVES` and re-run `import_halo_campaign.py "$maps" --mission d40 --part 5`. The detectors are damage targets (Damage targets); before, `unit_get_health` read -1 for them (they were never placed), which destroyed every manifold the moment `enc5_1_manager` woke. The route's use rows are written for all four controls; the s3 throw rows follow the s3 one. To go back to the route before the manifolds, replace the d40 entry's `UseControl`, `ShootTarget` and `ThrowTarget` steps in `SECTION_OBJECTIVES` with the four control points and re-run `import_halo_campaign.py "$maps" --mission d40 --part 5`.
- Sections 6 to 8 (BSPs 5 to 7, the Warthog run) are driven, not walked: they are `DRIVEN_SECTIONS` in `tools/import_halo_campaign.py`, which write an `.hcol` the Warthog drives on but no `.route`, and take their spawns, markers and route from the render raster as stock sections do. The walking body does not fit them: the run starts with a 45 to 65 degree ramp up from the elevator landing (BSP 5, x -94 to -82, z 107.6 to 119.4) and goes down ramps as steep in BSP 6 (x 358 to 366) and BSP 7 (x 815 to 830), the planner and `retailmove` walk only floors with a normal of at least 0.65 (49 degrees), and the outdoor scales (1.98, 3.23 and 2.24) make the body 1.6 to 2.6 units tall, more than the corridors and the elevator landing hold. The Warthog is the retail Warthog's size at those scales. Section 6's exit volume name `bsp 5,6` is used twice (the first at x -541 is outside every BSP); the raster layout takes the second, at the far end of BSP 5. **Not driven in the game yet** (testing is on hold). To put them back on stock geometry, add `('d40', 5)`, `('d40', 6)` and `('d40', 7)` to `STOCK_SECTIONS` (it wins over `DRIVEN_SECTIONS`) and re-run `python3 tools/import_halo_campaign.py "$maps" --mission d40`; the engine then drives the Warthog on the cube grid, and `package-macos.sh` stops asking for their `.hcol`.

The launchers use `~/Library/Application Support/Halo in AssaultCube` for settings, screenshots and `clientlog.txt`. Set `HALO_AC_PROFILE` to use another directory. Pass engine options directly to `play-macos.sh`. Use `--stock` first to smoke-test the AssaultCube baseline with a separate profile. For example, this launches the stock map in a window and requests a screenshot and a clean exit after startup.

```bash
./scripts/play-macos.sh --stock -t0 -w960 -h600 --loadmap=ac_complex \
  '-esleep 1000 [screenshot] 1; sleep 1800 [quit] 1'
```

## Presentation

**Not verified in the game yet**: everything in this section was written and compiled while testing was on hold; nothing of it has been seen or heard. `tests/test_presentation.py` is written but has not been run. The code is `source/src/halo_fx.cpp` (`halo_fx.h`); apart from the melee lunge (below) and the optional effect damage (`halo_effectdamage`, off by default; dealt by `source/src/weapon.cpp`), none of it changes the game's state (no damage, no movement, no script results).

```bash
python3 tools/halo_weapons.py "$maps"                    # zoom-in, zoom-out and loop sound roles
python3 tools/halo_vehicles.py "$maps"                   # engine, crash and suspension sounds
python3 tools/halo_effects.py "$maps"                    # packages/halo/effects.txt
python3 tools/import_halo_audio.py "$maps/a30.map" .     # decodes them; tags/classes.txt
```

`import-macos-assets.sh` runs `tools/halo_effects.py` before the audio import and `package-macos.sh` requires `effects.txt`. All of it is generated and git-ignored. The new `sound` roles and lines are skipped by older games and the tables keep their formats (weapons.txt 3, vehicles.txt 2).

- **Script effects.** `tools/halo_effects.py` reads every `effe` tag a mission script names (the effect constants of `effect_new` and `effect_new_on_object_marker`, and d40's effect globals such as `explosion_large`; 20 tags) and writes, per effect, a kind (explosion, burst, smoke or glow), a size (1.6 for the tags' large particle systems, 0.6 for small ones and grenades), its first light (radius, colour, duration), the area radius of its strongest `jpt!` part and its first sound. `effect_new <effect> <flag>` draws it at the cutscene flag's place in the section, `effect_new_on_object_marker <effect> <object> <marker>` at the object's marker (batch 9: the marker of the object's model from the `.hscb`, in the model's bind pose and the object's frame, as the seats are placed, so an animation does not move it; the object's origin when the model has no such marker, and for the player and actors). Every retail call is in a cinematic (the cyborg chip insertion at chief's and cortana's hands), where an effect only plays its sound, so the markers only place effects outside scenes. AssaultCube's particles stand in for the tags' particle systems: an explosion is a fireball (three for the large ones), sparks, smoke and a flash, and knocks the camera within three times its radius; a burst is sparks and a flash; smoke is smoke; a glow a flash and a few plasma sparks. The effect's light lights stock geometry only (the retail mesh keeps its lightmaps). The tags' particle systems, decals, debris and damage are not converted: Halo's `pctl` and `part` tags carry their own shaders, bitmaps and physics, which AssaultCube's fixed particle types cannot show without a particle renderer of their own, and the damage is left out by default (d40's escape sets explosions off along the route, and with the flags projected onto AssaultCube's maps a damaging one could kill the player where Halo's would not). Since batch 5's critic's gap 11 (not verified in the game; `tests/test_presentation.py` item 8, not run) `effects.txt` carries each effect's strongest damaging `jpt!` part (`damage` and `materials` lines: the script explosions carry the medium explosion's 15 to 25 inside 1.5 world units, 5 at 2.5; the cinematic variants none), and `halo_effectdamage 1` (off by default) makes `effect_new` and `effect_new_on_object_marker` in play deal it at the effect's place (`haloeffectblast`), in the scripts' name: the player's own hurt is self-inflicted (no difficulty scale) and an ally killed is not a betrayal; `halo_scripttrace 1` logs `deals its jpt! damage` or `has no jpt! damage`. The vehicles' death explosion always deals its damage (Vehicle damage, below). In a scene, whose flags and objects are in the scene's frame, an effect only plays its sound, unpositioned. An effect the table does not have still draws (an explosion when its name says so, else a burst; logged with `halo_fxtrace 1`).
- **Camera shake.** `player_effect_set_max_translation` (world units), `player_effect_set_max_rotation` (degrees: yaw, pitch, roll), `player_effect_set_max_rumble` (the controller's motors, 0 to 1: a fine fast tremble here), `player_effect_start <intensity> <attack s>` and `player_effect_stop <decay s>` shake the view; `player_effect_impact` and `player_effect_rumble` are a half-second knock. Vehicle crashes (the player's vehicle), the explosions above and grenade, rocket and other blasts within three times their radius knock the camera too. The shake moves only the rendered view (`transplayer` and the sky), never the player's yaw, pitch or position, and is smooth noise of the time (it does not draw on the game's random numbers); the player's shots still go where the shaken view's centre (the reticle) points, since the aim point is read from the drawn frame. It is capped at 8 degrees; `halo_shake 0` turns it off. A checkpoint revert stops the shake and clears the cinematic screen effects and the damage arcs (the reverted script starts its own again); the sound class gains are kept.
- **GL resources (batch 9).** The blur's copy of the frame and the flashlight's spot texture are deleted when the GL context is rebuilt (`resetgl`, which a display or resolution change in the options runs: `cleanupgl` calls `halofxcleanupgl`) and made again on their next use; a texture name that stops being one some other way is caught by `glIsTexture` before each use. The copy is reallocated whenever the viewport changes size (a window resize without a rebuild). Without non-power-of-two textures (OpenGL below 2.0 and no `GL_ARB_texture_non_power_of_two`) the copy is the next powers of two and only its lower left is drawn; a viewport larger than the driver's texture limit gets no blur. `qa_fx_state` prints the copy's size, the texture's, `npot`, whether both textures are live and how often they were remade.
- **Cinematic screen effects.** `cinematic_screen_effect_start` turns them on and `_stop` clears them. `_set_convolution <iterations> <mode> <from> <to> <s>` blurs the view from one radius to the other (retail radius in 480-line pixels): the frame is copied once and laid back over itself in up to three rings of eight offset copies. `_set_filter <desaturation from> <to> <tint from> <to> <additive> <s>` with `_set_filter_desaturation_tint <r> <g> <b>` takes the colour out of the view towards the tint, approximated without shaders (a multiply towards the tint and a grey wash); `_set_video <mode> <intensity>` (c10) draws scan lines and a cold cast. They draw over the 3D view and under the HUD and the scenes' letterbox and fades; `halo_screeneffects 0` turns them off.
- **Flashlight.** **X** (`halo_flashlight`) turns it on and off. Its light is the cyborg's `characters\cyborg\flashlight_cyborg`: 6 world units of radius, full inside 20 degrees and none past 45. On the retail mesh it is a pass after the solid surfaces that adds each surface's own texture lit by a spot projected from the eye, fading to nothing at the radius; on stock geometry it is an AssaultCube dynamic light a little ahead of the eye. Actors and models are not lit by it. It drains over 60 seconds on and recovers over 30 off (an engine choice: the tags hold no flashlight energy), and the retail HUD's flashlight meter shows its charge while it is on. It goes off at a section start and when the player dies.
- **Damage-direction indicator.** A hit on the player from an actor, the shield's included (a hit the shield takes whole used to show nothing), draws a red arc round the reticle towards where the attacker was, in the world (it follows as the player turns), fading over a second; hits from about the same way renew one arc, up to eight. It replaces AssaultCube's own indicator in the campaign; `halo_damageindicator 0` turns it off.
- **Sound classes.** `sound_class_set_gain <class> <gain> <ticks>` fades every retail sound class whose name starts with the given one (`vehicle` is `vehicle_collision` and `vehicle_engine`, `ambient` the three ambient classes) to the gain. It reaches the scenario's sounds (music, ambience, dialogue: `tools/halo_effects.py` writes each cache's `snd!` classes, and the channels already playing follow the fade), the decoded tag samples the engine plays (weapon, grenade, equipment, vehicle and effect sounds: `tags/classes.txt` from the audio import) and the engine loops. AssaultCube's own samples (footsteps, the fallback weapon sounds) have no class and are not faded. The gains go back to 1 at each section start and on Continue (they are not in the progress file: a class the script had faded before the save plays at full gain until the script sets it again).
- **Vehicle sounds.** `tools/halo_vehicles.py` adds the tag's sounds to `vehicles.txt`: the engine loop (the first looping sound attached to the vehicle: `warthog_7_engine`, `ghost_lp`, `banshee_engine`, the Scorpion's `warthog_engine`, `wraith_eng1`, the turret's `c_turret2`), the crash (`jeep_hit_solid`) and the suspension (`warthog_suspension`, the Scorpion's own). The engine loops from whoever drives (a turret's while its gunner turns it faster than 15 degrees a second, held for a quarter second), its pitch rising with the speed, and stops when nobody does; the crash plays when a vehicle loses more than a third of its top speed in one step against a wall, a body or another vehicle, the suspension on a landing faster than 14 units per second down, at most one of them every 0.4 s.
- **Weapons.** Zoom steps play the weapon tag's zoom-in and zoom-out sounds (`weap` +408 and +424 after the item section: the pistol's, sniper rifle's and rocket launcher's `sniper_2x_zoom` and `sniper_10x_zoom`). A looping sound attached at a trigger marker is the weapon's `sound loop`: the flamethrower's `fire_ft` (its only firing sound) plays while the trigger is held and it can fire, and the plasma pistol's `charge` hum once a charge has built for 0.15 s.
- **Melee lunge.** A hostile actor in sight within 1.8 times the blow's reach (7.2 units) and 25 degrees of the aim takes the blow, and the player is pushed towards it by a velocity impulse that the floor's friction takes out (about 1.9 units travelled per unit of impulse, closing the gap to about 3 units), as an explosion's knockback is applied; the movement constants are untouched. `halo_meleelunge 0` turns it off; `halo_meleetrace 1` logs it.
- **Rocket launcher.** Its tube rides the right shoulder with its top above eye height; in AssaultCube's wider view (75 degrees vertical for the weapon, where Halo's view is 70 degrees horizontal) it filled the right of the screen. It is now drawn 0.5 units further right, 1 forward and 1.5 lower than its retail animation holds it. This is a judgement call made without seeing it: `halo_viewmodeloffset <right> <forward> <up>` changes the current weapon's placement in the game (no arguments prints it), for tuning, and the values belong in the table in `source/src/halo.cpp`.
- **Reticles.** Vehicle guns use their own retail reticles where the HUD tags carry them (the turrets, Warthog, Ghost, Scorpion, Banshee and Wraith guns all do). A weapon whose HUD tags have no reticle (the flamethrower, whose weapon HUD is empty) gets a plain one, a circle and four ticks in the retail blue (red on an aim-assist target), and shows its rounds or battery as text; without `hud.txt` a seat's gun gets the plain reticle too.

## Vehicles

**Not verified in the game yet**: everything in this section was written and compiled while testing was on hold, the vehicle fixes (bodies inside vehicles, shoving, stuck recovery, the escape taking over a scenario Warthog, lift decks, routes round parked vehicles) included. `tests/test_vehicles.py` (items 7 to 9 for the fixes), `tests/test_maw_drive.py` (item 6) and the parked-vehicle check in `tests/test_traversal_routes.py` are written but have not been run.

`tools/halo_vehicles.py "$maps"` (run by `import-macos-assets.sh` before the campaign import) writes `packages/halo/vehicles.txt` from the retail `vehi` tags and exports their models to `packages/models/halo/vehicle/<name>` (git-ignored, like everything under `packages/`). It lists six vehicles in a fixed order: the Warthog (a car), the Covenant gun turret (a fixed gun with a gunner seat), the Ghost (a hover craft), the Scorpion (a tank), the Banshee (a flyer) and the Wraith (a hover craft the player may not drive); see Enemy and flying vehicles. Each record carries the class, whether the player may drive it (format 2; the game still reads format 1 tables, giving their props a class from the retail motion kind), the drive parameters, the model's bounds, the seats with their markers and the vehicle weapon from `weapons.txt`. `package-macos.sh` requires the file.
