Without it the engine falls back to a built-in Warthog with the model the actor importer writes to `packages/models/halo/warthog`; a vehicle with no model is drawn as a wire box.

`tools/import_halo_campaign.py` writes each mission's scenario vehicle placements (the `scnr` vehicle block) of those six types into the sections whose BSP holds them, as `PLAYERSTART` entities with team 102: the type, the placement's not-placed flags, the scenario object index and the section scale. On retail collision they stand at their retail point and settle onto the floor; on stock geometry they go on the nearest raster floor. Placements the scripts create (`object_create`, not-placed flag 1) appear when the script creates them; the others appear at the start, except on the difficulties their flags leave them out of. Re-run the campaign import (all missions) to place them. Imported: d40's six parked escape Warthogs in section 6, a Warthog and a turret in section 7 and the two enc7_7 turrets in section 8; a50's 14 turrets; the Warthogs, Ghosts and Banshees of a30, b30 and c40; b40's turrets, Ghosts, Wraiths and Banshees (its Scorpion stands outside every campaign section); the turrets of c10 and d20. Pelicans, dropships, lifepods and cinematic props are left out.

`source/src/halo_vehicle.cpp` runs them:
- **E** by a seat's door gets in (the driver's door of a Warthog, its rear for the gunner's seat, a turret's gun); in a seat **E** gets out beside the door, or on the other side, behind, ahead or on top, wherever the body fits, and stays in when none does. A seat nearer than a weapon on the ground wins **E**. The HUD prompts `E  Drive the Warthog`, `E  Warthog gunner`, `E  Man the Covenant Turret` or `E  Flip the Warthog`.
- The Warthog drives with **W**/**S** (throttle, brake, reverse) and steers towards where the camera looks, as the retail Warthog does; **A**/**D** steer directly. `halo_vehiclesteer 1` steers with the keys only. The camera orbits behind with the mouse. Top speed is the retail Warthog's at the section's scale, and never below 36 units/s (the old escape pace, which the 3:00 timer was set for); grip is strong with some slide, it climbs and jumps off crests, pitches and rolls with the ground, and flips when it lands on its side or back or stands over more than 58 degrees. Upside down for 0.7 s it throws the driver out; **E** beside a flipped vehicle puts it back on its wheels. Running into actors at speed kills them. It is not the retail physics, but its jumps follow retail arcs (batch 10, unverified in the game): leaving the ground (the floor ending under all four wheels, or a crest at speed) it keeps its velocity along the surface it was on (`tangent`: the floor's vertical rate at its velocity, from the floor's normal under the trailing wheels, which leave last, so the take-off has the angle of a ramp's lip; on the cube grid from the slope between the axles with all four wheels down; at most 0.8 of the speed upwards; a downhill edge keeps its descent, where it used to leave level and leave a ramp at its eased climbing rate), rising it stops at a ceiling (the chassis swept upwards), and in the air it falls at the retail gravity (the globals' falling damage block: 0.003565 Halo units a tick squared, times the phys tag's gravity scale, 1 for every vehicle) at the section's scale, times the square of the ratio of its top speed to the retail one (`vehicleGravity`): the engine runs the Warthog at 36 units a second or more, 2.1 times the retail speed at d40 section 8's scale, and retail gravity alone would carry it 4.4 times as far off a ramp as Halo's goes; with the square of that ratio it flies the retail arc over the same ground, that much faster (31.8 units a second squared in d40 section 8, 35.8 in section 6, 22.0 in section 7; 40 before, and still 40 with a `vehicles.txt` that has no `physics` line). The same gravity pulls it along slopes (halved) and drops the other ground vehicles and an empty Banshee.
- The bodies are the retail vehicles' size at the section's scale. On retail collision the Warthog drives on four wheel traces and two chassis capsules against the structure and the machines (doors); on stock geometry on the cube grid (it steps up whole cubes as the walking body does). Bodies collide with vehicles (`halo_vehiclecollide 0` turns that off, for checking a route walk a placed vehicle blocks); the walking routes go round the parked ones (above).
- Bodies inside vehicles. A body (the player on foot or an actor not in a seat) left part way inside a vehicle's box, by a vehicle the script creates, spawns, teleports or a revert puts back over it, an exit spot or a lift, is pushed out within a frame by the shortest way that has room, checked against the retail collision (or the cube grid), the devices, the other bodies and the vehicles: out of each face at the box's edge, its corners, each face a body's width further out, nearest first, or onto the roof when the feet are within a step of it; first onto floor, then over a ledge, never through a wall or a closed door between the body and the spot (a line of sight at the body's middle) (`Halo vehicle: player pushed out of the Warthog (N units)`, actors with `halo_vehicletrace 1`). A body with no room anywhere may walk out of that vehicle and is tried again every quarter second (`... has no room to get out; it may walk out`). The old rule, by which only a body deep inside a box could walk out and one part way in could not move, is gone. Ordinary movement is unchanged: walking never enters a vehicle's box.
- A moving vehicle (the player's Warthog, or an empty one rolling) shoves each body it runs into out ahead of it or aside, a few hundredths of a unit clear, every 8 ms step; a body pinned against a wall (or another body or vehicle) stops it instead. The player's Warthog at more than 8 units/s still runs actors down as before, once a frame each (killed outright above 20).
- The Warthog's gunner seat and the turrets fire their retail gun (warthog gun, turret gun) at the crosshair with its rate and heat; the driver cannot fire. The HUD shows the vehicle, the seat, the speed or the gun's heat, and the shield, health and motion sensor. Fire on a player at the wheel of a Warthog is cut by a fifth; the Warthog, the Scorpion and the turrets cannot be destroyed (their collision models have no body vitality, as in Halo CE), the Wraith, and the Ghost and Banshee while ridden, can (Vehicle damage, batch 7).
- Stuck recovery (`updateStuck`), as for the player on foot. Each Warthog keeps its last four safe spots, sampled every quarter second: grounded, upright within 30 degrees, clear of the structure, the machines and the other vehicles, with all four wheels on structure floor (not a lift deck or a light bridge), at least half a length apart. One that falls out of the world (8 units below the section's mesh, below -130 on stock geometry, or two seconds in the air with neither structure nor device below), stays wedged (the chassis more than 0.1 units into the structure while slower than 2 units/s, for 0.75 s; the capsules' bottoms ride up with its pitch and roll, so a steep ramp is not a wedge) or hangs in mid-air without moving for 1.5 s goes to the nearest spot within two widths where it fits upright with floor under its wheels (not after a fall), else to its newest safe spot that is still clear, with whoever is in it (`Halo vehicle recovery: <why>, the Warthog put back at x,y,z`); a second recovery within five seconds goes one spot further back. With nowhere to put it, the driver is put out (`no safe spot for the Warthog, driver put out`): beside it after a wedge, and after a fall onto the retail floor the player got in from (`halorecoverplayer`; getting into a seat drops the other safe floors), else killed and reverted as before; an empty one that fell goes back to its placement (or is removed). Turrets never move by themselves and get only the fall check, so they stay where the scenario put them; the Ghost, Scorpion, Wraith and Banshee keep safe spots and get the wedge and stall checks like the Warthog (a flying Banshee neither stalls nor falls for want of floor below). Upside down beyond the flip helper: E on an overturned vehicle that would not fit upright where it lies moves it to the nearest spot where it does or to its last safe spot; a driver who has no room to get out of an overturned Warthog (0.7 s on its back) stays at the wheel and the Warthog is put back on its wheels, and only when that fails is the driver thrown onto it (and later put back in by the recovery, see Falls, wedges, lifts and light bridges).
- Lift decks: a vehicle standing on a device (not all four wheels on structure floor) never goes to sleep, so it keeps following its floor, and lifts carry vehicles up and down as they carry bodies. Only on retail collision: on stock geometry a vehicle's floor is the cube grid.
- The mission scripts' seat commands act on these vehicles: `vehicle_test_seat_list`, `vehicle_test_seat`, `unit_enter_vehicle`, `vehicle_load_magic`, `unit_exit_vehicle`, `vehicle_unload`, `vehicle_riders` (the player only), `vehicle_driver`, `vehicle_gunner`, `unit_in_vehicle`, `unit_set_enterable_by_player`, `object_create`/`_destroy` (a bulk destroy leaves the player's own vehicle alone), `object_teleport`, and `ai_vehicle_enterable_distance`, `_actor_type` and `_disable`, `ai_go_to_vehicle` and `ai_exit_vehicle` for turret gunners. A vehicle carried by a scripted object (b30's jeep under its Pelican) follows it until the script lets it go. A Warthog or turret that is not in the current section reports nobody in its seats; dropships and Pelicans carry actors and, once a script makes one enterable, the player (Enemy and flying vehicles); other vehicles keep the stand-ins (seat tests true). The Maw's escape seats the player in a scenario Warthog where one is parked (below); in the seat tests (`halovehicleescapeseat`) that Warthog is itself and stands in for the scenario Warthogs not standing in the section (in sections 7 and 8, `trench_jeep1` to `3` and `asspain_1` to `3`), and a Warthog the engine spawned (bound to no scenario object) stands in for every scenario Warthog, as before, so `trench_jeep_test` (which asks for `trench_jeep1` to `4` and `asspain_1` to `3`) sees the player at the wheel and Cortana does not nag. Actors drive and fly the vehicles a script seats them at the controls of (Enemy and flying vehicles).
- Covenant actors man turrets: `vehicle_load_magic` and `ai_go_to_vehicle` seat them (one further away than the enterable distance walks to the turret first), and a free turret the script made enterable takes the nearest Covenant actor of the right kind within that distance. A gunner stays on the seat and fires the turret's gun at its brain's target (it used to fire its own weapon; Enemy and flying vehicles).
- A checkpoint saves the vehicles; a revert restores them, and a revert before any checkpoint puts the placements back (and clears what the last attempt's script set on them: enterable by the player, the AI boarding distance and actor type). The progress file (version 3) saves the checkpoint's vehicles too, and Continue restores them, the player in their seat (see Title menu and saved games); unverified in the game.
- Not handled: a vehicle does not follow the player into the next section, except that the Maw's run starts each driven section at the wheel; a shoved body is moved, not given the vehicle's speed, and a body standing on a vehicle's top is not carried when it drives off; vehicles ride lifts only on retail collision. (The chase camera's wall test, `intersectgeometry`, traces the retail collision and its scenery on retail collision and the cube raster only on stock geometry; a destroyed vehicle's explosion deals its `jpt!` damage and the wreck is optional, `halo_vehiclewreck`, off by default: Vehicle damage. Both corrected in the final integration review of the d40 section 3 batch, the cube-raster stamp and the ownerless-kill batch; an earlier text said the camera used the cube raster and the explosion dealt no damage.)

The Maw's escape (sections 6 to 8) is driven. The countdown, the route markers, the finale and `game_won` are as before; `halodriving()` now means the player is in a seat. Sections 7 and 8, and section 6 on retail collision, start at the wheel of a Warthog: an unused one within three lengths of the start, else the scenario's own nearest Warthog within 40 units (or ten lengths), created where it was placed if the script has not created it yet, else a new one at the start. Section 6 takes one of the six parked at the elevator landing (`trench_jeep1` to `3`, `asspain_1` to `3`, 10 to 28 units from the start), so the mission script's `object_create_containing trench_jeep` creates the others round it and leaves the player's alone (it used to spawn a Warthog of its own first, which the script's could then stand on). Sections 7 and 8 have no scenario Warthog near the start (`trench_jeep4` is parked at the far end of section 7) and get a new one, as before. Section 6 on stock geometry starts on foot by the parked Warthogs; when none stands within 80 units after 2.5 s (the script has not created them yet), the nearest parked one appears where it was placed, else a new one beside the player. A player killed at the wheel reverts at the section start in a Warthog. QA hooks: `qa_vehicle_state` (now with `inside` and `trapped` for the player, and each vehicle's `placed`, `safe` spot count and `length`), `qa_vehicle_approach`, `qa_vehicle_use`, `qa_vehicle_enter`, `qa_vehicle_input`, `qa_vehicle_flip`, `qa_vehicle_spawn`, `qa_vehicle_escape` and `qa_vehicle_autopilot`, and for the fixes `qa_vehicle_place_player`, `qa_vehicle_place_actor`, `qa_vehicle_inside`, `qa_vehicle_push`, `qa_vehicle_watch`, `qa_vehicle_stuck` and `qa_vehicle_throw`, and for the jumps (batch 10) `qa_vehicle_jump X Y Z YAW SPEED MS` (the player's vehicle put down there at that speed, full throttle straight ahead, its motion, gravity and launch rate logged every 50 ms) and `qa_vehicle_cruise MAP SPEED` (QA throttle input stops at that speed in that section) (`tests/native/vehicle_qa.inc`); `halo_vehicletrace 1` logs spawns, seats, flips and actors pushed out.

## Enemy and flying vehicles

**Not verified in the game yet**: everything in this section was written and compiled while testing was on hold. `tests/test_enemy_vehicles.py` checks it and has not been run; `tests/test_vehicles.py` and `tests/test_maw_drive.py` cover the Warthog and turrets as before.

Re-run the vehicle table, then c40's maps and scripts (all git-ignored under `packages/`):

```bash
python3 tools/halo_vehicles.py "$maps"                       # vehicles.txt format 2
python3 tools/import_halo_campaign.py "$maps" --mission c40   # sections 1 and 4 flown (an .hcol and a planned vehicle route)
python3 tools/import_halo_scripts.py "$maps" --mission c40    # section 1's projection changed
```

**Classes.** `source/src/halo_vehicle.cpp` drives four classes. The Warthog is the car described under Vehicles. The Ghost and the Wraith are hover craft: they float a little (0.12 world units) over the ground the wheel traces find (the model is drawn that far up, bobbing; the body's box covers it), turn on the spot towards the camera at their own rate (the tags' turn rate is 0, so the Ghost turns 150 degrees a second and the Wraith about 60, slower with their lower retail speed), strafe with A and D and slide, and never flip. The Scorpion is a tank: A and D turn it on its tracks, W with no key held steers towards the camera, and it barely slides. The Banshee is a flyer: piloted, it has no gravity, its heading and nose follow the camera (100 and 70 degrees a second, the nose within 55 degrees), it banks into turns, W pushes along the nose (S slows and backs it), A and D strafe, jump climbs and crouch descends (`halovehiclejump`, `halovehiclecrouch` from `physics.cpp`), and with no input it hovers in place; it slides along the structure, machines and vehicles it meets and may rest on the floor. Empty (the pilot out or dead) it falls and lands. Top speeds are the retail speed at the section's scale, never below the Warthog's 36 units a second scaled by the vehicle's retail speed against the Warthog's (the Banshee gets a third more), so each still outruns the player on foot. On stock geometry the same models run on the cube grid. Running actors down at speed applies to every class that drives.

**The player.** E by the driver's door gets in where Halo CE lets the player: the Ghost (`E  Drive the Ghost`; its second driver's seat is the Elite's and is not offered), the Scorpion and the Banshee (`E  Fly the Banshee`), never the Wraith, and never a vehicle a hostile drives (Halo CE has no boarding); with a friendly actor at the wheel (a marine driving a Warthog) the free gunner seat is still offered. In the air E does nothing (`Halo vehicle: the Banshee is in the air, land to get out`): a Banshee's pilot gets out only with the floor within 1.5 world units below, so land first. The driver works the gun of a Ghost, Scorpion or Banshee (the Warthog's driver still cannot fire): the trigger fires the gun's first trigger at the crosshair (the Ghost's and Banshee's bolts within 25 degrees of the nose, the Scorpion's cannon anywhere), and the zoom key (Mouse 2 or Z, `halo_zoom`) is the secondary trigger in a seat: the Banshee's fuel rod and the Scorpion's machine gun. A trigger with no rate of fire in the tags (the cannon, the fuel rod) fires at most every 1.5 s (0.4 s without a blast). Shots play the tags' fire sound (the second trigger its charged sound). The HUD shows the speed for every driver and, for a Banshee's pilot, the height over the floor below (`ALT`). Fire on the player is cut by a fifth in the Warthog and the Banshee and by half in the Scorpion; a Ghost's rider and a turret's gunner sit in the open.

**Actors at the controls.** An actor a script seats at the controls (`vehicle_load_magic`, `unit_enter_vehicle`, `ai_go_to_vehicle`) drives or flies the vehicle; `halo_vehicleai 0` makes them sit still. The brain thinks as before; the vehicle reads where it wants to go and whom it fights (`haloactorintent` in `halo_ai.cpp`: the current command list point, its combat or search position, the unit it follows) and steers there. Without a target it drives to that goal (a command list point counts as reached within 4 world units: a vehicle cannot stop on a point as a body does, so `runCommands` allows that much for an actor in a seat) and holds still without one. Against a target, a Warthog runs it down; a Ghost closes to 6 to 18 world units, faces it (its guns are fixed) and strafes round it, changing direction every 3 to 7 s; the Scorpion and the Wraith keep 12 to 28 units and circle. Ground vehicles steer round what blocks the way ahead (or a drop), and back off for a second when stuck. A Banshee pilot with a target makes strafing runs: out to 30 units and 12 up, a dive with the nose on the target (7 s at most), a pull out over and 35 units past it, and round again; without one it flies to its goal (a command list point exactly, a position on foot 6 units above it) and hovers. It keeps 3 units off the floor and climbs over what lies ahead. The actor in an armed seat (a Ghost's, Wraith's, Scorpion's or Banshee's driver, a Warthog's or turret's gunner) fires the vehicle's gun at its brain's target instead of its own weapon (`halovehiclearmedseat`): within the gun's range, within the nose's cone for a fixed gun, in bursts of 0.9 to 1.8 s for an automatic gun, leading the target by most of the shot's flight, with the variant's aiming error; the Wraith lobs its mortar at the angle that lands it at the target's distance (the engine's projectiles keep their speed and bend under gravity, so the angle is the distance times gravity over twice the speed squared; at 10 world units a second the mortar reaches about 85 units), and a Banshee pilot adds a fuel rod every 5 to 9 s inside 35 units. Actors in seats throw no grenades. `ai_vehicle_encounter` puts the vehicle's driver in the named squad, now or when the script loads one (c40's Wraiths). `ai_go_to_vehicle` beyond the enterable distance sends the nearest actor of the squad walking to the seat's entry point (`haloactorgoto`; it fights on the way) and seats it on arrival, or after 12 s within twice the reach; it gives up after 30 s; `ai_going_to_vehicle` counts the walkers.

**Recorded flights.** A vehicle on a recording or carried by another object follows it, as before, and now takes the recording's pitch when it is a Banshee; when the recording ends the vehicle carries on from there (an actor at the controls takes over, an empty Banshee falls). `object_teleport` of a Banshee keeps the flag's height (a30's `lz_banshee` flags are in the air) instead of settling it on the floor. No campaign section places an engine Banshee that a script flies on a recording (d40's trench Banshees and d20's are not among the six types), so this path is exercised only by flybys of the types above.

**Dropships and Pelicans.** The scenario's vehicles the engine does not drive move on their recordings and parents in the mission script, as in cutscenes, and are now drawn in play while a recording or a parent moves them, after one has (hovering where it set them down), or while someone rides in them (`Scenario::render`); before, only b30's landing Pelican was. Actors a script loads into one (`vehicle_load_magic dropship passenger (ai_actors squad)`, `unit_enter_vehicle`) ride at its seat markers, held there every frame (`updateCargo` in `halo_script_ai.inc`; a seat name matches by part, as Halo's labels do: `passenger` loads every `CD-passenger` seat, more riders than seats share the first), throw no grenades and fire nothing, until `vehicle_unload` with a seat name (or none) sets them down a step outside their seat, where they drop to the floor (`Halo cargo: <actor> set down by <dropship>` with `halo_scripttrace 1`); `unit_exit_vehicle` and `ai_exit_vehicle` let them out too, a destroyed dropship sets its riders down where they are, and a revert clears the cargo (the actors respawn at their squad starts). `ai_go_to_vehicle` on a Pelican sends one actor of the squad per free seat of that name walking to it (a30's marines to their extraction Pelican); within 4 world units of the seat it rides. This covers a30's three Covenant dropship waves (`rubble_rock_cship`, `pipe_cship`, `fort_cship` and the others), a50's `c_dropship_a`, b40's `c_dropship_b` (and the Ghosts it carries, `unit_enter_vehicle ext_b_a_ghost_a c_dropship_b cargo_ghost01`, which follow it as carried vehicles) and d40's `enc6_5_dropship`. b30's extraction Pelican keeps its own approach, which the engine starts (now a table, `EngineApproach`, with b30's one entry) and its own E (finishing the section). Dropships do not collide: bodies pass through them, as through the cutscene objects.

**Boarding.** A Pelican (any vehicle the engine does not drive) that a script makes enterable (`unit_set_enterable_by_player <pelican> true`) can be boarded on foot with E within 4.5 world units of a rider seat once it has stopped on its recording (`E  Board the Pelican`; the front riders, Halo's player seats `P-riderLF` and `P-riderRF`, come first). Aboard, the player rides at the seat (no walking, firing or pickups: `halodriving()` is true), E gets out where they got in, and the scripts' seat tests for the player on that Pelican (`vehicle_test_seat_list foehammer_cliff p-riderlf (players)`) answer whether they boarded a seat of that name; for a Pelican no script made enterable they still answer true, as before. `game_won` while the player is aboard ends the section (`Halo: game_won aboard the extraction`): a30's three extraction scripts (`mission_extraction_cliff`, `_rubble`, `_river`) land `foehammer_*`, make it enterable, wait for the player in a rider seat, then fade out and call `game_won`, which was ignored before (a30 ended at its last route marker). A Pelican that leaves or is destroyed with the player aboard holds them where its seat last was. The enterable flag is kept by a checkpoint, not by the progress file: after Continue the seat test falls back to the stand-in. The other missions the task names were checked against their scripts: a50's `insertion_pelican` and b40's `insertion_pelican` seat the player only inside their cutscenes (`unit_enter_vehicle (player0) insertion_pelican p-riderlf` in b40's insertion), b40 ends in the control room and c10's extraction is its last cutscene, so a30 is the only mission whose play waits for the player to board.

**Flown sections.** c40 sections 1 and 4 play on retail collision (Notes on c40, above). On them (`flownSection` in `halo.cpp`: mission 8, sections 1 and 4, on retail collision) the engine keeps a Banshee the player may take: 2.5 s in, and every five seconds on foot after that, if no free Banshee the player may enter stands in the section, one appears beside the player (`Halo vehicle: a Banshee for the flight at ...`, `halovehicleensuretype`). Section 1 has `fly_away_1`; section 4 gets one. Placements the script has not created yet are left to the script (c40 gives some to its own pilots).

**Judgement calls.** Vehicles with a body vitality take damage and blow up (Vehicle damage, below); the Wraith's driver sits inside its hull (an invisible seat) and is no longer shot through it. The Ghost's second driver's seat and the Scorpion's four rider seats are for actors (marines ride the Scorpion in Halo; no script seats them there). Pilots and riders are drawn at their seat marker in their standing pose (a Wraith's driver shows through its hull). An actor at a vehicle's gun aims and hits as the vehicle's actor variant (Vehicle damage, below), not as its own infantry variant. The Banshee's flight model, rates and speeds, the hover gap and turn rates, the strafing run's distances and the AI's ranges are the engine's, not the tags'.

QA hooks (`tests/native/vehicle_qa.inc`): `qa_vehicle_flight THROTTLE STRAFE CLIMB MS`, `qa_vehicle_look YAW PITCH`, `qa_vehicle_secondary 0|1`, `qa_vehicle_pilot VEHICLE KIND TEAM SEAT` (a new actor, or KIND -1 the nearest, at the controls, with magic sight), `qa_vehicle_flown`, `qa_cargo_state LABEL` (`CARGO_QA` rows per rider and per visible script vehicle: recording, flown, boardable, seats, model), `qa_cargo_approach OBJECT` and `qa_cargo_board`; `qa_vehicle_state` rows add `class` (0 car, 1 turret, 3 hover, 4 tank, 5 flyer), `pilot`, `phase` (the Banshee pilot's run: 0 out, 1 dive, 2 pull out), `target`, `carried` and `shots` (the gun has fired).

### Vehicle damage and the vehicle actor variants (batch 7)

**Not verified in the game yet**: written and compiled while testing was on hold; `tests/test_vehicle_damage.py` checks it and has not been run. Re-run `python3 tools/halo_vehicles.py "$maps"` and `python3 tools/halo_effects.py "$maps"` (both git-ignored outputs); the death explosion's sound (the frag grenade's) is already decoded by `tools/import_halo_audio.py`.

- **The table.** Each `vehicles.txt` record gains `vitality <body> <coll flags> <material type> <body multiplier>` from the vehicle's collision model (`coll`: maximum body vitality, its flags, the material type and body multiplier of its indirect damage material), `destroyed <effe>` (the body depleted effect), `variant <actv>` (the actor variant named after the vehicle, `<vehicle tag> pilot` or `gunner`: the retail vehicle variants reference no unit, so the match is by name) and each seat's unit seat flags as a ninth column. Older engines skip the new lines and column; the format stays 2. The retail values: the Wraith 450 with no flags; the Ghost 160 and the Banshee 100, both "only damaged while occupied" (flag 64), the Banshee also "takes body damage for its children" (2); the Warthog, the Scorpion and the turret 0 (indestructible, "only damaged by explosives"). All three destructible ones use the Wraith's death explosion; their material is metal_thick (7). The Wraith's driver seat is invisible (seat flag 1). `effects.txt` (`tools/halo_effects.py`) now also holds the vehicles' depleted effects, so `halofxeffect` draws and sounds the death explosion.
- **What hits a vehicle.** A vehicle with a body vitality stops shots: an instant shot (`halofiretrigger`) and a bolt in flight (`haloupdatebolts`) end on the first such vehicle's body box they pass through (`halovehicletrace`; since batch 5's critic's gap 11 the box is tilted by the vehicle's pitch and roll as its model is drawn, about its floor point, a flipped vehicle keeping the level box of 0.6 of its height; bodies still collide with the level box), unless the body they met is a rider of that vehicle in an open seat (the Ghost's pilot is still shot off it); a rider in an invisible seat (the Wraith's driver) or of a vehicle that takes its children's damage (the Banshee's pilot, the player included) is covered, and the vehicle takes the shot. A blast (rockets, fuel rods, mortars, the Scorpion's shell: `areadamage`; frag and plasma grenades) damages every such vehicle whose box is within its outer radius, at the falloff to the box's nearest point (a grenade also needs a clear line to the box's middle; the one a plasma grenade is stuck to takes full strength plus the attached damage). A shot never stops on the shooter's own vehicle (a blast does reach it, as it reaches the shooter's body: a fuel rod fired point-blank hurts the Banshee that fired it). An actor's shots stop only on a vehicle someone hostile to it rides, as they pass through friendly bodies (`haloactorhit`): a Covenant actor's fire passes through its own side's Wraith, or an empty one, as before (review judgement call: otherwise b40's Grunts firing at the player past their Wraith would wreck it); the player's shots stop on every such vehicle. The Warthog, the Scorpion and the turrets have no vitality: shots pass through them as before (a judgement call: no other behaviour changes for them). An empty Ghost or Banshee stops shots but takes no damage.
- **How much.** `halovehiclehit`: the hit's damage (range or blast falloff applied, the shooter's modifier included) times the jpt! modifier for the vehicle's material (`weapons.txt` and `equipment.txt` `materials` lines: small arms 0.25 on metal_thick, explosives 1) and the material's body multiplier, over the body vitality. Difficulty follows Halo's team rules by the vehicle's owner team (the driver's, else the gunner's, kept after they get out; Difficulty): damage owned by a team hostile to the player times enemy_damage, divided back out on the owner's own side, over the maximum vitality times enemy_vitality (a hostile team's vehicle, or one nobody has sat in) or friend_vitality (the player's or a marine's); until the difficulty-table work the player's vehicle took the engine's damage multiplier from anything but the player and every other vehicle was divided by its enemy health multiplier. A vehicle "only damaged by explosives" takes only blasts; one "only damaged while occupied" only while someone alive rides it. Scripted cinematics hurt nothing. `halo_vehicletrace 1` logs each hit (`Halo vehicle N (Wraith) takes X from the player: health H (team T)`; the team since the difficulty-table work).
- **Destroyed.** At no health the vehicle explodes (its depleted effect at the box's middle, `Halo vehicle: the Wraith is destroyed`), its riders are put out and killed (a large finite hit, so `object_cannot_take_damage` and the QA walker's protection still hold, credited to whoever destroyed it), it no longer exists, and the scripts' object is destroyed as `object_destroy` does (`haloscenarioobjectdestroyed`: cargo set down, volume tests and object lists without it). `unit_get_health` of a vehicle's object is now its body health (1 for one with no vitality) while it exists and -1 once it is gone (b40's `dialog_ext_a_c` waits for `crev_ent_wraith_a` and `_b` to reach 0 or less; it used to wait for ever). A script's `object_create` brings it back whole; until then `vehicle_load_magic` and `unit_enter_vehicle` into it fail (one merely not created yet is still created by them, as before). Since batch 5's critic's gap 11 (not verified in the game; `tests/test_vehicle_damage.py` items 6 and 8) the explosion deals its own damage and the wreck may be left; see Covered riders, death explosions and wrecks below.
- **Saved.** A checkpoint keeps each vehicle's health and whether it was destroyed; the progress file's vehicle records (version 3) carry them after the safe spots (a record without them, from before, still loads whole), and a destroyed vehicle restores as gone.
- **The vehicle actor variants (gap 13).** An actor at a vehicle's gun (a Ghost's, Wraith's, Scorpion's or Banshee's driver, a turret's or Warthog's gunner) now fires as the vehicle's actor variant (`variantOf`: the `variant` line, else the variant named `<vehicle tag> pilot` or `gunner` in actors.txt): its shots carry that variant's damage modifier (`halofiretrigger`'s `modifier`, which `rollshot` takes instead of the seated actor's own; the turret gunner's 0.333, the Ghost's, Banshee's, Scorpion's and Wraith's 0.5) and miss by its firing error (`aiFire`). A variant with no modifier (the Warthog gunner's, whose fire block is all zeros) is not used at all (`fightingVariant`): the seated actor's own modifier and error apply, so a marine at the Warthog's gun still misses by its own error rather than never missing. Before, an Elite's or Grunt's infantry modifier and error applied.
- QA hooks (`tests/native/vehicle_qa.inc`): `qa_vehicle_damage VEHICLE AMOUNT EXPLOSIVE` (as the player, no material modifier), `qa_vehicle_aim VEHICLE` (face its body box) and `qa_vehicle_protect 0|1` (`halo_vehicledamage`); `qa_vehicle_state` adds a `VEHICLE_HEALTH` line per vehicle (`name`, `health`, `destroyed`, `vitality`, `flags`, `material`, `occupied`, `variant` and its `modifier` and `error`, and since gap 11 `wreck`).

**Covered riders, death explosions and wrecks (batch 5's critic, gaps 8 and 11; not verified in the game, `tests/test_vehicle_damage.py` items 5 to 8, not run).**

- **Covered riders (gap 8).** Shots already left a rider the hull covers to the hull; blasts did not, so the Wraith's Elite driver could die to a rocket before the Wraith, and the player in a Banshee paid twice (the pilot at the Banshee's 0.8 and the hull in full). Now every blast (`areadamage`: rockets, fuel rods, mortars, the Scorpion's shell, the needle supercombine; frag and plasma grenades; `haloareablast`, below) skips a victim sitting in a seat the hull covers of a vehicle with a body vitality (`halovehicleridercovered`, the same rule as `halovehicletrace`: an invisible seat, the Wraith's driver; or a vehicle whose coll takes its children's damage, the Banshee's pilot, the player included). The hull takes the blast and the riders die with the vehicle. Exceptions: a plasma grenade stuck to the rider itself still hurts it; with vehicle damage off (`halo_vehicledamage 0`, `qa_vehicle_protect 1`, the QA vehicle walker) riders take blasts as before. The Ghost's seat stays open: its rider takes a blast in its own right and the Ghost's hull takes it too, as Halo's open seat does. The Warthog, Scorpion and turret have no vitality, so their riders take blasts as before (at the Warthog's 0.8 and the Scorpion's 0.5 for the player, `halovehicledamage`).
- **The death explosion's damage (gap 11).** `tools/halo_effects.py` now writes, after an effect's `blast` line, its strongest damaging `jpt!` part as `damage <inner> <outer> <lower> <upper min> <upper max> <side effect> <category> <core>` and that part's 33 material modifiers as `materials` (optional lines; the format stays 1 and older games skip them). The vehicles' death explosion (`vehicles\wraith\effects\destroyed explosion`) deals 55 to 65 inside 1.5 world units, 55 at 2.5, category 4 (high explosive); its modifier is 0 for every terrain and vehicle material (metal_thick included, so a destroyed vehicle sets off no other: no chain of explosions, as in Halo), 1 for the cyborg, humans, Grunts and Jackals, 0.4 for Elites and 0.2 for Hunter armour (the material modifiers apply to actors as the weapons' do: Hunters, Sentinels, the Monitor and Engineers always, the rest with `halo_actormaterials 1`). A destroyed vehicle now deals it at its box's middle after its riders die (`haloeffectblast`, through `haloareablast` in `source/src/weapon.cpp`: full inside the inner radius down to the lower bound at the outer, a body needs a clear line from the centre to its middle unless inside the AOE core, covered riders of other vehicles are left to their hull). Since the fresh-start batch (the critic's worry 3 of batch 6; not verified in the game, `tests/test_vehicle_damage.py` item 9, not run) the blast always has no owner and is dealt as the scripts' blasts are (in the player's name inside a script-damage scope, `haloareablast`): a marine or crewman it kills does not count towards a betrayal and the player's own hurt is not scaled by difficulty, whoever destroyed the vehicle. Halo credits the kill to nobody (the vehicle is the damage owner, with no player), and since the ownerless-kill batch so does the engine (not verified in the game; `tests/test_vehicle_damage.py` item 9's Covenant run, not run): `haloareablast` with no owner opens an ownerless script-damage scope (`HaloScriptDamage(true)`, `haloactorscriptdamage(on, ownerless)` in `source/src/halo_ai.cpp`), and a kill inside it reaches `haloactordeath` with no killer, so the dead actor's hostile neighbours are no longer alerted to the player (before, the stock bookkeeping's player killer alerted them, as when the player destroyed the vehicle). The hit itself is still dealt in the player's name because AssaultCube's `dodamage` and `dokill` need an attacker, so the player's own hurt stays self-inflicted (no difficulty scale, no damage arc) and the stock console kill line and frag counter still name the player (neither is shown in the campaign HUD). The scripts' `effect_new` damage (`halo_effectdamage 1`) goes through the same null-owner path and is nobody's too, as in Halo (`hs_effect_new` creates the effect with owner `NONE`). Since the final integration review of the ownerless-kill batch the scripts' own kills are nobody's too (not verified in the game): `ai_kill`, `ai_kill_silent` and `ai_erase` (`Scenario::eraseSquads`), `ai_erase_all`, `damage_object` and `damage_new` now open an ownerless scope (`HaloScriptDamage(true)` in `source/src/halo_script.cpp` and `halo_script_ai.inc`), so no hostile neighbour of an actor they kill is alerted to the player (still no betrayal), as in Halo, where `ai_kill` is an act of god (`actor_kill` sets the unit's act-of-god death flags, `ai/actors.c` of `../Halo3DS`) and `hs_damage_object` and `hs_damage_new` deal their damage with owner `NONE` (`hs/hs_library_external.c`); before, the batch had left them naming the player as a judgement call. `halo_damagetrace 1` logs `DEATH_TRACE victim=... killer=<player|name|-> ownerless=<0|1> alerted=<n>` for every actor death. The riders a destroyed vehicle kills are still the destroyer's (the player's for a vehicle the player destroyed). It used to be in the destroyer's name, so a Ghost the player shot near marines could turn them. Decided from the Halo CE source port in `../Halo3DS` (not checked in Halo): `object_deplete_body` creates the body depleted effect with the vehicle itself as its damage owner (`damage_effect_new_on_object` in `objects/damage.c`), the effect's damage takes the owner object's `owner_player_index` (`effects/effects.c`), and only the player's biped is given one (`game/players.c`), so a scenario vehicle's explosion carries no player. As before, the blast still hurts the player and allies; with no owner the player's own hurt is not scaled by difficulty (as for the scripts' blasts). `halo_vehicleexplosion 0` turns it off; `halo_vehicletrace 1` logs `the Wraith's death explosion deals its damage`.
- **The wreck (gap 11, optional).** With `halo_vehiclewreck 1` (off by default) a destroyed vehicle that was on the ground (not a flying Banshee, not one carried) leaves its wreck where it blew up: a static box bodies collide with (`halovehiclecollide`; a body caught in it is pushed out, `depenetrate`), drawn with the vehicle's own model (the converted models have no destroyed permutation) and giving off smoke, until the section ends, a revert goes back past it (the checkpoint's vehicles replace it) the script creates the vehicle again or the script's `object_destroy` (or `object_destroy_all`) removes it; a Continue after such a removal brings the wreck back (the record keeps only the destroyed flag, not the wreck). It does not stop shots or vehicles, has no seats and is not a scenario object any more. A Continue leaves the wreck of every destroyed ground vehicle in the save when the cvar is on (the record keeps the vehicle's last pose). Off by default because the planned routes were made without wrecks: one left on a walking route could block it, and none of this has been seen.
- QA hooks: `qa_vehicle_blast VEHICLE TYPE SIDE` (a grenade of the player's goes off SIDE units past the vehicle's right side at its middle height; `VEHICLE_QA blast` with the driver's health and shield before and after and whether the hull covers it), `qa_vehicle_tilt VEHICLE PITCH ROLL` (held there, asleep), `qa_vehicle_probe VEHICLE ALONG UP` (a level segment across it: `hit`, `fraction` and the level box's `back`, `front`, `halfwidth` and `top`) and `qa_vehicle_wrecks VEHICLE` (the wreck count and whether its wreck stops the player's body).

### Planned vehicle routes (batch 7)

**Not verified in the game yet**: written while testing was on hold; nothing of it has been driven or flown. Re-run `python3 tools/import_halo_campaign.py "$maps" --mission d40 --part 6` (7, 8) and `--mission c40 --part 1` (4) after `tools/halo_vehicles.py`; the layouts and so the `.hscb` projections are unchanged.

The driven and flown sections (`DRIVEN_SECTIONS`) used to keep the render raster's route, spawns and markers, whose end could lie far from the exit (c40 section 1's 90 units off, ending the section early at its raster end marker; d40 section 8's 176). `VEHICLE_ROUTES` in `tools/import_halo_campaign.py` now gives each a list of legs (walk, board, fly, drive), planned in order on the section's retail collision by `plan_vehicle_route`; its rows are written to `packages/maps/<section>.route` and its cells become the section's route markers, insertion and checkpoint (the checkpoint on a floor, never mid-flight), and the metrics carry `vehicle_route` (rows, vehicle rows, boardings, get-outs, extra BSPs), its `ends_in_exit` and the exit offset to the exit volume's middle. The raster's floor still holds the native cubes, the actors and the items.

- **Driving** (`VehicleGround` in `tools/halo_collision.py`): floor samples of every collision polygon whose normal is at least 0.4 (`VEHICLE_SLOPE`: the engine's car treats faces under 0.3 as walls and climbs steeper than the player with a run-up) with open space round the chassis (the BSP's open-space test on a ring of the chassis capsule's radius plus 0.4 at its bottom and top, following the polygon's slope), eight neighbours a step, rises within the step plus the slope, ledge drops of up to 4 Halo units (`VEHICLE_DROP`), cells near an edge costing more, the chassis's front checked ahead of each step, and jumps. A jump (batch 10, mirroring the engine's car, Vehicles above): where the floor ends, the car leaves the ground at the drive's speed along the surface its trailing wheels were on (the slope of the route's last two cells, up or down, at most 0.8 of the speed upwards), falls at the engine's retail-derived vehicle gravity (`vehicle_gravity`, the same formula as `vehicleGravity`; 40 units a second squared before), with open space at the body's middle and at its front along the arc (a doorway's lintel or a hanging slab meets the front first), and lands on the first floor under its path that has floor under both axles (`supported`: not a strut or a beam's edge), up to 25 Halo units below the take-off (`VEHICLE_JUMP_DROP`: the engine hurts neither a vehicle nor its riders when it lands, and the retail Warthog has no body vitality, so this only bounds the search; it was the 4 unit ledge drop before). The drive is planned at 0.9 of the car's top speed (the speed after a run-up); where no drive arrives at that speed, at 0.85, 0.8, 0.75 or 0.7 of it (`DRIVE_SPEEDS`), and the importer prints which (`the drive ... arrives only at N units a second`). The rows are the vehicle's floor point (flag 4096 with `warthog`; a jump's landing has flag 2 too). Each driven section's metrics record the jumps, the drive's time at its speed, the gravity, the speed and the top speed (`vehicle_route.drive`). Results: d40 section 6 (955 rows, from the elevator landing up the curved ramp, whose faces are 30 to 46 degrees, to `bsp 5,6`; the scenario has two `bsp 5,6` volumes and the planner takes the one by the exit, `nearest_volume_box`; about 34 s at 32.4 units a second, no jumps), section 7 (677 rows to `bsp 6,7`, about 24 s, its one jump the drop into the exit, as before) and section 8 (673 rows to `grand_finale`, about 30 s at 27 units a second, three jumps: the pit room's two and an 11 unit hop over a gap at about x 688; until batch 10 it stopped 176.7 units short at the pit room, and was a stub). Both climbing sections plan as before.
- **d40 section 8's pit room** (retail x about 878 to 925). The run's road enters it at the top and the corridor down to the Longsword's hangar leaves it 20 Halo units lower; between them hang the broken upper bridge's pieces (a thin slab level with the road, then a 4.5 Halo unit thick segment, retail x about 897 to 905, whose underside is 7.6 Halo units below the road), and under those the lower bridge, one long bowl (its middle 3.2 Halo units below its ends, a loose plank over it) whose far side rises at 30 and then 14 degrees to its end, 4 Halo units short of the corridor's doorway. The planned drive leaves the road's end (its last 7 units rise at 5 degrees), falls 51.5 units (23 Halo units) under the hanging segment and lands in the bowl 51 units on, rolls through it and up its far side, and is kicked off the 14 degree lip over the gap and the doorway's ledge into the corridor (18 units, under the doorway's lintel). The upper bridge is not drivable: its pieces stand higher than the road's end or overhang the bowl. Judgement call: the drop is the room's only way down that the geometry leaves, so it is taken to be the retail run's way; it was not checked against the retail game (Halo's Warthog at its own full speed flies the same arc as the engine's at 36, so how retail players pass the hanging segment, slower, or pitching nose-down off the lip, is not known). The drop clears the hanging segment only below about 0.78 of the Warthog's top speed (planned with the fall starting where the engine's car leaves the floor: once its rear wheels, 0.7 of the half length behind its origin, are past the edge; it stays level on them until then): at 0.8 and faster the chassis's front meets the segment, by more the faster; so the drive is planned at 0.75 of top speed, 27 units a second (still planned with the edge a whole cell on), and a player must ease off at the end of the road (the HUD shows the speed); since the jump governor batch the engine does that for a player at full throttle (Jump governors, below). The implementer's first plan started the fall at the last floor cell, about 2 units early, and so planned 0.8 (28.8), which the corrected plan finds hits the segment. The tests cap the QA throttle at that speed (`qa_vehicle_cruise <map> <speed>`, from `vehicle_route.drive.speed`: `tests/test_retail_collision.py`, `tests/test_maw_drive.py`, `tests/test_vehicle_jump.py`); the walker's code is unchanged. The kick off the bowl has a margin too: at 28.8 units a second (more at 27) a launch steeper than about 26 degrees would put the chassis's top into the lintel; the engine's trailing-wheel launch should leave at about the lip's 14 degrees (the slope between the axles there would give about 20 to 23, which is why the launch is measured under the trailing wheels). With retail gravity at the section's scale but the engine's speed (7.2 units a second squared) the car overshoots the bowl and finds no way; the old 4 unit limit on a jump's drop ruled the drop out whatever the gravity. The Maw's escape countdown is 180 s for sections 6 to 8; the planned drive takes about 88 s (`tests/test_traversal_routes.py` checks it is at most two thirds of the countdown). The raster's native actor spawns of section 8 (anchored a quarter and three quarters along the route) went from 2 to 0 with the longer route; since the jump governor batch a driven section's anchor that finds no actor moves to the nearest route cell that has some in reach (Native spawns on driven sections, below), and section 8 has 2 again; the mission script's squads are unaffected. Not modelled: the phys tag's air friction (0.005 a tick for the Warthog), which would shorten the arcs a little; the retail Warthog's rigid-body pitch over a lip.
- **Jump governors** (the jump governor batch; an engine accommodation and a judgement call, not verified in the game). The pit room's drop works only below about 0.78 of the Warthog's top speed (and not much slower: batch 10's critic put the window's floor at about 0.65), and its arc at full throttle does not depend on the top speed (the vehicle gravity scales with the square of the engine's speed over the retail one), so a lower top speed would not help: a player at full throttle meets the hanging segment. For every jump of a drive leg planned slower than `DRIVE_SPEEDS[0]` the importer writes `halo_vehiclegovern X Y Z RADIUS SPEED` into the section's git-ignored map `.cfg` (`GOVERNOR_SECONDS` and `GOVERNOR_RADIUS` in `tools/import_halo_campaign.py`: the take-off row's point, a sphere of one second of travel at the leg's speed, at least 12 units, and the leg's speed; the metrics' `vehicle_route.drive.governors` list the same, and `cfg_current` says whether the `.cfg` on disk is what the importer writes). d40 section 8 gets three, at the pit room's road end (459,154,7.75), the bowl's lip (541,154,-38.25) and the hop at about x 688 (688,153,-63.25), radius 27, speed 27; no other section has a drive planned below full speed. In `source/src/halo_vehicle.cpp` (`govern`, checked every 8 ms step of `halovehicledrive`), within a governor's sphere the player's own car (keys or QA input; not a flyer, not reversing, not an actor's) gets no throttle at or above the speed and the brakes above it by more than 0.25 units a second, so it stays within about a step's braking of the cap at any frame rate; the Warthog brakes from 36 to 27 in about 9.5 units, a third of the radius. `halo_vehiclegovernor` (persistent, default 1) turns the governors off; `halo_vehiclegovernorhint` (default 1) shows "Easing off for the jump" with the held speed under the crosshair while one holds the car. The map `.cfg` runs in AssaultCube's isolated map-config context, so `source/src/main.cpp` allows the one command there (`scriptcontext MAPCFG halo_vehiclegovern`, before `scontext.cfg` seals the contexts); every map load path resets (`resetmap`, which now empties the loading list: `halovehiclegovernorsreset`), runs the map's `.cfg` (the commands fill the loading list), then calls `startmap`, which makes the list the map's governors (`halovehiclegovernorsadopt`, before `startmap`'s no-respawn return). Before the ownerless-kill batch `halovehiclesclear` (from `halomapstart`) adopted them, which the edit-mode xmap restore never reaches, so its governors leaked into the next map loaded (duplicates on d40 section 8, governors on a map with none); not verified in the game. Judgement calls: every jump of a slow leg is governed, not only the drop (the leg's arcs were all planned at that speed); jumps of drives planned at 0.9 of top speed (d40 section 7's) are not governed; the governor only caps, so a player slower than about 0.65 of top speed can still fall short; the retail way through the room is unconfirmed (above). `qa_vehicle_governors` lists them; `qa_vehicle_cruise` stays for the tests' capped passes.
- **Native spawns on driven sections** (the jump governor batch, not verified in the game). The raster's two native encounters stand by anchors a quarter and three quarters along the route (actors within 55 units, placed within 35 of the anchor). On a driven section (`VEHICLE_ROUTES`, not flown) an anchor that finds no actor moves to the route cell nearest it from which actors not yet placed are in reach: the first encounter's within the route's first half, the second's past the first's anchor, so they keep their order. d40 section 8: encounter 1 at route step 232 (326,151), a Flood actor; encounter 2 at step 321 (415,151), an Elite with a shotgun at about 381,147, which stands before the halfway checkpoint (step 336), so it spawns behind the player at the second contact (judgement call: kept, the drive goes on past it). Its `.cgz` changed only in those two entities (the cubes, route, `.hcol` and mesh are the same); sections 6 and 7 find actors at their anchors and are unchanged.
- **Flying** (`FlightSpace`): a coarse 3D grid (`FLIGHT_CELL`, 4 campaign units) of body positions for the Banshee (the engine's flight capsules plus a margin of 1), free where the body's box is open space in the BSPs the section holds (points beyond them all are open: the engine loads nothing there) and no polygon, two-sided ones included, crosses the lines from its middle to its sides; a weighted A* (1.6) to a low hover over a landing cell, straightened where the body moves in a straight line, resampled every two cells (flag 4096 with `banshee`: the vehicle's origin), then a get-out row (16384) at the hover. The landing cell is under open sky (2 to 10 Halo units clear above), with room for the Banshee hovering 0.25 to 1 unit over it, and the walk from it to the leg's target shortest (the walking planner run from the target outwards), else the nearest such cell at the target's height.
- **Walking and boarding**: the walk legs use the walking planner (`retail_route`) on the standing floors with the walls, doors and parked vehicles; a board row (8192 with the vehicle type) stands where the walk ends, beside the scenario vehicle's driver seat (c40 section 1's `fly_away_1`) or at the start (c40 section 4, whose Banshee the engine puts beside the player).
- **c40 section 4** (167 rows since the final integration review resampled its flights at most 7 units apart, so no two flight rows are more than 8.5 apart once rounded to cells, as `tests/test_traversal_routes.py` checks; 134 before): boards the Banshee beside the start, flies 30 points to the landing pad and walks into `bsp 0,5`. Ends in its exit. Its end anchor (the raster's, on the canyon floor) is kept for the layout, so the map and scripts are unchanged; the old raster end marker there, which could end the section before the flight, is gone.
- **c40 section 1** (1,576 rows (1,557 before the final integration review resampled its flights), ends in `bsp 2,10`; the route batch, gap 2 of batch 5's critic, not walked): walks from the control room to `fly_away_1`, boards it and flies to pulse generator 1's platform, walks through the doorway corridor into the generator room and into `pulse_1_trigger` (which powers `door_c1`), back to the Banshee, flies down to the c bridge before `door_c1` and walks through it into `bsp 2,10`. The generator room is BSP 12, which BSP 2 walls in: `EXTRA_BSPS` bakes BSP 12's collision into the section's `.hcol` (4,010 surfaces, so the player can walk in rather than into the void) and draws its render in the `.mesh` (`write_world_mesh`'s `extra_bsps`). Until the route batch the route stopped on the platform, which batch 7 put down to the doorway being narrower than the engine's body at the section's outdoor scale (4.1). Measured, it is not: the corridor (retail y -619 to -635.4, x 251.1 to 253.1) is 1.2 units wide at its floor (z 103.48), 2.0 at 0.8 up, about 1.75 at 1.27 (the top of the engine's body at 4.1) and 2.8 high, and the body at 4.1 is 0.54 across and 1.27 tall, so it fits with room to spare and no scale change or doorway assist is needed (the section's layout, and so `c40.hscb`, which was re-imported and came out byte for byte the same, are unchanged). What kept the walk out was the two BSPs' seals: BSP 2 and BSP 12 overlap in the corridor (y -621.75 to -634.48), and each closes it with a polygon without a material (BSP 2's at y -621.75, BSP 12's at y -634.48, across the whole doorway), the face a BSP ends a doorway with; Halo switches BSPs in the corridor, so its player never meets either, but the section holds both, so the planner's wall test (and the engine, which collides with every polygon in the `.hcol`) met BSP 12's seal. `held_bsps` in `tools/import_halo_campaign.py` (with `overlap_seals` in `tools/halo_collision.py`) now leaves out of the walking and of the `.hcol` each seal whose middle is open space in the other BSP on both sides (two here; a section without `EXTRA_BSPS` is unchanged). The flight down to the c bridge could not end over its pad (the landing between the bridge's railings has no flight-grid node within 3 units free of them): where no node over a pad is free, a flight may now end on a node within 6 units that sees the hover point in a straight line, and the get-out row is at the hover point as before (the other flights, which have such a node, are planned as before). The walker has no door row at `door_c1`: it walks into it, as at the other walked sections' scripted doors, once `pulse_1_trigger` has powered it. Left out of `KNOWN_STUBS` and `KNOWN_BLOCKED`, in `PENDING_WALK`.
- **The walker** (`qaRetailVehicle` in `tests/native/retail_qa.inc`): on a board row it walks to the nearest vehicle of that type's driver seat and presses use; on drive and fly rows, seated (the Maw's escape seats the player itself; otherwise use gets in, or flips the vehicle back first), it holds full throttle steering at a row a little ahead (`qa_vehicle_input` with the camera steer; a flyer's nose at the row, the climb trimming the height, easing off only before a get-out), counts rows reached within reach of the vehicle (up to 40 ahead), backs off a second when it stalls, and fails after 25 s without a row (`FAIL vehicle-stuck`); on a get-out row it hovers down over the row and presses use. It keeps every vehicle from harm (`halo_vehicledamage 0`) as it protects the player, logs `RETAIL_QA vehicle ...` lines every 5 s and removes hostiles within 40 units as the foot walker does. The movement-feel trial skips vehicle rows, and the fall-recovery resync is not applied while seated or just in or out of a seat. Not done: a second QA walker in `vehicle_qa.inc` (the route walker drives these rows itself).
- To put a section back on its raster route, delete its `VEHICLE_ROUTES` entry (and for c40 section 1 its `EXTRA_BSPS` entry) and re-run its import.

## Campaign asset library

Run `python3 tools/import_halo_asset_library.py "$maps"` to extract the Xbox campaign source assets into `build/halo-assets`. Each mission's JSON index maps original tag IDs and virtual addresses to its tag region, BSPs, texture images and mip chains, sound permutations, model parts, animation tracks and recordings. The material and lens-flare entries identify their bitmap dependencies and preserve the authored stages and reflections. Shared payloads use their SHA-256 as the filename in `blobs/`. Use `--mission` for a single cache or `--output` for a different library path. The playable app uses the converted assets in `packages/`. The source library is available for subsequent engine integration.

## Retail weapons

`tools/halo_weapons.py` reads every `weap` tag in the ten campaign caches, follows each trigger to its projectile and damage tags, and writes `packages/halo/weapons.txt`. The game loads that table at startup. Rounds, reload time, rate of fire and its ramp, spread, heat, battery, charge time, zoom levels, aim assist, projectile speed and homing, and damage all come from the tags. The importer also records the first-person and pickup models from the asset library, so run it after `import_halo_library.py` and before `import_halo_campaign.py`. The campaign importer stores each enemy's carried weapon (from its actor variant) and the scenario's weapon placements in the section maps.

```bash
python3 tools/halo_weapons.py "$maps"
python3 tools/import_halo_campaign.py "$maps"
```

The player carries two of the nine campaign weapons. Walking over a weapon you carry takes its rounds, and **E** swaps the current weapon for one on the ground. **Mouse 2** or **Z** steps through the weapon's zoom levels. Holding fire charges the plasma pistol. Covenant weapons show battery and heat instead of rounds, and overheat. Enemies fire their actor's weapon with the variant's share of its damage (rolled and exact per shot, Damage fidelity) and drop it when killed. Zoom steps play the weapon tag's zoom-in and zoom-out sounds; a weapon whose HUD tags carry no reticle (the flamethrower) gets a plain one, and the rocket launcher sits a little lower, further right and forward than its animation holds it (Presentation; none of it seen in the game yet).

**Starting profiles (batch 8, not verified in the game).** A mission's player now starts with the scenario's player starting profile 0, not the old fixed assault rifle, pistol and two frags. `tools/import_halo_scripts.py` (`profile_rows`) writes every starting profile into the `.hscb` (version 9): the name, the starting health and shield modifiers, the primary and secondary weapons as retail tag paths with their rounds loaded and total, and the frag and plasma counts (scenario +840, 104-byte rows). The engine resolves the weapons against `weapons.txt` when it applies a profile (`haloweaponfind`), so regenerating the weapon table keeps them right. Profile 0 per mission: a10 none (the player starts unarmed, no grenades), a30 the assault rifle 60 + 120 and the pistol 12 + 64 with no grenades, a50 the sniper rifle 4 + 64 and the assault rifle 60 + 360 with four frags, b30 the rifle 60 + 240 and pistol 12 + 72, b40 60 + 300 and 12 + 96, c10 60 + 180 and 12 + 96 with four frags and four plasma grenades, c20 60 + 180 and 12 + 60, c40 the plasma pistol and the shotgun 12 + 48 with four frags and two plasma, d20 the rifle 60 + 240 and the plasma pistol, d40 the rifle 60 + 120 and the plasma rifle with two of each grenade (most with four frags; see `tests/test_starting_profiles.py`). A fresh start (a new mission, a section started on its own, Restart section without a carried loadout) takes profile 0 once the mission script is loaded (`applyStartingProfile` in `source/src/halo.cpp`), and that becomes the loadout a revert before the section's first checkpoint returns to; a section entered from the one before keeps what the player carried, and Continue what was saved. `FreshProfile` adds to a fresh start of a later section what an earlier section's script gives and a script started afresh there does not give again: a10's `bridge0_profile` (the pistol) from section 3 on. Its counterpart for the script, `freshScripts` in `source/src/halo_script.cpp` (gap 6), brings a10's mission script forward on a fresh start of sections 5 and 6 (the notes on a10). `player_add_equipment <unit> <profile> <reset>` is bound for the player: `reset` true replaces the loadout (c20's `easy_start` on Easy: the shotgun and pistol, four frags), false adds to it (a10's `bridge0_profile` at Keyes's bridge: the pistol into the empty selected slot); a weapon already carried takes the rounds, a full pair takes none. Judgement calls, not checked against the retail executable: "rounds total" is the reserve beyond the magazine, clamped to the weapon's maximum; a battery weapon starts charged whatever its rounds say; a modifier of 0 leaves health or shield as they are (a10's bridge profiles have 0), otherwise it is a fraction of full. With an `.hscb` older than version 9 (or without `weapons.txt`) the old default loadout stays. Re-run `python3 tools/import_halo_scripts.py "$maps"` after pulling this (version 9 files are the version 8 ones byte for byte up to the new trailer, but their hash changes, so a game saved before continues at its checkpoint with the script restarted).

**Empty weapon slots.** With `weapons.txt` a carried slot may be empty. An empty slot is AssaultCube's knife slot: nothing is drawn in the player's hands, no reticle (retail or fallback) shows, the fire button does nothing (`haloplayerunarmed`, `shoot` in `weapon.cpp`), melee still strikes (50 points, the fallback melee damage) and the retail HUD shows the unit panels without a weapon panel. A weapon picked up fills the empty slot and is selected (with both slots armed **E** swaps as before); switching weapons does nothing with one weapon or none. The progress file writes `empty <slot>` for an empty slot; a slot with neither a `weapon` nor an `empty` line (older saves, saves made without `weapons.txt`) gets the default weapon, as before.

**Damage (`jpt!`).** Weapons, melee and grenades read their damage with one layout, `JPT_*` in `tools/halo_weapons.py`, checked against the bytes of every campaign `jpt!` tag (not yet tried in the game). From the tag body: radius bounds at 0, cutoff scale at 8, flags at 12; the damage section at 452 holds side effect and category (two 16-bit enums), damage flags, the area core radius, then the damage lower bound at +12, the upper bound's min and max at +16 and +20, the vehicle passthrough penalty at +24, active camouflage damage, stun, maximum stun and stun time, and instantaneous acceleration at +48. The frag explosion reads 80 lower, 120-120 upper; the sniper bullet 101-101 with a 0.5 passthrough. `weapons.txt` is format 3 and `equipment.txt` format 2: `projectile`, `melee`, `blast` and `attached` lines carry lower, upper min and upper max, and format 3 adds to each `projectile` line the area's inner radius, the projectile's air damage range (`proj` +84 after the object section, two floats in world units) and the `jpt!` side effect. The game refuses older tables, so re-run `tools/halo_weapons.py` after pulling this. How the bounds are used is described under Damage fidelity below.

### Damage fidelity

Written while testing was on hold; nothing here has been run in the game (`tests/test_damage_model.py` checks it, and `tests/test_melee_materials.py` the batch 9 additions: the melee from behind, the AOE core, the needle supercombine; neither has been run).

**The jpt! bounds.** A full-strength hit deals a random amount in the upper bound's range; a weakened hit at scale s deals `lower + (full - lower) * s`, the reading of Halo's damage formula used here (the community tag definitions describe the lower bound as what a scaled hit falls to; not checked against the retail executable). Scale comes from:

- **Range falloff.** An impact closer than the near end of its projectile's air damage range deals full strength, falling linearly to the lower bound at the far end and staying there (a far end of 0 means no falloff). From the tags: shotgun pellet 1.5-3 world units (18-25 down to 8), plasma rifle bolt 20-50 (12-14 down to 10), plasma pistol bolt 20-50 (16-20 down to 10), Ghost and Banshee bolts 20-50 (10-14 down to 10), covenant turret bolt 0-100 (12-14 down to 8), Warthog chaingun 0-100 (16-24 down to 16). The assault rifle (5-15), pistol, sniper rifle, needle, the charged plasma bolt (5-20) and flame are flat (lower equal to upper), so their range does not matter. The distance is the shot's travel (hitscan: muzzle to impact) in Halo world units. Falloff applies to impact damage only, not to the blast of an exploding projectile (rockets, fuel rods, mortars, the Scorpion shell), and to the player's and the actors' shots alike.
- **Blast edges.** Area damage is full inside the inner radius and falls to the lower bound at the outer radius; beyond the outer radius there is none, so the blast steps from the lower bound to nothing at its edge. Grenades: frag 1.5-2.5, plasma 0.5-2.0 world units, both 120 at the centre and 80 at the edge. Exploding projectiles now use their inner radius too (rocket 0.5-2.0, 300-330 down to 80; fuel rod 0.5-1.5, 75 down to 40); before, they fell to none at the outer radius. The `jpt!` cutoff scale is zero in every campaign tag. The AOE core radius (batch 9; frag and plasma grenades 0.4, rocket 0.6 world units, a `core` line after the projectile or blast, older games skip it) is read as the radius within which the blast is at full strength and, for a grenade, reaches a body even when the line of sight from its centre is blocked (a grenade at rest against a wall or the floor has its centre a hair inside it). Only the rocket changes: full strength out to 0.6 instead of 0.5. This reading is an engine judgement, not checked against the retail executable; `halo_aoecore 0` ignores the radius.
- **Melee** keeps the full-strength roll (50-60; the flamethrower 70-80). The melee tags' radius bounds are 0.5-0.5, which gives no scale between full strength and the lower bound, so there is no sensible mapping for the lower bound (40). Every campaign weapon's melee carries side effect 2, "lethal to the unsuspecting" (Halo's kill from behind), which is now modelled (batch 9): the `melee` line has a fourth field, the side effect (the energy sword's is 3, EMP), and the player's melee on an actor from behind (the player more than 120 degrees off where it faces) that is not fighting the player (`ai_status` below combat, or its target someone else) deals the actor's whole shield and health, whatever the shield's modifier for the blow (`DAMAGE_UNSUSPECTING` with `halo_damagetrace 1`). A body its material leaves unhurt stays unhurt (the Monitor; a Hunter's shield arm can never be behind it), the player's empty-handed blow carries side effect 2 too, and the actors' melee is not affected. `halo_meleeunsuspecting 0` turns it off. The 120 degrees and the awareness rule are engine judgements. **Allies and story figures (batch 5's critic, a judgement call; not verified in the game, `tests/test_melee_materials.py` item 6 and `tests/test_ai_attach.py` item 11, not run):** the kill from behind now applies only to an actor hostile to the player at the time. The player's allies (the marines, the friendly Sentinels; a betrayal that turns them hostile turns it back on) and every actor `ai_attach` made of a story figure (a50's Keyes and freed marines, c10's `marine_suicidal`) take the melee's normal 50 to 60 instead (`DAMAGE_UNSUSPECTING_SPARED` with `halo_damagetrace 1`), which still counts towards a betrayal. Why: the tags give side effect 2 to every weapon's melee and say nothing about allies; the kill from behind is described for Halo CE as a way to kill enemies unaware of the player, and whether Halo CE's own check (which is in the executable, not the tags) also kills an ally that is not in combat could not be confirmed from the tags or the scripts, while an instant kill there would let one stray tap from behind end a50 (Keyes's death is `game_lost_script`'s loss) or kill a marine the scripts count on. If it is later confirmed that Halo kills allies from behind too, dropping the ally test in `halodamage` (keeping the story-figure one) restores it.

**Grenades by thrower.** The player's grenades roll the retail full-strength range (120, and a stuck plasma grenade adds its attached 120). Grenades thrown by actors roll from the lower bound, 80 to 120, the balance they had before the `jpt!` layout correction, for the blast and a stuck plasma grenade's attached damage alike; with the edge falloff their blast deals 80 to 120 anywhere inside the outer radius. Against the player's 150 of shield and health on Normal an actor's frag at the player's feet deals 80 to 120 (Heroic 104 to 156, Legendary 136 to 204), and at the edge of its blast 80 (it dealt nothing there before).

**EMP.** Side effect 3, carried by the plasma grenade's blast and attached damage and by the plasma pistol's charged bolt (and the needle supercombine, below): the shield the hit reaches is drained to nothing after the hit is taken, the player's (its overshield layers too, ending the powerup) and any actor's (Elites; Jackals' shields count too). The hit itself is absorbed as usual, so EMP does not add damage to health. Vehicles inside a plasma grenade's outer radius, the one it is stuck to and one a charged bolt hits are stunned for 2 s (`HALO_EMP_STUN_MILLIS`; the tags' stun times are all zero, so the duration is the engine's): a vehicle's throttle does nothing (it coasts and steers; a hover craft does not strafe either, a Banshee sinks), whether the player or an actor is at the controls, and the seat's gun cannot fire, the player's or an actor gunner's. Charged bolts now stop on a vehicle's body; other shots still pass through vehicles. Stunning a vehicle is Halo 2's behaviour more than Halo CE's; it is here because it was asked for.

**Needle supercombine (batch 9).** The needle's projectile has the "has super combining explosion" flag (0x8) and a super detonation effect (`proj` +16: `weapons\needler\effects\explosion`, whose `jpt!` `weapons\needler\explosion` deals 60, falling to 40 over 0.5 to 1.0 world units, side effect 3, EMP), now written as a `supercombine <timer> <inner> <outer> <lower> <upper min> <upper max> <side effect> <category>` line after the needle's projectile (timer 0.75 s, the stuck needle's detonation timer) and a `sound supercombine` line (`needler_super_expl`, decoded by the audio import). The player's needles that strike one body (any body but the player's) count while they are stuck, each for its 0.75 s; when seven are stuck at once they detonate together at the body: the super detonation's area damage from the player (the shields it reaches drained, the vehicles in it stunned) and its sound (`SUPERCOMBINE` with `halo_damagetrace 1`). The tags hold no needle count: 7 is the engine's (about what the needler's full rate of fire, 10 a second, lands inside one timer), `halo_supercombine N` changes it and 0 turns it off. The needles themselves still deal their damage when they strike (not after their timer), and the actors' needles on the player do not combine.

**Enemy fire.** Every actor shot now rolls the weapon's full-strength range (upper min to upper max), applies the range falloff, and multiplies by the variant's damage modifier (0.5 without one); halodamage then applies the difficulty and the vehicle cut. The amount stays fractional: `halohit` hands it to halodamage, the shield takes its exact share, and the health damage keeps the fraction to the next hit (per target), so a 6.5 bolt costs 6.5 points on average where the slot damage dealt 6 or 7. An actor's shot deals at least 1 point, the floor the integer slot damage had. That floor is the one retune: without it the Sentinel beam (1 x 0.5) would take twice as long to kill. The player's shots go the same exact way (their rolls are unchanged). `halo_damagetrace 1` logs every hit halodamage sees (`DAMAGE_TRACE`).

Time to kill the player on Normal (shots from 75 shield and 75 health; plasma and needles hit the shield twice as hard; shield recharge ignored; the cadence is unchanged, so the time changes as the shots do). "Old" is the integer slot damage (the upper min times the modifier, rounded); "new" the mean of the roll times the modifier, kept exact. Computed from `weapons.txt` and `actors.txt` by `tests/test_damage_model.py`'s table check (it fails above 16% at close range); the combat range is the variants' mean of the `actr` combat range bounds.

| Actor weapon (modifier) | Old per shot | New per shot, close | Old shots | New shots, close | Change | At combat range (wu) | New per shot there | Change there |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Sentinel beam (0.5) | 1 | 1 (floor) | 150 | 150 | 0% | 6.5 | 1 | 0% |
| Covenant turret gun (0.33) | 4 | 4.33 | 38 | 35 | -8% | 0 | 4.33 | -8% |
| Assault rifle, Flood (0.4) | 4 | 4.00 | 38 | 38 | 0% | 3.0 | 4.00 | 0% |
| Flamethrower, Flood (0.4) | 4 | 4.00 | 38 | 38 | 0% | 1.8 | 4.00 | 0% |
| Fuel rod, Grunt spec-ops and Hunter (0.5) | 38 | 37.5 | 4 | 4 | 0% | 2.8-7.0 | 37.5 | 0% |
| Needler, Flood (0.4) | 4 | 4.00 | 28 | 29 | +4% | 3.0 | 4.00 | +4% |
| Needler, Elites and Grunts (0.5) | 5 | 5.00 | 23 | 23 | 0% | 4.2 | 5.00 | 0% |
| Needler, Elite spec-ops (0.6) | 6 | 6.00 | 19 | 19 | 0% | 6.0 | 6.00 | 0% |
| Pistol, Flood (0.4) | 10 | 10.0 | 15 | 15 | 0% | 3.0 | 10.0 | 0% |
| Plasma pistol, Flood (0.4) | 6 | 7.20 | 19 | 16 | -16% | 3.0 | 7.20 | -16% |
| Plasma pistol, Grunts and Engineers (0.5) | 8 | 9.00 | 14 | 13 | -7% | 4.0 | 9.00 | -7% |
| Plasma rifle, Flood (0.4) | 5 | 5.20 | 23 | 22 | -4% | 4.0 | 5.20 | -4% |
| Plasma rifle, Elite minor and major (0.5) | 6 | 6.50 | 19 | 18 | -5% | 5.0 | 6.50 | -5% |
| Plasma rifle, Elite commander and spec-ops (0.6) | 7 | 7.80 | 16 | 15 | -6% | 6.8 | 7.80 | -6% |
| Rocket launcher, Flood (0.4) | 120 | 126 | 2 | 2 | 0% | 5.0 | 126 | 0% |
| Shotgun, Flood (0.15, per pellet) | 3 | 3.23 | 4 | 4 | 0% | 2.5 | 1.88 | +50% |
| Sniper rifle, Flood (0.4) | 40 | 40.4 | 4 | 4 | 0% | 1.5 | 40.4 | 0% |

The rolling moves no time to kill by more than about 15% except the Flood's plasma pistol (-16%, its 6.4 per bolt was rounded down to 6 before); that is at the edge of the tolerance and is not retuned. The Flood shotgun is unchanged at close range but takes half as long again at its combat range of 2.5 world units, where its pellets have fallen most of the way to their lower bound: that is the retail range falloff, not the rolling, and is not retuned either; it is the first thing to check in play. Plasma falloff only starts at 20 world units, beyond every variant's combat range bounds, so Covenant plasma inside 20 world units is as in the table; beyond it (actors fire out to their maximum firing range, up to about 40) it weakens towards the lower bound, as the player's does. With the Monte Carlo of the rolls (not the means) the figures agree to within a shot.

## Grenades, equipment and weapon audio

**Not verified in the game yet**: everything in this section was written and compiled while testing was on hold. `tests/test_combat_equipment.py` is written but has not been run.

`tools/halo_weapons.py` also writes `packages/halo/equipment.txt` (git-ignored like the rest of `packages/`) and adds `sound` and `ammo` lines to `weapons.txt`. The equipment table holds the globals' two grenade types (carried maximum, the thrown projectile's fuse, whether its material responses attach it to units, the detonation's blast radius and damage and a stuck grenade's attached damage, the throw and explosion sounds), every campaign `eqip` tag (its powerup kind and time, pickup sound and pickup model, and the weapons whose magazines take it as ammunition) and the interface's low-shield and low-health sounds. `package-macos.sh` requires the table. Without it the frag grenade falls back to AssaultCube's grenade and there is no plasma grenade.

- **Grenades.** **G** throws the selected type, **H** (`halo_grenade_switch`) switches to the other type when you carry some. Both counts show under the retail grenade panel (which shows the selected type), the selected one in brackets. The last grenade of a type switches to the other, and picking up a type when the selected one has run out selects it, as in Halo. Each type is carried up to its tag maximum (4).
