Frag grenades bounce and explode once armed (1.5 s after the throw) and 0.5 s after their first bounce; plasma grenades stick to the unit or vehicle they touch (a bounce off a body, or coming to rest against one) and explode 2 s after sticking or coming to rest. The blast is the detonation effect's `jpt!`: full damage inside its inner radius, falling to the lower bound at the outer and none beyond, blocked by walls; the unit a plasma grenade is stuck to also takes the attached damage. Actors' grenades roll from the lower bound (Damage fidelity). Damage to the player goes through the difficulty scaling like any enemy weapon. A grenade the player throws can hurt the player. The plasma grenade's EMP drains the shields it reaches and stuns vehicles (Damage fidelity).
- **Actors** throw their variant's grenade type (`actors.txt`; the Covenant and Flood Elites throw plasma, marines and human Flood frags; a variant that names no known type throws plasma on the Covenant side and frags on the human side), and drop the grenades they still carry (at most two) beside their body when killed, unless `unit_doesnt_drop_items` is set.
- **Scenario equipment.** `tools/import_halo_campaign.py` writes each mission's scenario equipment placements (the `scnr` equipment block) that lie on the route's floor as `PLAYERSTART` entities with team 103: the `equipment.txt` index, the not-placed flags and the scenario object index. Sections with retail equipment no longer get the old stand-in ammunition, health and grenade boxes; sections without any keep them. Placements the script creates (flag 1, for example b40's supply dumps) appear on `object_create`/`object_create_containing` and go on `object_destroy`; the difficulty flags (not on Easy, Normal or Heroic, Legendary following Heroic) leave placements out as for vehicles. A checkpoint saves what is on the ground; a revert restores it. Re-run the campaign import (all missions) and `tools/halo_weapons.py` before it. The Crash Site (`halo_a30`) is imported like the other sections since batch 6 (`CRASH_SITE`) and gets the scenario's equipment the same way; the authored map (with `CRASH_SITE` off) keeps its stand-in items.
- **Pickups** happen by walking over an item that does the player good: an ammo pack adds its rounds to the carried weapon it belongs to (up to the weapon's maximum); a health pack restores full health; a grenade adds one of its type; the **overshield** fills the shield and charges two more layers over 1.5 s (three times the shield), which take damage first and drain over the powerup time (60 s); **active camouflage** lasts its powerup time (45 s) and fades over its last 3 s. Under camouflage actors see the player only within about an eighth of their vision range (two fifths for a second after the player fires), lose track of a player they were fighting after 4 s, and aim with extra error. The screen takes a faint tint and the HUD counts the camouflage down; the overshield shows as green, then red layers over the retail shield meter. Double speed and full-spectrum vision are not campaign pickups and stay on the ground.
- **Weapon audio** comes from the weapon tags for every campaign weapon the player or an actor fires: the first trigger's firing, empty (dry fire, and the overheat click of a Covenant weapon) and misfire effects, the second trigger's charged shot, and the first-person animation graph's sounds (ready, reload and empty reload, melee, the overheat, and the shotgun pump and rocket cycle played with each shot), plus the weapon's ammo pickup sound. Grenades play their throw and explosion sounds (and the plasma grenade its hiss), equipment its pickup sound, and the player hears the low-shield alarm while the shield is below a quarter and not recharging and the heartbeat while health is below a third with no shield. `tools/import_halo_audio.py` decodes every sound the two tables name from whichever campaign cache holds it to `packages/audio/halo/tags/` (`sound\sfx\weapons\needler\fire` becomes `tags/sfx-weapons-needler-fire.wav`); the flamethrower's firing effect has no sound of its own: its fire is the looping sound attached at its trigger marker (`sound loop`), held while the trigger is (Presentation). Re-run it after `tools/halo_weapons.py`:

```bash
python3 tools/halo_weapons.py "$maps"
python3 tools/import_halo_audio.py "$maps/a30.map" .
python3 tools/import_halo_campaign.py "$maps"
```

QA hooks (`tests/native/combat_qa.inc`): `qa_combat_state`, `qa_combat_items`, `qa_grenade_give`, `qa_grenade_select`, `qa_grenade_switch`, `qa_grenade_throw`, `qa_grenade_state`, `qa_grenade_face_actor`, `qa_item_drop`, `qa_item_goto`, `qa_camo_give`, `qa_combat_protect`, `qa_enemy_throw`, `qa_actor_grenades` and `qa_sound_table`.

## Actor AI

`tools/halo_actors.py` writes `packages/halo/actors.txt` from the campaign `actr` and `actv` tags: vision and hearing, firing range, rate and error, burst timing, combat range, target leading, grenades, vitality, shield-based hiding, panic chances, berserk thresholds and position hold times; format 2 adds each variant's unit team, unit melee and collision materials (Factions and special enemies); format 3 (the squad-count and major-upgrade batch) adds each variant's `major` (its actv's major upgrade reference, +36: 19 of the 89 variants have one) and the globals' 35-value difficulty table (`difficulty` lines, Difficulty). `haloactordefault`, which picks a variant for an actor spawned with only a family and weapon (a script's or a wave's), still prefers the minor rank; squad placement upgrades by Halo's rule instead (Difficulty). Run it after `halo_weapons.py`. `import_halo_scripts.py` then writes version 6 `.hscb` files. These add each encounter's combat positions, squad states and position-group masks, platoon rules, per-start variants and command lists, and the scenario's command lists.

The campaign ending is driven by the scripts. `game_won` on The Maw's last section, or the finale cue finishing that section, starts an ending: a credits roll over the level (Enter skips), `config/halo-progress.txt` marks the campaign beaten (`beaten 1`, with every mission unlocked and no game left to continue), and the game opens the title menu (**Halo**) over the level (until this batch it opened the **Halo Campaign** pause menu; the change is not yet verified in the game). `game_won` on earlier sections is ignored, because sections advance on their own. `game_lost` kills the player so the normal revert applies.

Campaign bots think with `source/src/halo_ai.cpp` instead of AssaultCube's bot AI. AssaultCube still handles movement, pathfinding and physics; its pathfinding follows the waypoints it floods over the section's cube raster, which since the cube-raster stamp holds the baked scenery (Scenery collision, The cube raster; not verified in the game). Actors:
- notice targets inside their vision cone, or hear gunfire, after a reaction delay
- alert nearby squadmates
- take combat positions from their squad's groups for attacking, defending or pursuing
- fire in retail bursts and throw grenades, scaled by difficulty for the teams hostile to the player (Difficulty, Combat by difficulty; the combat-scaling work, unverified)
- hide when their shields are low, panic when friends or leaders die, and go berserk when badly hurt
- follow command lists.

Marine and crew encounters now spawn on the player's side and fight the Covenant and Flood. Platoon rules switch squads between attacking and defending as casualties mount. The scripts' AI commands work, including `ai_actors` lists, `ai_status`, `ai_living_fraction`, `ai_strength`, magic sight, follow and fight orders, `ai_migrate` to new positions, command lists and their status, and `objects_can_see_*`. Vehicle seat and boarding commands act on the engine's vehicles and on dropships and Pelicans (see Vehicles, and Enemy and flying vehicles); for other vehicles they are stand-ins. The AI, unit and object-state commands live in `source/src/halo_script_ai.inc`. `ai_berserk`, `ai_grenades`, `ai_allow_dormant`, `ai_maneuver_enable`, `ai_renew`, `ai_prefer_target`, `ai_follow_target_ai`/`_disable`, `ai_follow_distance`, `ai_braindead_by_unit`, `ai_free`, `ai_try_to_fight_nothing`, `ai_link_activation` (waking the linked encounter once one is alert), `ai_teleport_to_starting_location*`, and the migrations (`ai_migrate_by_unit`, `ai_migrate_and_speak`, `ai_attach` on an actor) change what the actors do; flags survive a revert. `object_cannot_take_damage`, `unit_impervious` and `unit_doesnt_drop_items` apply to actors and the player, `unit_set_current/maximum_vitality` set the player's health and shield (maximum shield 0 removes the shield) or, since the script-vitality change (unverified), an actor's in Halo's points: the current as a fraction of its stored maximum (capped at 1), the maximum anew at full vitality (Not yet verified in the game, Script vitality in points), `unit_suspended` stops an actor thinking, and `object_set_permutation door_off` removes a cell door and its collision. `damage_object` and `damage_new` do not read the retail `jpt!` effects: names with guaranteed, doom, plummet or death kill, any other effect does 40 damage. `ai_attach` on a scripted biped in play makes it an actor of the squad (Factions and special enemies, Attached actors and story figures). Recorded but without visible effect: `unit_open/close`, `vehicle_hover`, `object_set_collideable`, `unit_set_enterable_by_player`, `ai_attach` on other scripted objects and inside scenes, `ai_attach_free` on scripted objects, `ai_automatic_migration_target`, `ai_exit_vehicle` and `ai_vehicle_enterable_*` on vehicles the engine does not drive, `ai_allow_dormant` (no dormancy exists), `unit_set_emotion`, flashlight state and `ai_dialogue_triggers`. `(player0)` now evaluates to the player and `(player1)` to nothing. Balance has only been checked in short QA runs: enemies are accurate at close range.

Only living actors count towards the 128-actor cap on scripted placements; dead squad entries stay for `ai_living_fraction` and to stop a killed start respawning. The Flood life cycle is not scripted: a dying Carrier Form bursts into Infection Forms, Infection Forms walk to a fallen marine or Elite (bodies last 90 s) and, on contact, are spent and turn the body into a Flood combat form 2.5 s later, and Infection Forms leap at a target 2.5 to 7 Halo units away every 1.5 to 3 s. **A carrier's forms join its squad** (the carrier-squad change; written while testing was on hold, not verified in the game; `tests/test_flood.py` items 7 to 11, not run). Halo's `actors_spawn_from_unit` puts the released actors in the dying unit's encounter and squad, alert, with no command list (`../Halo3DS/source/ai/actors.c:2855-2872`, `2921-2931`), so `halokilled` records the carrier's squad (`haloscenariosquadof`) and each form `spawnRing` places joins it (`haloscenarioadopt`, `Scenario::adoptSpawned`: the squad's combat positions and platoon posture, as a placed member gets). So `ai_living_count`, `ai_actors`, `ai_erase`, `ai_kill`, `ai_migrate`, `ai_magically_see_*` and the other switches, the platoon rules and `game_all_quiet` reach them; `ai_teleport_to_starting_location` passes over every swarm actor, placed or released, as Halo does (`ai_script.c:4175-4176`). The counts follow the port (`Scenario::countSquad`): `ai_living_count` is the living bodies, swarm or not (`ai_script.c:4711-4712`, `encounters.c:964-990`); `ai_nonswarm_count` leaves out the infection forms (4717-4718); `ai_living_fraction` is living bodies over the original count, which counts an actor once (`encounter_attach_actor`, `encounters.c:2124-2125`), and Halo keeps up to 16 living infection-form bodies of one variant and squad in one swarm actor (`actors.c:2539-2555`, the newest with room first), so a swarm squad's fraction can pass 1 (thirty placed forms are two actors: 15 while all live), and `ai_strength` counts a swarm actor's living over all its bodies. The platoon rules use the same counts. A static list of the retail calls (scratch analysis): no `ai_living_fraction` names an encounter with Flood swarms; c20's and d20's `enc*_spawner` scripts and d40's managers wait on `(< (ai_living_count <encounter>/carriers) min_carrier_spawn)`, which now counts the carriers' forms, so the next carriers come once those forms die too, as in Halo; d20's `enc4_3` reads `ai_strength enc4_3`; the `ai_nonswarm_count` waits (c20, c40, d20) are unchanged. A carrier outside every squad (stock wave sections, `qa_actor_spawn`) still bursts into squadless forms, where Halo releases none (2874; judgement call). The combat forms that rise from bodies an infection form reaches are the engine's own (the port has no such conversion) and stay squadless. **Erase:** Halo's `ai_erase` deletes the units (`actor_erase`, `actors.c:3845-3886`: no death, so no body destroyed effect), so `ai_erase` and `ai_erase_all` now delete at once (`haloerasebot`) the actors whose death would set something off (`Scenario::erasedWithoutDeath`: a `pop`, `spawn` or `destroyed` line: infection forms, carriers, Sentinels) instead of killing them, with no pop, burst or blast; other erased actors still fall where they stand (unchanged), and `ai_kill` still kills (the forms pop). **Checkpoints:** Halo's checkpoint is the whole game state, so a living released form is kept like any living member (`SquadBot::spawned`, its own start in `Scenario::spawnedStarts`): a revert and a Continue bring it back where it stood, with its fractions, as its variant, and a saved game writes it as an optional `s spawned` record (Title menu and saved games). Its swarm actor is kept through a revert; read from a saved game a form joins one afresh (judgement call). `halo_damagetrace 1` (or `halo_scripttrace 1`) logs `FLOOD_ADOPT <name> squad=<encounter>/<squad> variant= swarm=`, and `FLOOD_SPAWN` ends with `squad=<e>/<s>`; `qa_squad_counts <label> <encounter>` prints `SQUAD_COUNT` per squad (members, living, spawned, swarms, actors, fraction, strength, nonswarm), `qa_squad_kill_one <encounter/squad>` kills its first living member, and `SQUAD_STOOD` ends with `spawned=` and `swarm=`.

**The body destroyed blast** (same change, unverified). A unit destroyed after dying (unit flags bit 1) sets off its coll's body destroyed effect when `unit_destroy` runs (`units/units.c:2711-2716`, at the end of dying, `:6679` and `:10043`; `object_destroy`, `objects/damage.c:606-632`), before it releases its actors. `tools/halo_actors.py` writes it as an optional `destroyed <effe>` line (`destroyed_effect`; not for the infection forms, whose effect is the pop) and `tools/halo_effects.py` adds those effects to effects.txt: the carrier's `characters\floodcarrier\effects\body destroyed` (its jpt! `bdoy destroyed`: 1 to 2 world units, 40 to 70-85, metal 0, `carrier_boom`), the five Sentinel variants' `characters\sentinel\effects\death` (0.3 to 1, 30 to 60) and the Engineer's. `halokilled` queues it (`UnitBlast`) and `updateFlood` sets it off the next frame, before the burst it precedes (the burst waits a frame): drawn and heard (`halofxeffect`), its damage by `haloeffectblast` owned by the dead unit (Halo's effect damage is owned by the object, `effects/effects.c:920-947`, 1762-1772: its team's fire, the difficulty's enemy_damage on the player, no betrayal), so it hurts the player, nearby forms and other carriers (a chain goes off a frame apart) but never the forms it releases. `halo_unitblast 0` keeps the effect without its damage; `halo_damagetrace 1` logs `UNIT_BLAST variant= effect= dealt= owner=`. Re-run `tools/halo_effects.py`, `tools/halo_actors.py` and `tools/import_halo_audio.py` (the two new sounds). Older tables load (no line, no blast); an older engine skips the line.

**The carrier burst, the infection pop and the speed stagger from the tags** (the Flood tag batch: gap 5 of the batch 9 critic; written while testing was on hold, not verified in the game; `tests/test_flood.py` items 0, 2, 5 and 6, not yet run). `tools/halo_actors.py` adds three optional lines to an actor variant's record in actors.txt (format 3 still: an engine from before them skips keys it does not know, and an actors.txt without them loads, with the fallbacks below):
- **`spawn <actor index> <lower> <upper> <throw velocity>`**, from the unit tag's `spawned_actor_variant`, `spawn_actor_lower_bound`/`_upper_bound` and `spawn_throw_velocity` (bipd +588, +604, +606, +608; `../Halo3DS/source/units/unit_definitions.h:152-155`). Only `characters\floodcarrier\floodcarrier` has one in the ten caches: `flood_infection`, 3 to 6, 3.0 world units a second. Halo releases `random_range(lower, upper + 1)` of the variant when the unit is destroyed (`units/units.c:1666-1693`, from `unit_destroy`, :2712-2716; `random_range` draws `lower` to `upper` inclusive, `math/random_math.c:203-209`) and throws each at `velocity / 30` a tick (`units.c:1688`): along its heading at 0.5 to 1 times that and upwards at 0.8 to 1.5 times (`ai/actors.c:2944-2958`), halved for a living biped (`units/bipeds.c:1639-1644`). The engine (`halokilled` and `spawnRing`/`throwSpawned` in `source/src/halo.cpp`) spawns that many of that variant in the ring it already used (placement still needs floor), on the carrier's team, and gives each its horizontal throw as velocity outwards along its ring angle (in the engine's units of a bot's 16 cubes a second at the section's scale: 0.75 to 1.5 world units a second for the retail carrier) and its ordinary jump for the upward part (the engine's jump is fixed by the movement constants; the retail throw's upward 0.04 to 0.075 units a tick is 0.6 to 1.1 times the cyborg's 0.07 jump velocity, so a jump is the nearest the engine has; the infection forms' leap uses the same). Before, a carrier burst into 4 to 8 forms of the kind's default variant, unthrown; that stays the fallback without the line. The burst still comes at once on the carrier's death (Halo releases the forms from `unit_destroy`, when the dead unit's object is destroyed, which can come a little after the death; the engine has no such step: judgement call, unchanged).
- **`pop <inner> <outer> <lower> <upper min> <upper max> <jpt flags>`**, the jpt! of the body destroyed effect of the unit's collision model (coll +188, its effe's first jpt!) when it carries the infection-form pop flag (bit 12, `objects/damage.c:207-209`): `characters\flood_infection\body destroyed` (0.5 to 0.75 world units, damage 1, flags 4096) on `flood_infection`, and `body destroyed nopop` (flags 5120, also does-not-hurt-infection-forms) on c10's `flood_infection nopop`. Until this batch the engine had no infection pop at all. Halo's rule (`damage.c:2343-2375`): such a blast only hurts units whose tag is inconsequential (unit flags bit 19: the two infection bipeds) other than its owner; when infection_forms is above 0 (Heroic 0.5, Legendary 1) or the jpt! has does-not-hurt-infection-forms, it hurts one object at most, and with infection_forms above 0 each form resists with chance infection_forms * 0.25; the scale falls from 1 at the inner radius to 0 at the outer (:2389-2402). The engine (`popInfectionForms`) queues a pop when such a form dies (not when it is spent on a body: Halo deletes that one) and sets it off the next frame at the body's middle: the forms inside the cutoff, nearest first (Halo's order is its object iteration: judgement call), each with a clear line from the centre (the collision test at :2309-2323), take the damage, lower to upper by the scale; against the variant's 0.01 body vitality the retail pop is lethal anywhere inside the cutoff, so they die, and their pops go off a frame later. So on Easy and Normal shooting one form in a cluster pops every form within 0.75 units (about 2 to 3 cubes), a ripple through a tight swarm; on Heroic and Legendary each pop takes one neighbour at most and a form resists 12.5% or 25% of the time, a resist spending the pop (Halo marks the blast as having damaged its one object for a form it hurt or refused, `damage.c:2447-2450`; a form behind a wall is skipped without spending it); c10's nopop forms pop one neighbour at most on every level. Nothing but infection forms is hurt (the player takes nothing from a pop, as in Halo). At most 64 pops wait at once. `ai_kill` kills, so its forms pop; `ai_erase` and `ai_erase_all` delete infection forms with no pop since the carrier-squad change (below), and `ai_erase_all` clears the queue.
- **`unit <unit flags> <biped flags>`**, the unit tag's flags (unit +380) and a biped's (bipd +756; the biped section starts at +752, `units/biped_definitions.h:53-87`). The engine reads the inconsequential bit (above) and the random speed increase bit (bit 11), which only `characters\flood_infection\flood_infection nopop` has (c10). Its movement in combat is staggered (below).

**infection_forms** (-0.4/0/0.5/1 Easy to Legendary, matg's value 8, already in actors.txt's `difficulty` lines) drives the pop toughness above and a speed stagger: a biped with the random speed increase flag, moving at the alert aiming speed (the infection swarm sets alert only while it closes on or backs off a combat target, `ai/actor_type_infection.c:470-474`, casual otherwise, :334), moves at `infection_forms * (biped index % 137) * 0.00729927 + 1` of its speed (`units/bipeds.c:3580-3590`): up to about twice as fast on Legendary, down to 0.6 on Easy. The engine (`halomovementscale` in `source/src/halo_ai.cpp`, applied in `physics.cpp` to bots only, never the player) uses the bot's client number for Halo's object datum index, applies it while the actor fights a target, and scales the bot's whole horizontal step (so a leap in flight too, where Halo scales only the movement the biped asks for: judgement call; vertical motion is untouched). It maps without touching the player's movement: the bots' speed cap (16) is applied before it, so a staggered form can outrun that cap as Halo's does. Without the table `halodifficultyget` answers 0 for infection_forms (no stagger, no toughness: Normal's), not Halo's 1.0 for a missing table, so an older actors.txt leaves the Flood as it was. `halo_damagetrace 1` logs `FLOOD_SPAWN <unit> variant= lower= upper= count= velocity=`, one `FLOOD_THROW <name> speed= vel=` per thrown form and `FLOOD_POP variant= candidates= hit= resisted= one= toughness=` per pop; `qa_flood_stagger <label>` prints `FLOOD_STAGGER <label> <name> cn= flag= combat= scale= value=` per living actor.

## Factions and special enemies

**Not verified in the game yet**: everything in this section was written and compiled while testing was on hold. `tests/test_factions.py` checks it and has not been run.

Re-run the tables and the models after pulling this (all git-ignored under `packages/`):

```bash
python3 tools/halo_weapons.py "$maps"          # weapons.txt and equipment.txt gain `materials` lines
python3 tools/halo_actors.py "$maps"           # actors.txt format 2
python3 tools/import_halo_campaign_actors.py "$maps" .   # adds halo/monitor, halo/keyes, halo/engineer
```

The game still loads a format 1 `actors.txt` (variants then take their family's side and have no materials) and weapon tables without `materials` lines (every modifier 1; Hunter armour falls back to the values below).

**Teams and allegiances.** The engine has six teams (`halo_ai.h`): Covenant, human, Flood, Sentinel, player and "other" (the retail teams 6 to 9). An actor's team is its encounter's team, or on the default team its unit's default team from the biped tag (`actors.txt` `team`, read at unit +384: marines and crew human, Covenant and Engineers covenant, the Flood flood, Sentinels and the Monitor sentinel). Every team is hostile to every other except its own and the allegiances set; the player and the humans start allied (the missions also say so). `ai_allegiance` and `ai_allegiance_remove` set and clear an allegiance between two of the script's team literals, and the actors' targeting, hearing and shot hits follow the matrix at once, so Covenant, Flood and Sentinels now fight each other (c40, d20, d40) and c10 and c20's Sentinels fight beside the player. Allegiances reset when a mission starts, a checkpoint keeps them and a revert restores them, and saved games write them in the state block (`s allegiance <allied> <broken>`, optional on reading). Who counts as an ally (the motion tracker's yellow, enemies left in a wave, aim assist, `game_safe_to_save`, and the QA hooks that pick or clear hostiles: the route walker in `retail_qa.inc`, which also spares the passive Monitor and Engineers, `combat_qa.inc` and `damage_qa.inc`) asks the matrix. An actor whose target's team becomes an ally drops that target at once. A saved checkpoint writes the checkpoint's allegiances (not later betrayals); a mission or a section started afresh resets them even when it has no script. Infection Forms from a Carrier, and combat forms rising from bodies, are on the Flood team (they were Covenant); since the Flood tag batch a carrier's forms take the team the carrier fought on (the Flood in every retail placement), and the Flood team only without actors.txt's `spawn` line (unverified).

**Betrayal.** When the player kills two members of a team allied with the player (the count is the engine's choice; Halo turns allies on a player who keeps killing them), the allegiance breaks: that team turns hostile, the console says `Halo: the human turn on the player`, and `ai_allegiance_broken` answers true for the pair (a50's `traitor_bitch`) until `ai_allegiance` renews it. Kills the scripts deal in the player's name (`ai_kill`, `ai_erase`, `ai_erase_all`, `damage_object`) do not count.

**Material damage.** `tools/halo_weapons.py` writes after every melee and projectile line of `weapons.txt` and after the grenades' blast and attached lines of `equipment.txt` a `materials` line: the `jpt!` tag's 33 damage modifiers, one per retail material type (`jpt!` +512). `tools/halo_actors.py` writes each variant's collision materials (`coll` +564, 72-byte entries: type, head flag, body and shield damage multipliers). For Hunters, Sentinels, the Monitor and Engineers `halodamage` multiplies the hit by the material's body multiplier times the weapon's modifier for that material; other bodies take damage as before, unless `halo_actormaterials 1` (batch 9, off by default: it changes every time to kill and is there for a later balance pass) applies the model to them too: the hit lands on the variant's head-flagged material at head height (the top fifth of the body, from the hit's point where the hit carries one: shots and the player's melee), else on its first other material, and takes that material's body multiplier times the weapon's modifier for its type (an Elite's flesh 1 for most weapons; the sniper rifle 0.05 and the assault rifle 0.65 on Flood combat forms; the plasma weapons 2 on Sentinels); an Elite's energy shield takes the weapon's modifier for the elite energy shield (30: the assault rifle's 0.7, which the engine applied to every shield before, the pistol's 0.8, the sniper rifle's and the plasma rifle's 2), and a shield a weapon cannot hurt (modifier 0) stops the hit whole; the body material's multiplier scales only what passes an Elite's shield (the shield takes the hit at its own modifier). **Shield leak** (the shield-leak work, not verified in the game; `tests/test_melee_materials.py` items 8 and 10, `tests/test_actor_tags.py`, not run). Every actor without a special body has its shield step from its coll: the `material` lines' fifth field is the material's shield leak (`coll` material +40) and the `resistance` line holds the indirect damage material (+4), the coll's body and shield vitality (+8, +204), the shield material type (+210) and the failure curve (+236, +240, +244). Halo's shield takes (1 − leak) of the hit (`../Halo3DS objects/damage.c:842-843`), less once the shield is at or below the failure threshold (844-857: (1 − maximum) × f(shield/threshold) + maximum, f linear here: every retail unit with a threshold uses function 0), at the material's shield multiplier (890-891); the rest goes to the body. The engine picks the material without coll regions (`hitMaterial`): a blast, a hit with no attacker or the actor's own lands on the indirect material (damage.c:1614-1635); a direct hit from within 60 degrees of where the actor faces, below the top fifth, on a hand-held shield material (not head-flagged, body multiplier 0, shield multiplier above 0, leak under 1) while the shield holds; else the head-flagged material in the top fifth, else the first other one. So the Jackal's 200 to 250 shield covers only its hand-held shield (type 16, leak 0, body multiplier 0, so nothing reaches the body, the excess of the breaking hit included), at the weapon's modifier for the jackal energy shield even with `halo_actormaterials 0` (0 for the assault rifle, pistol, shotgun and sniper rifle, 1 for the plasma weapons); its body, head and every blast take the hit whole and leave the shield as it is (its other materials leak 1). Judgement calls: the Hunter's 60 degrees for the shield's arc, and a depleted shield no longer covering anything (Halo swaps the shield regions' permutation when it empties, `object_deplete_shield`, damage.c:636-658, and back when it recharges, 1945). The marines leak 0.2 with threshold 1 and maximum 0.2: 20% of a hit reaches the body at full shield, 84% near empty. Elites, Sentinels and the player leak 0 and are unchanged; the Hunter, Sentinel, Monitor and Engineer keep their special bodies. EMP still drains the shield wherever it lands. An actv that sets a body vitality and no shield now has no shield (Halo's `actor_customize_unit` sets both, `ai/actors.c:2397-2404`): the crewmen, both captains, the wounded and suicidal marines and the stealth Elites, which had the body's 24 or 100 before. Without these fields (an older actors.txt) every shield covers the whole body, as before. Hits carry their `jpt!` (projectiles, blasts, grenades and the player's melee); a hit without one uses modifier 1 (0.5 on Hunter armour). `halo_damagetrace 1` logs `DAMAGE_MATERIAL` for these hits.

**Hunters.** From the tags: the shield arm (material hunter shield, body multiplier 0; every campaign weapon's modifier for it is 0 except the energy sword's) takes a direct hit from within 60 degrees of where the Hunter faces, so frontal fire does nothing; a hit from behind (beyond 130 degrees) lands on the exposed skin (hunter skin, multiplier 1, flagged as head: the pistol 1.0, assault rifle 0.5, shotgun 1.0); anything else, and every blast whatever the facing, lands on the armour plates (hunter armor: pistol and shotgun 0.2, assault rifle and plasma 0.5, frag and plasma grenades 0.25, rocket 1.0). The engine has no hit locations, so the region comes from the attacker's bearing and (batch 9) the height the hit struck: with `halo_hunterregions` (on by default) a hit in the top fifth of the body from outside the shield arm's 60 degrees lands on the skin (the coll's head-flagged material is hunter skin: the head bonus), and a frontal hit between 30% and 60% of its height while the Hunter fires its cannon (the 0.6 s after a shot, when the shield arm swings aside) lands on the skin of its belly. Since batch 5's critic's gap 11 (not verified in the game; `tests/test_melee_materials.py` item 7, not run) that window opens from the Hunter's own cannon fire (`haloactorfired`, recorded by `halofiretrigger` for every actor's shot), not from its last action, which the smash sets too: a smash no longer opens the belly. Without `weapons.txt` the cannon is not a tag weapon and the window never opens. The heights, the firing window and the body as a cylinder are engine judgements, not the retail coll regions; blasts still land on the armour. Hunters pair off with the nearest unpartnered Hunter of their team (bond brothers) and rejoin a partner more than 10 units away; when one dies the other goes berserk for good and charges whoever killed it. Inside 1.6 world units a Hunter closes in and smashes: the unit's melee `jpt!` (35 to 45; `actors.txt` `unitmelee`) every 1.6 to 2.2 s plus its actr's melee delay (0.1 s) with a shove, through the usual difficulty scaling (Difficulty, melee_delay_scale). Further out it fires its fuel rod cannon from the tags (`hunter fuel rod`, its 75 to 40 blast), as before.

**Stealth Elites.** Variants with the `actv` active camouflage flag (the stealth Elites in a50, b30, b40 and c40, and d40's `floodcombat elite stealth unarmed`) are cloaked: actors see them only within about a fifth of their vision range (an eighth under super camouflage), as with the player's camouflage, and aim at them with extra error. They are drawn translucent through AssaultCube's translucent model path with a per-body opacity (`halomodelalpha`): a faint shimmer (about 0.05 to 0.13) that shows more of the body while the cloak flickers weaker, which it does for a second after the Elite fires or is hit (its cloak drops to about a third and recovers), the same fading the player's camouflage uses. Super camouflage (the stealth majors) hides a little more. A dead stealth Elite is visible. Spec-ops Elites have no camouflage flag in the Xbox tags and stay visible. Variants that cannot use ranged weapons (the energy sword Elites) now fight in melee with the sword's `jpt!` (151) times their damage modifier, one 1.2 s swing and then their actr's melee delay on the level (Difficulty, melee_delay_scale).

**Flyers.** Actors whose `actr` has the flying flag (bit 21: Sentinels, the Monitor, Engineers) have no gravity, no jumping or landing and no ground snap (`halohovering` in `physics.cpp`; the player's movement is untouched); the brain sets their climb rate. They hold a height over the floor (a few times a second it traces the floor and ceiling under them: the retail BSP on retail collision, the cubes on stock geometry; over a drop with no floor in reach they hold the height they are at), fly straight to points instead of following the walking waypoints, and in combat hold 1.5 to 3.5 units over their target, keep their combat range and strafe across the target's line of fire, changing direction and height every 1 to 2.3 s. Killed, they fall. Sentinels fire their beam from the tags as before (a hitscan 1-point beam at 30 shots a second); the shielded variants have their `actv` shield (60). The brain's charge rule now reads the right `actr` flags (always charge; always charge or berserk while attacking): Sentinels and the Monitor no longer charge into melee, the Flood charges while attacking (all their squads start so), Infection and Carrier Forms always. **Enforcers** are a Halo 2 enemy: no Xbox Halo CE campaign cache has their tags, so there is nothing to place.

**343 Guilty Spark, Engineers, Keyes and Cortana.** The Monitor (`monitor` and `monitor_no_light`, kind 10) and the Engineer (kind 11) are new bodies: they fly, never fire or throw, and nobody targets the Monitor; its collision material has body multiplier 0, so it cannot be hurt (c20's scripts also set `object_cannot_take_damage`). c10, c20 and d40 have Monitor squads (c20 places them with `ai_place` and leads them with command lists), which it now follows in the air (`bsp0_monitor` to `bsp3_monitor` in c20). Engineers float in place and drift away from a threat; no Xbox campaign encounter places one (b30's cache carries the tags), so they only appear if a script spawns them. `tools/import_halo_campaign_actors.py` bakes `halo/monitor` (c20, its one idle clip), `halo/keyes` (a50's captain biped and graph) and `halo/engineer` (b30; batch 9: its graph has no node table of its own, so its tracks are read against the model's 25 nodes in order, as the device graphs are, and it is baked with its idle, move and alert clips instead of its bind pose); without them the Monitor draws as a Sentinel and the Engineer as a Grunt. The scripts cannot kill the Monitor (batch 9): `ai_kill` and `ai_kill_silent` of a squad pass over him, and `ai_erase` (c20 erases `bsp0_monitor` once the player moves on) and `ai_erase_all` remove him at once with no death, body or sound, as Halo's erase deletes the unit; other actors still die where they stand, as before. The captain and captain_ingame variants (a50's Keyes squads) draw as Keyes and fight as marines.

**Attached actors and story figures (batch 8, not verified in the game; `tests/test_ai_attach.py`).** `ai_attach <object> <squad>` in play now turns a scripted biped into an actor (`Scenario::attachActors` in `source/src/halo_script.cpp`): once nothing poses or carries it (no custom animation, recording or parent seat) and two ticks have passed since the attach, an actor of the squad's default variant (the `.hscb`'s version 9 squad defaults: a50's Keyes squads default to `captain_ingame`, its marine squads to the marine variants) spawns at the object's pose on the encounter's team, as a member of that squad (`ai_living_count`, `ai_actors`, migrations and orders reach it), and the scripted model is no longer drawn. An erased actor's live grenades go with it (`removebounceents`), since nothing may explode with a deleted owner. The object then stands for the actor: `unit_get_health` is the actor's health (-1 once it is dead or gone: a50's `game_lost_script` waits for Keyes's to reach 0), the unit commands, volume tests and `object_teleport` act on it, `ai_erase` and `ai_kill` kill it with its squad, and `object_destroy` (or `object_create_anew`) erases it at once with no death or drops (`haloerasebot`). A checkpoint keeps the living actor where it stands (a revert brings it back there, Continue too: the state block's optional `s attached` record) and one dead at the checkpoint stays dead after a revert. Where this applies: a50's `captain_keyes` and `free_marine_1` to `_3` after `ini_post_rescue`, its jailed marines once `unit_stop_custom_animation` frees them from their cell poses, `keyes_prison` never (he sits until the rescue destroys him), c10's `marine_suicidal`. A fix found on the way: `object_create` of an object that does not exist yet now makes it at its scenario placement, as Halo does; in a section started without an opening scene it was left at the origin (the object's pose was only set by a scene's start or by `object_create_anew`). Attaching inside a scene only records the squad, as before: a50's insertion marines and its final Hunters are attached in `cutscene_insertion` and `cutscene_extraction`, so they stay figures of those scenes. Limits: a custom animation started on an object whose actor exists does not pose the actor (none of the retail scripts does this in play). Since gap 4 of batch 5's critic (not verified in the game; `tests/test_ai_attach.py` items 6 to 9): `unit_set_current_vitality` of an object attached but without its actor yet is queued and applied when the actor spawns (c10's `marine_suicidal`, given its maximum and current vitality right after its attach, applied in that order; since the script-vitality change in Halo's points), and so are `object_cannot_take_damage` and `unit_suspended` set on the object meanwhile; the queue is kept with the checkpoint, not in the progress file. A vitality given to an actor carried in from the section before that has not spawned yet replaces the health it carries (it spawns with it, and `unit_get_health` answers it meanwhile); `object_cannot_take_damage` and `unit_suspended` given to it before it spawns are not applied (judgement call: the object's own flags may be stale from before its attach); `ai_migrate` is, since the fresh-start batch (below). A Call or Exit scene's start, which hides every scripted object and puts it back at its placement, shows each attached object whose actor is alive where the actor stands (its position and facing), so the scene does not draw it at the stale attach pose; a Call scene's end gives the objects back as before. **Carried into the next section:** the attached actors alive when the player walks on into the mission's next section (a50's `captain_keyes` and the freed marines, from the cell block into the hangar; every live tick keeps a plain copy of them, since the old section's actors are gone when the next script starts) spawn behind the player on that section's first live ticks, one to three Halo units back and spread to the sides, on retail floor the player can see at chest height (the spawn helper, `haloscenariospawnenemy`; since the final integration review a spot behind a wall, a closed door or a ledge's lip from the player is passed over; a spot with no floor, or none in sight, is tried again a second later), with the health and shield they had and their squad, script flags and suspension, following the player, and stand for their objects again (`unit_get_health`, so a50's `game_lost_script` watches the real Keyes; `ai_living_count`; `mig_into_hangar`'s `ai_migrate_by_unit`). Until one spawns, `unit_get_health` of its object answers its carried health, and an `ai_erase` or `ai_kill` of its squad kills it (-1). Since the fresh-start batch (gap 1 of batch 6's critic; not verified in the game, `tests/test_ai_attach.py` item 12, not run) a pending one also takes part in migrations and counts: `ai_migrate` and `ai_migrate_and_speak` of its squad or encounter, and `ai_migrate_by_unit` of its object (a50's `mig_into_hangar` and `mig_into_control` name `captain_keyes` and the freed marines), move its entry to the new squad as a living actor's would (`Scenario::migrateCarried`; migrated to a whole encounter it keeps its squad index, or takes the encounter's first squad where that index has none), so it spawns there and a later `ai_erase` or `ai_kill` of its old squad no longer takes it; `ai_living_count` counts it as a living member of its squad, and `ai_strength` and `ai_living_fraction` count it with the health it carries (`list_count` of an `ai_actors` list still leaves it out, since `list_get` cannot return it). With `halo_scripttrace 1` a migration logs `captain_keyes, carried in and not spawned yet, migrates to hangar_captain_halls/squad_switch`. While no spot will do, the log says why it waits, once each time the reason changes: `HaloScript: captain_keyes waits to come along into section 5: a wall, a closed door or a ledge between the player and every spot (5 of 5 spots blocked from sight); trying again every second`, or no floor at any spot, or some of each (the critic's worry 5 of batch 6). `halo_carriedhold 1`, a QA hold, keeps them from spawning; `qa_attach_state` lists a pending one as `ATTACH_QA <label> carried=<object> squad=<encounter>/<squad> health=<h> waiting=<0 to 3>`. The carried-in section's checkpoint, usually taken before they spawn, keeps the ones still to come, and a revert spawns them behind the player again; the progress file writes them as `s attached` records flagged to do the same (Title menu and saved games). Judgement call: Halo's actors walk through the doorway with the player, the engine's next section is another map. A checkpoint now also keeps an attached actor's health and shield, so a revert or a Continue brings Keyes back as hurt as he was (since the stood-members change every living squad member does too, where it stood: Title menu and saved games, Squad members where they stood). **Its own start (batch 5's critic, gap 11; not verified in the game, `tests/test_ai_attach.py` item 10, not run):** a checkpoint used to keep where an attached actor stood by rewriting its start, so `ai_teleport_to_starting_location` after a checkpoint sent it to where it stood then. Now the checkpoint keeps where it stood separately (`SquadBot::stood`, used by the revert's respawn and written as the `s attached` record's point) and its start stays where it took over (its attach pose, or for one carried in from the section before where it spawned behind the player), which `ai_teleport_to_starting_location` goes back to; the record keeps the start in four new optional fields (Title menu and saved games). The kill from behind spares attached actors (Damage fidelity, Melee).

Story figures, the scripted figures drawn outside scenes, are now flagged by the importer instead of picked by name (`story_figures` in `tools/import_halo_scripts.py`, `OBJECT_STORY_FIGURE` in the `.hscb`'s version 9 object flags): a biped not placed dead, or a character posed as scenery (a tag under `characters\`), that a gameplay script creates, or that a Call scene creates and leaves alive. Gameplay scripts are the startup and continuous scripts that are not scene cues and what they reach through static calls and `wake` without entering a cue script; the cue table is read from `source/src/halo_script.cpp` the way `tests/test_campaign_tables.py` reads it. The import prints each mission's list: a10 `keyesa10`, a30 `gunner`, a50 `captain_keyes`, `keyes_prison`, the freed, jailed and ship marines, c10 `marine_suicidal`, d20 `keyes_flood` (the Flood-infested Keyes, scenery, which the name rule drew too), d40 `cortana` (left on the bridge by `cinematic_bridge` until `chief_duck` destroys her) and `nipple_grunt`. The rule draws a flagged figure while no squad owns it, or while a custom animation poses it and no actor has taken it over (`keyes_prison` sitting in his cell, the jailed marines). Corpses placed dead (a30's `dead_marine_*`, b40's dump units, c10's bodies, d20's Grunts) and figures only scenes use are not drawn in play, and no scripted `monitor` object is drawn beside c20's Monitor actor. With an older `.hscb` the engine falls back to the old name prefixes (`keyes`, `captain_keyes`, `cortana`, `monitor`, not chips, effects or teleport effects). `ai_conversation_advance` was already bound; `recording_play` drives scripted objects, not actors, so the Monitor follows its command lists only; `unit_set_emotion` is still recorded without effect.

## Retail mission scripting

The importer reads the original scenario syntax datum array, strings, script and global tables, oriented trigger volumes, camera points, flags, chapter strings, AI squads and conversations. It keeps the compiled nodes and their validated handles. The script import includes 1,673 device placements, 119 BSP switch records, and 349 recorded input streams across the ten missions. Some Xbox voice tags carry no subtitle text.

The client executes script expressions at 30 ticks per second. It handles script calls, startup and dormant threads, conditionals, timing, globals, original volume tests, supported hostile placements, conversations and checkpoint requests. It keeps thread and global state during the ordinary section-to-section handoff. Cinematic entry and exit cues connect the retail script functions to the current route. Those scenes use the original camera data, scripted audio starts and waits, and retail BSP and object assets. Camera transitions use the observer's quintic interpolation for position, field of view and axis-angle rotation, carrying velocity and acceleration across waypoints. Compressed skeletal clips are sampled at the retail cadence. The in-game console command `halo_scripttrace 1` logs camera cuts and audio starts. `halo_script_status` lists the unbound commands reached in the current mission. Save and pacing commands are bound: `game_safe_to_save` is false while a Covenant actor is in combat, `game_save_cancel` drops a pending checkpoint, and `game_revert` kills the player. One HUD countdown (`hud_set_timer_time`, `show_hud_timer`, `pause_hud_timer`, `hud_get_timer_ticks`) follows the engine's escape deadline on the Maw run, so d40's timer script no longer wakes `endgame_cinematics` early. Tutorial prompts (`player_action_test_*`) finish on the matching input or after five seconds, and presentation-only calls (rumble, rasterizer, screen effects, `display_scenario_help`, `enable_hud_help_flash`) are no-ops. Scenes the scripts call in the middle of a section, the scripts' moves of the player and the remaining unbound commands are under Script scenes and player moves. Nav points and the HUD show and blink commands are drawn (see Nav points and HUD script commands). `tests/test_script_coverage.py` fails if a command used in a `sleep_until` or `if` condition has no handler.

**Camera point orientation (static comparison, the batch after the c10 section 5 batch).** After the final integration review found Halo's placement rotation (`euler_rows`, Scenery collision), the cinematic camera was compared with Halo's own: `scripted_camera_set` in the Halo CE source port in `../Halo3DS` (`camera/camera_scripting.c`) turns a camera point's orientation into forward and up by `vectors3d_from_euler_angles3d`, which is `matrix4x3_rotation_from_angles` (forward and up are rows 0 and 2 of Rx(roll) Ry(-pitch) Rz(yaw)). `source/src/halo_script.cpp`'s `poseOffset` (used by `cameraView` for camera points, and for objects and markers) rotates about z by yaw, then about the world's y by pitch (`vec::rotate_around_y`, which is Ry(-pitch)), then about the world's x by roll: the same composition. A scratch comparison of 20,000 random (yaw, pitch, roll) found `poseOffset`'s forward and up equal to Halo's to the last bit, and the view `halocinematiccamera` builds from them (`renderAngles`, AssaultCube yaw `90 - yaw`, pitch, roll, through `transplayer`'s `glRotatef` order and its y reflection) looking along Halo's forward with Halo's up, mirrored into the AssaultCube frame, within 1e-15. So there is no mismatch to fix for combined yaw, pitch and roll; nothing changed. As in Halo, a camera point relative to an object (`camera_set_relative`) follows the object's position, not its turn. Not seen in the game.

| System | Implemented | Remaining parity work |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Mission scripting | Retail programs, door and lift position tracks, shared device groups, player controls, adjacent BSP transitions, checkpoints and supported squad operations | Multi-level traversal, alternate BSP routes, complete command bindings, mission branches and co-op |
| Cutscenes | Retail cue scripts, cameras, fades, titles, scene geometry, skeletal clips, recorded movement, seat and attachment markers | Original movement simulation, per-frame marker transforms, lighting, additional effects and full cue coverage |
| Dialogue and sound | Original impulse and conversation assets, tag-defined script timing and selected music and Foley tracks | Speaker and permutation selection, positional mixing and class gain transitions |

### Damage targets

**Not verified in the game yet** (written while testing was on hold; `tests/test_damage_targets.py` checks it). A damage target is a scripted unit with no AI that the player hurts by shooting it: d40's eight manifold detectors (`levels\d40\devices\invisible_collision_object\invisible damage detector`, flat invisible bipeds whose health `enc5_1_destroyed_poll` reads). `tools/halo_cast.py` (`damage_target`) picks the invisible bipeds (the tag path says "invisible"; no other retail scripted biped qualifies) and `tools/import_halo_scripts.py` writes HSCB version 8, whose object rows end with the target's body vitality (125 for the detectors, from the collision model), its collision box in its own frame (1.1 by 1.6 by 0.24 units) and whether its placement exists at the start; every other object has zero vitality and behaves as before (a10's and a50's Keyes stay unhurt). The engine reads versions 5 to 9 (version 9 appends the starting profiles, the squads' default variants and the object flags: Retail weapons, Factions and special enemies). Placed targets are visible from the start and never drawn. The player's instant shots stop on the first target box they pass through, unless a body is nearer, and deal their usual damage (range falloff included); the player's projectiles do the same; the player's rockets, fuel rods and grenades hurt every target within their outer radius that their blast centre can see (structure and machines), falling from full at the inner radius to the `jpt!` lower bound at the outer. This is how d40 section 5's route destroys the s3 manifold since batch 5: frags thrown into its channel's mouth (`ThrowTarget`, route flag 2048; `tests/test_frag_throw.py` throws the planned aims in the game). Only the player's damage counts (enemy fire near the manifolds never destroys one), `object_cannot_take_damage` stops it (d40 makes the s1, n1, n3 and s3 detectors immune while their piston is closed), and `unit_get_health` answers the share of the vitality left (0 once destroyed, -1 for a target not placed). Since the difficulty-table work (unverified) the vitality is scaled by enemy_vitality, the detectors being on the Covenant team (Difficulty): 75, 125, 150 and 175 points destroy one on Easy to Legendary. The damage is kept in the checkpoint like the other object state, but not in the progress file's state block (a Continue starts the targets whole; the scripts' `manifold_*_destroyed` globals, which are saved, keep a destroyed manifold destroyed). With `halo_scripttrace 1` each hit logs `Halo target: <name> took N, health H`. Regenerate the scripts with `python3 tools/import_halo_scripts.py "$maps"` (all ten change only in their object rows); the new files have a new hash, so a game saved before continues at its checkpoint with the mission script restarted, as after any script change (Title menu and saved games).

## Script scenes and player moves

**Not verified in the game yet**: written and compiled while testing was on hold. `tests/test_mission_exits.py` (new) and items 12 and 13 of `tests/test_frontend_progress.py`, item 3 of `tests/test_script_coverage.py` and the title check of `tests/test_campaign_ending.py` are written but have not been run.

**Scenes the scripts call.** The cue table (`cues` in `source/src/halo_script.cpp`) has a fifth trigger, Call: when a gameplay thread calls a scene script that has a Call row for the section, the scene plays there and then, on the scene stage like any other cue, and the calling thread waits for it and carries on after the call (as in Halo, where `(if (cinematic_skip_start) (cutscene_rescue)) (cinematic_skip_stop) (ini_post_rescue)` runs the scene, then what follows it). The scripted objects are kept aside while it plays and put back after, except those the scene created or destroyed (d20's captain scene destroys the implants). A Call scene plays only when the script calls it, as in Halo: if the script is held up short of the call, the scene does not play. Call rows: a50's `cutscene_rescue` (section 4; it used to be that section's Exit scene, played when the player left the prison), b30's `cutscene_ledge` and `cutscene_shaftb_goal` (any section), d20's `cinematic_drop` (`cutscene_fall`, any section) and `cinematic_captain` (section 5; it used to be its Exit scene), and d40's `cinematic_bridge`, which now plays on the Autumn's bridge (section 2, BSP 1, called by `enc2_5`) instead of at the start of the engine room (section 5), where it used to be an Entry cue. The sections' engine interactions (a50's "Free Captain Keyes", d20's "Recover the Captain's implants" at their exits) still end those sections, now without a scene. A Call scene is not kept in the saved state: a save is never taken while one plays. Skipping a Call scene (Enter, or the QA walker) runs the rest of its script at once before it ends, with its waits passed over and `game_reverted` true (so a `cinematic_skip_start` part is passed over, as Halo's skip does by reverting), so what it does for the play after it still happens: b30's `cutscene_shaftb_goal` closes `shafta_lock_door` first and opens it later in the scene, and a skip that only ended the scene would leave it closed (log, if a loop holds it past 1024 passes: `Halo scene: <scene> did not run to its end when skipped`). A revert while a Call scene plays (not expected; the actors are held) ends it without handing the objects back or moving the player. Scripts named in the table are still never run inline by a gameplay thread; an Entry, Exit or Timeout scene still plays at the route's boundary and its call is passed over. Judgement calls: b40's `cutscene_insertion_b` is not cued, because it is not a camera scene (`camera_control false`, the first-person Pelican ride after `cutscene_insertion_a`), and b40's `cinematic_crev_ent_*` and `cinematic_ext_a_*` take no camera (dropship flybys); `tests/test_mission_exits.py` lists these and the other uncued camera scripts (a10's cryo exits `fast_setup` and `tutorial_action`, a50's `game_lost_script`, c10's `mission_control`, the uncalled placeholders) with the reason each plays as gameplay, and fails on any new one.

**Music and conversations across a Call scene (batch 8, not verified in the game).** A Call scene used to cut the gameplay music and conversations for good: every scene stops the script's sounds when it starts and ends. Now the looping sounds playing when a Call scene starts (`sound_looping_start` from gameplay, with their gain and alternate track) are started again when it ends, unless the scene stopped them (`sound_looping_stop`) or switched their alternate track (kept as the scene left it); and a conversation a scene cuts (any scene) counts as finished (`ai_conversation_status` 5), so a script waiting for it moves on instead of waiting at 2 for good. A loop restarts from its start (or its loop track), not where it was cut. A revert during a Call scene stops them again, as any revert stops the script's sounds. `tests/test_mission_exits.py` item 5 checks d40's bridge scene and a50's rescue (`qa_audio_state`, `qa_conversation_state`).

**a30's extraction.** The Pelican comes for the player at the last of the three marine sites they clear: its three exit scenes are variants of section 3's exit (rows with a vehicle, a volume and a site flag). The exit plays the variant whose Pelican the player boarded (`foehammer_cliff`, `_rubble`, `_river`), else the one whose site volume holds the player (`cliff_all`, `rubble_all`, `river_all`), else the one whose `*_hide_flag` is nearest the player (log: `Halo scene: <scene> chosen for the exit (<why>)`). The section's route ends at the river landing zone, so on foot that is the river scene, as before; the cliff and rubble sites lie outside section 3's map (the scripts' cliff and rubble extractions, which the player boards with E, now pick their own scene).

**The player moved by the scripts.** `object_teleport` and `object_set_facing` of `(player0)` (or the player list), and `volume_teleport_players_not_inside` (a player outside the volume, or a volume the scenario does not have such as `null`, goes to the flag), move the player in gameplay through `Scenario::placePlayer` and `haloplayerplace` (`halo.cpp`): to the flag's place on this section's map, facing its way, when the body fits there (retail collision: standing room on the mesh, as the recovery's safe spots; stock geometry: clear of the cubes and inside the map, on the floor of its cube column no more than 4 units below the flag), then through `haloplayermoved` like any other move that is not walking (log: `Halo script: player placed at <flag> (<command>)`). A flag that is not on a floor of the section's map (flags of another BSP fall outside it) leaves the player where they are (`... has no floor in this section's map (or the player is seated or dead); the player stays where they are`); so does a seated, dead or ending player. Inside a scene the player is off stage: the scene's last `object_teleport` or `object_set_facing` of `player0` is read from its script when it starts (following its calls, so a skipped scene ends the same way) and updated as it runs, and when a Call or Cartographer scene ends the player is put there. Entry, Exit and Timeout scenes leave the player to the section's start or the next section as before. Examples: d20's captain scene leaves the player at `player0_capt_done`, d40's bridge scene at `player0_playon_base`, b30's Cartographer at `player0_map_done` (the handoff that was special-cased before), a50's `ini_post_rescue` after the rescue at `prison_player0_teleport` (each 1.6 to 2 units from where the routes put their next leg), a10's `cinematic_x20` at `chief_basea10` and `mission_a10` turns the player to `facing_flag_1`. `object_teleport` of an actor (`(list_get (ai_actors unlock_elite) 0)`) moves its body.

**Scripted seats (batch 9, not verified in the game; `tests/test_script_seats.py`).** A gameplay script seating the player in a vehicle the engine does not drive (`unit_enter_vehicle (player0) cryotube_1 ct-driver` in a10's `tutorial_setup` on Normal and `fast_setup` on the other difficulties) holds the player there, as a Pelican boarded with E carries them (`Scenario::seatPlayer` in `halo_script_ai.inc`): the eye is at the seat's camera marker (the model's `camera <seat marker>`, the Pelican's `camera p-riderlf`, else its `camera`, the cryotube's, when it is within 1.5 world units of the seat marker; else the seat marker and the usual seated height), facing the way it looks, carried with the vehicle; nothing to walk, fire or pick up (`halodriving()`), E does nothing and the HUD offers no way out. `unit_exit_vehicle (player0)` lets them out: back where they stood when seated (a10: the start in front of the tube; `tutorial_action` then teleports them to `tutorial_exit_cryotube_flag`), or where the seat is when the vehicle carried them off. Guards: `halo_scriptseatmax` (180 s by default; 0 for none) lets the player out of a hold the script never ends (`Halo: the scripted seat lets the player out (held too long ...)`); since gap 10 of batch 5's critic, a seat the vehicle has carried off (a Pelican possibly in mid-air) puts them on the first floor under it that the body fits on (`haloplayerfloorbelow`: straight down the retail mesh at the seat or at the spawn helper's ring of nearby spots; the cube column's floor on stock geometry; log `the player is put on the floor under the seat`), else back where they were seated (`no floor under the seat`); a seat not carried off lets them out where they were seated, as before; anything else that moves the player more than 1.5 world units from the seat (a QA teleport) lets them go where they were put; death, a section start and the vehicle's destruction end it; `halo_scriptseat 0` ignores the command as before. A checkpoint keeps the seat and a revert puts the player back in it (the reverted script lets them out). Since gap 10 the progress file records it too: `s boarded` with a trailing scripted flag (and seat -1 allowed for a scripted seat in a vehicle with no seats in the table), so a Continue during a10's cryotube hold puts the player back in the tube, where the restored tutorial's `unit_exit_vehicle` lets them out, instead of on foot inside it with `unit_exit_vehicle` doing nothing (`tests/test_frontend_progress.py` item 15). A save from before has no flag and is read as before; an older engine refuses a save with the flag (malformed record) and restarts the script. The tutorial's prompts still time out on their own after 5 s each, so on Normal the tutorial lets the player out after its introduction, look and health lessons (expected about one to two minutes, from the script's sound lengths; not measured). The QA route walker waits while held, turning for the look prompts, and the time does not count against its deadline (`RETAIL_QA held in a scripted seat`). b40's insertion Pelican ride is reached since gap 3 (below, not verified in the game; `tests/test_script_seats.py` items 6 and 7): its `unit_enter_vehicle` is in `pelican_up`, which the Entry scene `cutscene_insertion_a` wakes on the scene stage; the seat is remembered there and taken when the scene ends, with `insertion_pelican` already on its recording as the scene left it (`Halo scene: cutscene_insertion_a leaves the player seated in insertion_pelican (p-riderlf)`), and the gameplay `cutscene_insertion_b` waits for the recording and lets the player out at the landing. A scene skipped before `pelican_up` wakes (about 10 s in, after the scene's first `sleep 300`) starts the player on the ground at the landing as before. a50's, b30's and c10's insertions seat the player and let them out inside their scenes, so nothing is carried (a later `unit_exit_vehicle` on the scene stage cancels the remembered seat).

**Scripts woken by scenes (gap 3 of batch 5's critic, not verified in the game).** A `wake` on the scene stage (from a scene's script or a thread it woke) starts the script there, as before, but a gameplay script (not one of the cue table's scenes) that is still running when the scene ends now goes on in play from where it is, as Halo's threads do after a cutscene (`Scenario::sceneWoken`; log `Halo scene: <script>, woken by <scene>, goes on in play`); one that finished in the scene, one the scene put to sleep for good, and one the gameplay threads already run stay as they are. A skipped Call scene still runs the rest of its script first (its wakes then start from their top in play). A skipped scene that the retail scripts make skippable (`(if (cinematic_skip_start) (<scene>))`: a10's x10, x20 and x30; a30's `cutscene_intro` and its three extractions; a50's energy lift, rescue and extraction; b30's map and extraction; the extractions of b40, c10 and c40; c10's x50; c40's insertion; the `cinematic_intro` scenes of c20, d20 and d40; d40's bridge scene) carries nothing on and takes no seat, since Halo's skip reverts the game to before the scene and the script after the call sets up what follows; the engine's skip of a scene Halo cannot skip (the insertions of a50, b30, b40 and c10, a30's bridge, b30's ledge and shaft B scenes, c20's extraction, d20's drop, captain, lift and outro scenes, d40's finale) keeps what the scene started (judgement call). `tests/test_mission_exits.py` lists the 69 gameplay scripts the 19 scenes wake (`SCENE_WAKES`, read from the maps; it fails on a change): mostly music and dialogue (b30's `music_b30` director, woken on the insertion's first tick and waiting for the mission's music cues, used never to run: b30 now has its music; a50's `dialog_insertion`, d40's `insertion_dialog`), crew, recordings and pyrotechnics, b40's `pelican_up` (the only one that seats the player), and c10's x50 helpers. c10 section 6's doors were checked: x50 (section 5's exit) wakes `door_open`, which opens the lab's `control_door_a`, `control_infection`, which places Flood the scene stage does not place, and `the_horror`; all finish inside the scene, and none moves a door of section 6 (BSP 4), whose route has no door rows and which is not in `KNOWN_BLOCKED`, so nothing changes there.

**Conditions.** The static scans (`tests/tools/hs_conditions.py`, which now also reads the values `set` stores, and `test_script_coverage.py`) found no command in a `sleep_until`, `if`, `cond`, `while` or `set` that falls through to the unbound fallback. Three were bound with fixed or partial answers and now answer from the game: `game_all_quiet` (false while a hostile actor is fighting, the test `game_safe_to_save` makes without the player check), `game_safe_to_speak` (false while a scripted line or conversation is still playing) and the volume tests, which answered only for `(players)`: `volume_test_objects`, `_all` and `volume_test_object` now test the player however it is named (`(list_get (players) 0)`, d40's ovens and `prelift_slayer`), the living actors of a list (`(ai_actors enc6_1_flood)`, d20's `enc6_3` and `1_to_2_transition_trigger` waits, a50's `gravity_trigger`), one actor or a scripted object; `_all` passes when every one is inside, so an empty list passes as in Halo. The condition commands still answered with a fixed value are deliberate and listed in the test: `game_is_cooperative`, `game_saving`, `player_count`, the default-controls queries, `unit_solo_player_integrated_night_vision_is_active` (no night vision), and the fallbacks of `vehicle_test_seat_list` and `ai_going_to_vehicle` for vehicles neither driven nor boardable. Bound as no-ops (presentation only): `breakable_surfaces_enable`, `breakable_surfaces_reset`, `scenery_animation_start`, `scenery_animation_start_at_frame`, `custom_animation_list`, `camera_set_dead`, `cinematic_suppress_bsp_object_creation`, `device_set_never_appears_locked` and `ai` (used only inside scenes, which hold the actors anyway), next to the existing ones (`rasterizer_*`, `object_pvs_*`, `object_beautify`, `objects_predict`, `print`, `display_scenario_help`, `enable_hud_help_flash`, `show_hud`, `player_enable_input`, `player_camera_control`, `garbage_collect_now`, `game_speed`, `game_skip_ticks`, the HUD timer layout and time code commands). `player_add_equipment` (a10's `bridge0_profile` at Keyes's bridge, c20's `easy_start` on Easy), the last command a script called that was unbound, now hands out the scenario's starting profile (batch 8, Retail weapons).

**Carried-in sections, Continue and the title.** A section entered from the one before (the script carried in) now takes the in-memory checkpoint once its opening scene, if any, has ended, so a death before the section's first checkpoint reverts to the script as it came in, in live play as after Continue from that section's start (it used to restart the script on the section in live play). The vehicles, ground items and stock items go back to the section's placements in both, as before. Continue and New Game clear the last progress warning (a corrupt file restored from its backup) once they start a game; a Continue whose saved state does not fit sets its own warning as before. After the credits the title menu comes up (it used to be the pause menu), with New Game and every mission in Mission select.

## Reference and verification

The local Halo3DS fork supplies the Xbox cache layout, BSP and texture formats, model animation transforms, Xbox IMA ADPCM and compressed animation reference decoders, and HaloScript execution semantics. The importers read the prepared Xbox CE 1.0 A30 cache directly. On this Mac the expanded file is under `My 3DS/build/local-assets/NEW2DS/halo-source/` in the neighboring project. Its SHA-256 is `287959f34a7379a1dbec5fec15b4d94d17fe97ccd861f5c0c8fc80148b7ed293`. [Halo3DS upstream](https://github.com/Generalkidd/Halo3DS)

The signed Apple Silicon app played The Pillar of Autumn opening at normal speed through the return to player control. The flyby uses the original filtered hull lightmaps and six nozzle flares. The camera position and viewing direction matched the Azahar capture within 0.003 Halo units and 0.024 degrees across eleven sampled ticks. Sky compositing and the hull's bump and emissive shading still differ. The native device checks exercised door collision before, during and after opening, the first Autumn BSP switch, and the second Assault on the Control Room lift carrying the player. A separate replay completed all 55 sections with injected positions and combat outcomes, including direct placement in the Library's elevated BSP switch volumes (before the work listed under Not yet verified in the game, which has not been replayed). A continuous manual playthrough and the Library lift approaches remain unverified. No iOS simulators were used.

| Mission | Playable sections | Adapted landmarks and flow |
| --- | ---: | --- |
| The Pillar of Autumn | 7 | Cryo area, ship corridors, lifeboat route |
| Halo | 3 | Crash Site, structure and cavern approach, evacuation area |
| The Truth and Reconciliation | 5 | Canyon, muster bay, hangar, prison and return |
| The Silent Cartographer | 3 | Beach approach, Cartographer interaction, return to the beach |
| Assault on the Control Room | 7 | Forerunner corridors, snowy exterior and control room |
| 343 Guilty Spark | 7 | Swamp, containment interiors, escape into the swamp |
| The Library | 4 | Four Library BSPs and Index interaction |
| Two Betrayals | 5 | Reverse Control Room route and final pulse generator interaction |
| Keyes | 6 | Cruiser exterior and interiors, implant recovery and hangar return |
| The Maw | 8 | Autumn interiors, reactor interaction and timed Warthog escape |

## Robustness, sanitizers and CI

**Not yet run** (written while testing was on hold); the test plan is in `tests/README.md`.

**Sanitizer build.** `-DHALO_SANITIZE=ON` (with `-DHALO_BUILD_QA=ON`, which it requires) instruments the whole build tree with AddressSanitizer and UndefinedBehaviorSanitizer (`-fsanitize=address,undefined`, without the `function` and `vptr` checks: the stock engine calls console commands through generic function pointers), with frame pointers and debug info. `./scripts/build-macos.sh --asan` configures and builds it in `build/macos-asan` (RelWithDebInfo), so `build/macos` stays the shipping build; `HALO_BUILD_DIR` picks another directory. `package-macos.sh` refuses to package a `build/macos` configured with `HALO_SANITIZE=ON`. The QA client links Apple's sanitizer runtime from Xcode (`@rpath/libclang_rt.asan_osx_dynamic.dylib`). UBSan findings in the stock engine that are triaged and harmless go in `tests/data/ubsan.supp` (empty for now); findings in the `halo_*` files are to be fixed.

**Soak.** `tests/test_soak.py` runs `qa_soak` (`tests/native/soak_qa.inc`): one QA client process loads all 55 sections in campaign order (the Crash Site, `halo_a30`, as a30's first) as section selects, twice by default, letting each run for 1.5 s (4 s with `--asan`) once its opening scene is skipped, and logs the process's resident size, peak and physical footprint after each (`tests/native/qa_memory.cpp`, Mach `task_info`). It fails on a crash or hang, any sanitizer report, a section that does not load or leaves the player dead, or growth: pass 1 fills the engine's texture, model and sound caches by design, so from pass 2 on the median per-section footprint growth over the pass before must stay under 64 MB (`--growth-mb`), as must the growth from the end of pass 1 to the end of the last pass per extra pass. `--asan` uses `build/macos-asan/halo-ac-qa`. Memory per section goes to `build/qa/soak-memory.csv`.

**Damaged assets.** `tests/test_asset_robustness.py` builds, per case, a mod directory under `build/qa/` that mirrors `packages/` through symlinks with one generated file removed or damaged, loads a30 section 2 from it and exercises it (`qa_robust_exercise`: fire, zoom, swap, both grenade types, a Warthog spawned, driven and left, a death and a revert, with the HUD drawn throughout). The game must log a fallback and exit cleanly. The loaders' fallbacks, as they now are:

| File | Missing | Damaged | Falls back to |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `packages/halo/hud.txt` | `Halo HUD: ... is missing` | `invalid line ...`, then `... is unusable` (bitmap ids are bounded to 0-4095 and page sizes to 1-16384; a failed parse is cleared) | the fallback HUD and plain reticles |
| `weapons.txt`, `equipment.txt`, `actors.txt` | `... is missing` | `invalid line N`, `is format N`, `ends inside` a record (a cut-off table is refused), then `... is unusable`; non-finite projectile speeds are refused | stock AssaultCube weapons; AssaultCube's grenade and no plasma grenade; the built-in actor profiles, and for the difficulty table (format 1 or 2, or an unusable file) the engine's former constants: enemy damage 0.6/1/1.3/1.7, enemy vitality and shields 0.75/1/1.25/1.5, every other value 1 (Difficulty) |
| `vehicles.txt` | `... is missing ... (built-in Warthog only)` | `invalid line`, `ends inside`, `is unusable`: a record with a non-finite or non-positive size or scale, or a box inside out, is refused | the built-in Warthog |
| `effects.txt` | `... is missing` | `... is not format 1`; light radius, time and blast are clamped | every effect drawn from its name (an explosion or a burst); the flashlight's built-in light; no sound class gains for the scenario's sounds |
| `maps/<section>.hcol` | stock geometry (no `loaded N retail collision surfaces`) | `retail collision unavailable for <section>` | stock geometry |
| `maps/<section>.mesh` | `... .mesh is missing or unreadable` (new log line) | the same; positions beyond 10^6 are refused | the section's cube geometry drawn |
| `halo/<mission>.hscb` | `no retail scenario program for <mission> (...)`, now naming the file and the importer | the same (header, version, sizes, node handles and projections are checked); a device panel with no frames is dropped; a saved recording position at or past its end is refused | the section without its mission script |
| `maps/<section>.route` (QA walker only) | `RETAIL_QA FAIL invalid-route-file` | the same, now also for non-finite rows | the walk stops cleanly |
| `config/halo-progress.txt` | a fresh profile | `campaign progress was corrupt ...` (bad check, NUL bytes, over 32 MB, out-of-range values, and now non-finite checkpoint or vitals); a state block that fails (now also on non-finite numbers in any record; a script value that went non-finite is saved as the largest finite number, or 0 for NaN, so it does not trip this) restarts the script with `could not be restored` | the backup, else reset; the script restarted on the section |

A spawn wave's actor kind read from a section map is now range-checked at both ends (a negative kind indexed the profile table).

### Route metrics

Every campaign import now writes `packages/maps/<section>.metrics.json` beside the section's map (git-ignored with it): the traversal mode, route steps and rows, reachable and total floor columns and their share, the entry and exit offsets in source units, whether a walked route ends inside its exit volume, the placements, the walking assists and scenery placements in its `.hcol` (`assists`, `scenery`), the planner's warnings, SHA-1s of the map and route it writes and whether the files on disk match them, and the stub reasons. `python3 tools/import_halo_campaign.py "$maps" --metrics-only` plans every section as an import would and writes only the metrics (about seven minutes on four workers since the route batch, d40 section 5's throw search the longest part; about four before, for all 54 imported sections, 55 with the Crash Site since batch 6); the maps, meshes, `.hcol` and `.route` files stay as they are. `tests/test_route_metrics.py` is the stub-route gate: a route with fewer than 100 steps, a reach under 5% of the floor columns, an exit offset over 25 source units, or (walked) an end outside its exit volume is a stub. A walked route that ends inside its exit is not held to the reach share (judgement call: a big outdoor window with floors the route never needs). The gate fails when a stub is not in its known-bad table (`KNOWN_STUBS`: empty since the c10 section 5 batch, which took `halo_c10_05` out, now walked round `bridge_c` up the west bay's debris pile over one walking assist, 1,101 steps ending in the lower BSP 3/4 doorway; the route batch took `halo_c40_01` out, now planned through `pulse_1_trigger` and `door_c1` to `bsp 2,10`, 1,576 rows (1,557 before the final integration review resampled its flights) ending in its exit; the scenery batch took `halo_c10_06` out, now walked over two walking assists, 889 steps ending on `lift_d`'s deck; batch 10 took `halo_d40_08` out, now planned to `grand_finale`, 673 rows ending in its exit; batch 6 took `halo_c10_03` out, now walked over two walking assists, 1,359 steps ending in the upper doorway, and added the Crash Site's metrics, `halo_a30`, walked, 1,115 steps ending in `first_marine_extraction`, not a stub), when a listed section stops being one (so the table stays current), when a section's mode disagrees with the importer's tables, or when the files on disk are not what the importer writes.
