Batch 5 took `halo_a30_01` (re-anchored on the valley floor, 1,281 steps, ending on the cave ledge), `halo_b30_03` (walked return, 170 steps to the Pelican's ledge) and `halo_b40_03` (ending in `bsp 1,8`) out of the table, from the metrics the importer wrote for the regenerated sections (none of them is a stub there); the gate itself was not run (testing on hold), and none of the three has been walked. `cgz_current` compares the map by content since the content-currency fix: the `.cgz` on disk gunzipped (`cgz_disk_map`) against the uncompressed map the importer builds, and a file that does not gunzip (empty, truncated, a damaged stream or CRC, not gzip) is stale. Before it the check compared the gzip bytes, which depend on the Python that writes them (the use-aware rule's batch: Python 3.13's `gzip.compress` writes a different deflate stream and header from 3.15's for the same map, so a full `--metrics-only` run under 3.13 flagged the 49 maps written under 3.15 stale, though their decompressed maps were the same). The metrics carry `cgz_raw_sha1`, the SHA-1 of the uncompressed map, beside `cgz_sha1`, which is kept for information only: the SHA-1 of the gzip bytes this run's Python writes (the file's own after a real import, which still rewrites the `.cgz`; it may change with the Python or zlib while the map and `cgz_raw_sha1` do not). The metrics format stays 2: `cgz_raw_sha1` is an added key, `cgz_current` keeps its meaning (the map on disk is what the importer writes) and only how it is decided changed, so format 2 files without the key still load and pass the gate, and the next `--metrics-only` adds it. `tests/test_content_currency.py` (no game, no caches, not yet run) checks `cgz_disk_map` and the PNG comparison on synthetic files, and that every metrics file has `cgz_raw_sha1` and, where current, matches the gunzipped `.cgz`. The fix's own runs: a full `--metrics-only` under Homebrew Python 3.15.2 (all 55 current, format 2, 50 walked, 3 driven, 2 flown, no stub, no warning, 4,123 scenery placements, every `cgz_sha1` equal to the file's bytes), and one under the system's Python 3.9.6 (`/usr/bin/python3`) into a scratch copy of `packages/maps`: the same 55 current with the same metrics but `cgz_sha1`, which differed for all 55 (3.9's `gzip.compress` writes header byte XFL 0, 3.15's 2, with the same zlib 1.2.12), so the old byte comparison would have called every map stale. `--check-scenes` reported all ten missions current under both; both interpreters link zlib 1.2.12 and `zlib.compress` gives the same bytes, so that run does not exercise the PNG content comparison across zlib versions (the synthetic cases in `tests/test_content_currency.py` do).

### Local CI

`./scripts/ci-macos.sh` runs, each step logged under `build/ci/<time>/` with a pass or fail summary: shell and Python syntax, `check-no-retail.sh`, the import check (every file the 55 sections need is there), `test_campaign_tables.py`, the route gate, `package-macos.sh -DHALO_BUILD_QA=ON` (build, the full asset verification, dylib bundling, signing), a rebuild of the `halo_*` sources that fails on any compiler warning other than the stock engine's deprecation warnings, and the quick tier. `--no-tests` stops before the tests; `--release` adds the release tier (`test_asset_robustness.py`, `test_soak.py`); `--asan` also builds `build/macos-asan` and runs both with it; `--metrics` refreshes the route metrics first (`HALO_SOURCE` is the expanded Xbox map directory). It stops at the first failed step. Not run yet.

## Verification

Run `./scripts/test-macos.sh` to build the game and the `halo-ac-qa` client, package the app and run the quick test tier. Add `--slow` for the full-speed opening and keystroke tests and `--release` for the release tier (damaged assets and the 55-section soak); `./scripts/ci-macos.sh` runs the whole chain with the static checks first (Robustness, sanitizers and CI). Results, logs and profiles go to `build/qa/`. The test plan for when testing resumes is at the end of the [test guide](../tests/README.md).

## Difficulty

`halo_difficulty` (0 Easy, 1 Normal, 2 Heroic, 3 Legendary; default Normal) is a saved setting, also in the pause menu under Difficulty. Squad sizes, major upgrades and actors' maximum vitality are taken from it when squads are placed (so a change shows from the next placement, or fully after a section start or restart); the damage factor on each hit, the shield recharge, the actors' combat values, the Flood pop and stagger and homing shots read it live, so a change in the pause menu applies to them at once (Halo cannot change the level mid-mission, so this has no retail counterpart). `game_difficulty_get` returns the setting. Damage, vitality and shield recharge scale by the retail globals' difficulty table and Halo's team rules (below), and since the combat-scaling work so do actors' firing, grenades, melee and homing shots (Combat by difficulty, below; unverified); until the difficulty-table work the engine used multipliers of its own (Covenant and Flood health and shields 0.75/1/1.25/1.5, their damage to the player and the player's vehicle 0.6/1/1.3/1.7, allies and the player unscaled), which are now only the fallback.

**Damage, vitality and recharge by team** (the difficulty-table work, not verified in the game; `tests/test_difficulty.py`, not yet run). The values are matg's `difficulty_information` (globals+284, one element of 35 values by four levels, enum `../Halo3DS/source/game/game_globals.h:63-99`), which `tools/halo_actors.py` (`difficulty_rows`) writes to actors.txt format 3 as `difficulty` lines. The ten caches carry one globals tag with the same table (checked by reading all ten: one `globals\globals` each, every value equal), so one exported table is exact for every mission; the exporter warns if they ever disagree. Easy, Normal, Heroic, Legendary: enemy_damage 0.3/1/1.4/1.8, enemy_vitality and enemy_shield 0.6/1/1.2/1.4, enemy_recharge 0.5/1/1.5/2, friend_vitality and friend_shield 0.8/1/1.2/1.4, friend_recharge 0.5/1/1.5/2 (friend_damage 0.4/1/1.4/1.8 is in the table but nothing reads it for damage). The rules, all from the port (`source/src/halo_ai.cpp` `halodifficultyget`, `halodifficultyteamvalue`, `halodifficultydamagefactor`):
- **Damage.** Damage whose owner's team is hostile to the player is multiplied by enemy_damage, whoever it hits (`objects/damage.c:1415-1419`): the Covenant's and the Flood's fire on the player, the marines, the player's vehicle and each other. When the target's team is not the owner's enemy (its own side: a grunt's plasma on an Elite) the multiplier is divided back out (`damage.c:1603-1612` sets the flag; the shield divides at 881-887, the body at 1159-1165), and what passes a shield is divided once more, as Halo's body step divides the shield's excess again. The player's own, the marines' and ownerless damage (scripted explosions, a vehicle's death explosion) are never multiplied. The owner team is the attacker's current team, and for an actor that has died since it fired (its grenade or projectile still in flight) the team it spawned on (`halodamageteam`; Halo's damage keeps the owner team it was created with), not the engine's Covenant default for an unknown body (the code review's fix, unverified). Before, only fire on the player and the player's vehicle was scaled, by anything but the player (ownerless and allied damage included), and never fire between actors.
- **Maximum vitality.** A unit's maximum body and shield vitality are multiplied by its team's value (`damage.c:433-456`, `game_difficulty_get_team_value`, `game_globals.c:204-225`): enemy_vitality and enemy_shield for a team hostile to the player or no team (an invalid team is an enemy, `game_allegiance.c:192-206`), friend_vitality and friend_shield for the player's team and its allies. Actors take theirs at spawn from the team they spawn on (`spawnEnemy`; Halo reads it at every hit from the current team, which differs only on Easy, 0.6 against 0.8, for an actor whose side changes later: judgement call). Vehicles take their owner team's at every hit (`halovehiclehit`, `teamOf`: the driver's team, else the gunner's, kept when they get out, `units/units.c:5327-5359`; before anyone has sat in it the vehi tag's default team, 0 in all of them, hostile to everyone, so enemy_vitality; the last occupant's team is not in saved games, which only the never-player-driven Wraith could notice, and its drivers are all hostile). d40's manifold detectors (the only damage targets; their biped's default team is 3, the Covenant) take enemy_vitality (`Scenario::damageTarget`): on Heroic 150 points destroy one instead of 125.
- **The player.** Traced end to end in the port, the player's unit is scaled by friend_vitality and friend_shield: a player's `team_index` is 1 (`game/players.c:1140`) and the unit's `owner_team_index` is set from it (`players.c:1321`); every team is friendly to itself (`game_allegiance.c:171-180`), so `game_team_is_enemy(_game_team_player, 1)` is false and `game_difficulty_get_team_value` answers the friend values (`game_globals.c:213-221`); `object_get_maximum_body_vitality` and `_shield_vitality` (`damage.c:433-456`) apply them with no check for a player-controlled unit, and their callers (the shield step, the body step, `object_get_actual_*`) pass `ignore_difficulty` only for falling damage to a unit on the player team (`damage.c:820-825`, `1142-1147`), an exception that would be pointless if the player were not scaled. The shield recharge (`damage.c:1926-1932`) likewise uses friend_recharge with no player exclusion. So the engine scales the player too (judgement call on a point the critic flagged as surprising): the code is unambiguous and the ten caches agree. It does not make the player tougher on Legendary: with enemy_damage the player loses, per hit relative to Normal, 0.3/0.8 = 0.375 of Normal's share on Easy, 1, 1.4/1.2 = 1.17 on Heroic and 1.8/1.4 = 1.29 on Legendary (before: 0.6/1/1.3/1.7), shields and health alike; the shields come back from empty in 8 s, 4 s, 2.7 s and 2 s of recharge after the unchanged 6 s stun (the engine's own times; Halo's recharge rate times friend_recharge), so on Legendary the player dies sooner under fire but recovers faster between fights. The shield and health still count out of 75 (Normal's maximum): the hit is divided by the scale instead, which takes off the same fraction Halo does and leaves the HUD, health packs, saves and starting profiles as they were. There is no falling damage in the engine, so its exception does not arise.
- **Shield recharge.** The full-recharge time (the player's 4 s, an actor's from its coll) is divided by enemy_recharge or friend_recharge for the unit's current team (`rechargeTime` in `source/src/halo.cpp`); the delay before it is not scaled (Halo's stun ticks are not). Since the shield-leak work (not verified in the game; `tests/test_melee_materials.py` item 9, not run) each actor's stun and recharge come from its coll, the `shieldtime` line of actors.txt (minimum stun damage +264, stun time +268, recharge time +272, runtime recharge velocity +448; `enemyShieldTiming`): Halo stuns the shield for (short)(stun × 30) ticks whenever a hit's shield damage reaches the minimum or the shield is empty (`damage.c:978-982`; the minimum is 0 for every retail unit, so every hit, a Jackal body hit included) and then adds the velocity per tick (1925-1932), a full recharge in 1/(velocity × 30) s. The minor and major Elites and the Sentinels keep 12 s and 10 s, the commanders and spec-ops Elites (the `elite special` unit) 8 s and 10 s, the marines 8 s and 1 s, and the Jackals 15 s and one tick (their recharge time 0 gives velocity 1). Without the line every actor uses the Elite's 12 s and 10 s, as before.
- **Fallback.** With an actors.txt older than format 3 (no `difficulty` lines) `halodifficultyget` answers the engine's former constants for enemy_damage (0.6/1/1.3/1.7) and enemy_vitality and enemy_shield (0.75/1/1.25/1.5), 0 for the three values Halo adds rather than multiplies (target_tracking, target_leading and melee_delay_base; since the combat-scaling work) and for infection_forms (since the Flood tag batch: Normal's neutral value, no stagger or pop toughness), and 1 for every other value, so the player, allies, recharge and every actor's firing stay as they were, with the team rules applied.

`qa_difficulty` prints the values in use (`DIFFICULTY ... damage= health= shield= recharge= human= player= none= table=`) and one `DIFFICULTY_DAMAGE <owner>><target> factor= divisor=` line per team pair; `halo_damagetrace 1` logs `DAMAGE_DIFFICULTY owner= target= level= factor= divisor=` on each scaled hit, and `halo_vehicletrace 1` the vehicle's team. Saves keep loading unchanged (no format changes; the `.hscb` files are unchanged).

**Combat by difficulty** (the combat-scaling work: the batch 9 critic's gap "Enemy AI firing and grenade behaviour do not change with difficulty"; written while testing was on hold, not verified in the game; `tests/test_actor_ai.py` item 6, not yet run). Halo scales actors' firing by the same table at use, on the actor's team (`game_difficulty_get_team_value`: a team hostile to the player, or no team, takes the level's value; the player's side, the marines included, Normal's, `game_globals.c:204-225`), and a few values by the level for every team (`game_difficulty_get_value`). The engine applies them where Halo reads them, to the variant's actv values (actors.txt keeps the tag numbers), through `halocombatscaling` and the brain's `combatValues` (`source/src/halo_ai.cpp`). Easy, Normal, Heroic, Legendary, from the table:
- **rate_of_fire** 0.8/1/1.2/1.5. Halo pulls the trigger every `30 / (rate_of_fire_tag * value)` ticks, at least 2 (`ai/actor_combat.c:2380-2404`); a variant rate of 0 holds the trigger at the weapon's own cadence, unscaled (`:2378-2382`). The engine's burst cadence is the slot's `1000 / rate` ms from spawn (`spawnEnemy`); `fire()` now writes `max(66, 1000 / (rate * value))` into the slot before each shot (no exported rate reaches the 66 ms floor on Normal, so Normal is unchanged). Variants with rate 0 (the Elites, the fuel rod Grunts, several marines) are unchanged on every level, as in Halo. A vehicle gun's AI fire keeps the gun's own cadence (`triggerDelay`: the firing variant's rate is not used for vehicles in the engine), but the engine's extra wait between a semi-automatic gun's rounds (the mortar, the cannon; 0.4 to 1.3 s) is divided by the seated actor's value.
- **projectile_error** 0.75/1/0.8/0.5 multiplies the variant's error angle (`actor_combat.c:1268-1272`): the infantry aim spread in `fire()` (the camouflage term is the engine's own and unscaled), the seated actor's error a vehicle fires with (`HaloActorIntent::error`, and the vehicle variant's error times `errorScale` in `aiFire`). Easy is more accurate than Normal: that is the table's 0.75, kept.
- **burst_separation** 1.2/1/0.8/0.5 multiplies the pause between bursts (`actor_combat.c:599-605`): the variant's separation in `fire()` and the engine's 0.7 to 1.5 s between a vehicle gun's bursts (`separationScale`).
- **target_leading** 0/0/0.2/0.4. In the port it is added to the variant's lead fraction only for the test `MIN(1, sum) > 0`; the lead itself (`point_from_line3d`) uses the variant's own fraction (`actor_combat.c:2194-2210`). Followed as written (the conservative reading; a retail claim that Legendary leads harder is not in the code): the engine leads by the variant's fraction when the gate passes. Every exported lead is 0 or more, so in practice nothing changes. **target_tracking** (0/0/0.2/0.4) is the same gate for the burst's tracking (`:2174-2189`); the engine aims at the target's centre every frame and has no tracked burst origin, so it has no counterpart.
- **grenade_chance_scale** 0.2/1/1.5/2 multiplies the throw chance (`ai/actions.c:3642-3647`), in `throwGrenade`. **grenade_timer_scale** 1.2/1/0.8/0.5 multiplies Halo's encounter-wide timeout after any member's throw (`actions.c:2350-2373`): the testing actor's actv `encounter_grenade_timeout` (+424, actors.txt's `grenade` line's optional seventh field; Grunts 4 to 7 s, spec-ops Elites and Engineers 5, marines 20 or 30 at Normal's value, Sentinels 3) times the value on its team, in whole ticks, from the last throw of any member of its encounter (`last_grenade_throw_time`, set at :3604-3607; the engine keeps it per encounter index in `grenadeTimeoutLeft` and forgets it at a section start or revert, where Halo's checkpoint would restore it). An actor in no encounter has no timeout (Halo skips the test). The engine's 4 to 8 s between an actor's checks stays, unscaled: it stands for Halo's `throw_grenade_delay` between chance rolls (0.5 to 1 s, :3636-3641) together with the target, enemy-count and trajectory tests the engine does not make (judgement call: rolling every 0.5 s with the engine's simpler test would throw far more than Halo). Without the field (an older actors.txt) the former stand-in: the actor's own 4 to 8 s wait after its throw times the value.
- **melee_delay_scale** 2/1/0.8/0.5 and **melee_delay_base** 1.5/0/0/0 s, for every team: Halo starts a melee charge once scale times the actr's `melee_attack_delay_timer` (+888, actors.txt's `berserk` line's optional fourth field; 0 while berserk) plus base have passed since `last_melee_attack_time` (`actions.c:2563-2568`, 2593-2601), which the charge refreshes every tick until its attack ends (`action_charge.c:400-402`), so the delay follows the attack. The engine plays no melee animation, so its own interval stands for the attack and is not scaled (judgement call; `meleeInterval`): a Hunter's smash takes 1.6 to 2.2 s (its retail `stand unarmed melee` is 61 frames, 2.03 s) and a melee-only actor's swing its slot delay, at least 1.2 s (the Elite's sword melees are 28 and 35 frames). With the Hunters' 0.1 s timer they smash every 1.7 to 2.3 s on Normal, 1.65 to 2.25 s on Legendary and 3.3 to 3.9 s on Easy (berserk: 1.6 to 2.2 s, Easy 3.1 to 3.7); the Elite commander's sword (2 s) swings every 3.2 s on Normal and 2.2 s on Legendary, the stealth major's (0 s) every 1.2 s (2.7 s on Easy), a carrier (0 s) every 0.9 s (2.4 s on Easy). Only these melee in the engine (the other actors' melee charges are not modelled), so the other actr timers (Flood combat forms 2 s, Elite minors 5) are carried but unused. Halo's rule skips swarm actors (`actions.c:2541`): the infection forms, like every actor of an actors.txt without the field, keep the former rule (`halomeleedelay`: the whole interval scaled).
- **guidance_vs_player** 0.5/0.5/0.75/1 multiplies a guided projectile's turn rate when its target is the player, whoever fired it (`items/projectiles.c:798-803`), in `haloupdatebolts` (`source/src/weapon.cpp`): the needles and the plasma pistol's charged bolt home at half their rate on Easy and Normal, as in Halo. This is the one change on Normal.
- **No counterpart, documented rather than invented.** **burst_error** (1.25/1/0.8/0.5) scales Halo's burst geometry (the burst origin and return radii, `actor_combat.c:1400-1408`): the engine has no swept burst and does not export the geometry. **new_target_delay** (1.4/1/0.6/0.3) scales how long after acquiring a target the actor fires its `new_target_pattern` (modifiers on burst length, separation, rate and error, `actor_combat.c:1230-1235` and 481-484): the engine has no firing patterns and exports neither; its reaction delay is the first-burst delay, which Halo does not scale. **special_fire_delay** (1.2/1/0.8/0.5) and **overcharge_chance** (0.2/1/1.5/2) are read into locals that the port never uses (`actor_combat.c:1776-1800`; the special fire delay at :1812 is the tag's, unscaled), so they have no effect in Halo's code either; the engine has no special fire for infantry, and the Banshee pilot's fuel rod wait (5 to 9 s, `aiFire`) is left unscaled. The playfighting factors of the same functions (1.7 on separation, 0.6 on burst length, doubled error and grenade timeout) are not modelled.

`qa_actor_combat <label>` prints two lines per team, `COMBAT_DIFFICULTY <label> level= table= team= rof= perror= burst_error= new_target= separation= tracking= leading=` and `COMBAT_DIFFICULTY_MORE <label> team= overcharge= special= grenade_chance= grenade_timer= melee_scale= melee_base= guidance=` (the console cuts a line at 260 characters), and one `ACTOR_COMBAT <label> cn= variant= team= rate= delay= slot= error= scaled_error= lead= leads= separation= grenade= grenade_scaled= melee= smash=` line per living actor (the values `combatValues` gives the brain; `slot` is the delay the weapon code last read). `ACTOR_TIMERS <label> cn= variant= berserk= swarm= melee_timer= grenade_timeout= encounter= grenade_wait= grenade_left=` per living actor gives the two tag timers (-1 without them), the encounter and its scaled timeout and what is left of it; `qa_actor_grenade_note <label>` records a throw for the first actor with an encounter and a timeout (`GRENADE_NOTE`). actors.txt gains only the two optional fields (still format 3: older engines read the fields before them); the `.hscb` files and saves are unchanged, and an actors.txt older than format 3 leaves combat as it was (Fallback).

**Squad sizes** (the squad-count batch, not verified in the game). The script importer stores each squad's Easy, Normal, Hard and Impossible start counts in the `.hscb` (version 7 and later) by Halo's rule (`../Halo3DS/source/ai/encounters.c:2191-2201`, the squad's `min_count` and `max_count` at squad+124 and +126, `ai_scenario_definitions.h:296`): Easy and Normal place `min_count`, Hard `(max_count + min_count) / 2` in integer division, Impossible `max_count`, even where it is below `min_count` (132 of the 2,988 campaign squads, such as 16/12, 8/6 and 8/0: on Legendary Halo places fewer of them than on Normal), each clamped to at least 0 (`tools/import_halo_scripts.py` `difficulty_counts`). Until this batch the importer made Easy three quarters of Normal, rounded Hard up and took Impossible as the larger of the two; the rule changed 601 squads' Easy count, 444 Hard and 132 Impossible. The offsets were checked against the port's struct and the caches (a30's `first_marine` squads 2/2, c10's `grunts_insertion` 4/4; the enum at +128 has exactly the 2,851/9/25/89/14 split below). `ai_place` places the first N starts that name an actor; `ai_spawn_actor` ignores the count. Re-run `tools/import_halo_scripts.py` after pulling this change; older `.hscb` files keep loading (versions 5 and 6 place every start, 7 to 9 their stored counts).

**Major upgrades** (the major-upgrade batch, not verified in the game). Halo places an actor as its variant's major when the actv has a major reference (`actor_definitions.h:408`, +36: elite minor needler or plasma rifle to elite major plasma rifle, the grunt, jackal and stealth elite minors to their majors, hunter to hunter major, the marines to their or marine_armored's majors, the sentinels to theirs, and the Flood elite sniper to its needler) and the squad's roll succeeds (`encounters.c:3040-3051`, `actors.c:2687`). The squad's `major_upgrade` (squad+128, enum `ai_scenario_definitions.h:37`; 2,851 normal, 9 few, 25 many, 89 none and 14 all across the ten caches) sets the chance (`ai.c:615`): normal the globals' `major_upgrade` (0.15/0.25/0.5/0.75 Easy to Legendary), few `major_upgrade_1` (0.05/0.1/0.3/0.5), many `major_upgrade_2` (0.25/0.5/0.75/1), none never and all always (values 28 to 30 of matg's `difficulty_information`, `game_globals.h:63-99`; actors.txt format 3 carries all 35 as `difficulty` lines, the same in the ten caches; without them the engine uses these numbers, `halomajorupgradechance`). The choice is Halo's error diffusion (`ai.c:1875`): the larger in magnitude of the map-wide error times -1/3 and the squad's error, negated, is added to the chance; a draw below that upgrades; both errors grow by (upgraded - chance). So a squad's majors stay within one actor of its share and a section's within about three. The draw is Halo's generator (`math/random_math.c:211`, the LCG of :69-70) on a seed of the upgrades' own, started each fresh section from Halo's default game seed 0xDEADBEEF (`game/game.c:541`) moved on by the section's index. Judgement calls: Halo draws from its one global seed, shared with everything random, so its exact sequence cannot be reproduced, and a fixed per-section seed keeps a section's placements the same from one fresh start to the next; the errors are zeroed when a fresh section starts (Halo zeroes them per map, `ai.c:1929`, `encounters.c:1767`) and carried on when the player walks into the mission's next section (Halo's mission is one map). Only squad placement (`ai_place` and `ai_spawn_actor`, both through `encounter_place_actor` in Halo) upgrades; an `ai_attach`ed object keeps its squad's default variant, and a carried attached actor its own. The variant an actor was placed as is kept: a revert respawns it as that variant (`SquadBot::major`), and the errors and seed go back to the checkpoint's, so the squads placed after the checkpoint are placed as before. A saved game's state block carries both: a trailing `1` on a major's `s squad` record and an optional `s upgrade <seed> <map-wide error> <count> (<encounter> <squad> <error>)...` record; saves without them load (every member as its start's variant, the errors afresh). An `.hscb` older than version 10 has no squad enum, so nothing is upgraded with it (the engine cannot tell normal from none); an actors.txt older than format 3 has no majors, so likewise. `halo_scripttrace 1` logs each roll (`major upgrade chance ..., errors ...`), and the placement line ends `, N major`.

## Title menu and saved games

**Not verified in the game yet**: everything in this section was written and compiled while testing was on hold. `tests/test_frontend_progress.py` is written but has not been run.

The app and `./scripts/play-macos.sh` now start with `halo_title` instead of `halo_campaign`: a title screen (a plain backdrop over the stock level the engine boots into) with the **Halo** menu from `config/halo.cfg`:

- **Continue** resumes the saved game: its section, at its last checkpoint when one was reached in that section, with the loadout (both weapons with their rounds or battery, frag and plasma grenades and the selected type, health and shield) and difficulty it was saved with. The entry shows the mission, section, difficulty and whether a checkpoint is saved, and is greyed out when there is nothing to continue. With a version 3 save the game resumes exactly at the checkpoint: Continue starts the section, skips its opening scene, loads the saved checkpoint into the in-memory checkpoint and reverts to it as a death would, so the mission script carries on with its threads, globals, doors and lifts, scripted objects, squads, nav points and HUD state as they were, the vehicles stand where they stood (a player saved in a seat continues in it), the weapons and equipment on the ground and the stock items are as they were, the scripts' limits on the player (`unit_set_maximum_vitality`, `unit_impervious`) hold, a running overshield or active camouflage keeps its time left, and a later death reverts there too. A save at a section's start (no checkpoint yet) holds the script carried in from the section before, and Continue resumes it at the section's start. Without a usable state block (a version 2 file, a save at the start of a mission's first section, or a block that does not fit, below) the mission script starts afresh on the section as before: its opening scene is skipped when resuming at a checkpoint, the player is put back at the checkpoint (with the engine's route stage and marker), and that spot becomes the checkpoint a death reverts to. On The Maw's run the escape countdown resumes with the time left at the section's start. All of this is unverified in the game; `tests/test_frontend_progress.py` (items 3, 4 and 8 to 11) checks it.
- **New Game** asks for a difficulty and starts The Pillar of Autumn from the beginning (the Crash Site preview when a10 is not imported). Unlocked missions stay unlocked; the saved game is replaced once the section starts.
- **Mission select** lists the ten missions; those up to the furthest reached are playable (all of them once the campaign is beaten), the rest are greyed out as locked or not imported. Picking one asks for the difficulty. The in-game pause menu (**Halo Campaign**, Escape) uses the same list; the `halo_mission N` console command is not restricted.
- **Settings** and **Quit**. Escape with no menu open on the title screen brings the menu back.

The pause menu keeps Resume, Restart section, Mission select, Difficulty, Settings and Quit, and adds **Main menu**, which shows the title menu over the paused campaign (Escape returns to the game). Console commands behind the menus: `halo_title`, `halo_new_game <0-3>`, `halo_continue`, and the menu helpers `halo_can_continue`, `halo_continue_label`, `halo_unlocked`, `halo_mission_state <1-10>` (1 playable, 0 locked, 2 not imported), `halo_mission_title <1-10>`, `halo_difficulty_name` and `halo_progress_note`. `halo_campaign` (resume the furthest section from its start) is unchanged; tests and `play-macos.sh '-e...'` pass their own start command, which replaces `halo_title`.

The game is saved to `config/halo-progress.txt` in the profile at the start of every campaign section (section starts, section changes, restarts) and at every checkpoint, the engine's and the scripts' (`game_save`). Section selects and the Crash Site preview are not saved. The file is version 3, one record per line (`furthest`, `beaten`, `game`, `checkpoint`, `vitals`, `grenades`, `weapon` by retail tag path, `current`, `stock` for the fallback weapons without `weapons.txt`, then the state block) closed by a `check` line holding an FNV-1a hash of the rest. Version 2 files (the same without the state block) and version 1 files (`1 <mission> <section> [1]`) still load; a version 1 file's Continue starts the furthest section from its start.

The state block (`source/src/halo_state.h` for the record syntax) starts with `state <format> <mission> <section> <checkpoint> <.hscb hash> <section map hash> <records> <hash>`, the last two the number of records that follow and their own FNV-1a, so a damaged block is found even when the file's check holds. Its records are `s` (the mission script, `halo_script.cpp`): what the in-memory checkpoint keeps for a revert (every global by name, each thread by script name with its whole call stack, the scripted objects that differ from their defaults by object name, the living squad members by encounter, squad and start, every device group and device by object name, the external globals, the objective and help text, nav points, the HUD parts hidden or blinking, the chapter and the tick) and what the scripts set on the AI and a revert leaves alone (actor lists, `ai_link_activation`, migration targets, the platoon rules that fired, `ai_grenades`, the HUD timer); `v` (`halo_vehicle.cpp`): each vehicle of the checkpoint by vehicles.txt type and scenario object, its pose, motion, script settings, gun, safe spots and the player's seat (other riders and times in milliseconds are not kept); `w` (`halo.cpp`): the weapons and equipment on the ground (by tag path, scenario object and flags) and the stock items' spawned flags; `p`: `unit_set_maximum_vitality` and `unit_impervious` on the player and the powerups' time left. A save at a section's start writes only `s` and `p`, and only when the script was carried in (a section opened by the one before it, or continued); its opening scene ends first. Text is escaped so a record is one line; text and object pointers are written as offsets and indices; thread frames are the `.hscb`'s node datums. Continue checks the block before anything changes: its header, count and hash; that it is for the saved section; that the loaded `.hscb` has the hash it was saved with (frames mean nothing in another one); that every name matches the index it was saved at, every table has the size it had, and every frame's node exists. If any of that fails, the script restarts on the section as before, and the log (`Halo: The checkpoint's mission script state could not be restored; the script restarted on this section (<why>)`), the title menu's header and the pause menu's header warn about it; the Continue entry already says `(scripts changed: the section's script restarts)` when the `.hscb` on disk has another hash. If only the section map (`.cgz`) changed, the script's state is kept and the vehicles and ground items go back to their placements, with a warning. A file is expected to hold a few hundred kilobytes, perhaps a few megabytes late in a long mission (not measured; it is read up to 32 MB). Each save writes a temporary file, moves the previous file to `halo-progress.txt.bak` if it is valid, then renames the new one into place. On launch a progress file that fails its check is moved to `halo-progress.txt.corrupt` and the backup is restored and rewritten; the log (`campaign progress was corrupt ... restored from the backup`), the title screen and the title menu's header warn about it. With no usable backup progress is reset, with the same warnings. A save interrupted between its two renames is recovered from the temporary file.

Loadout persistence also applies within a session: finishing a section carries what the player carries into the next section of the same mission (Halo's sections are BSP switches), and a revert before the section's first checkpoint, or Restart section, returns to the loadout the player brought into the section rather than the starting one. A new mission (and a section started on its own) starts with the mission's player starting profile (batch 8, Retail weapons: a10 unarmed), or with the default assault rifle, pistol and two frags with an older `.hscb`. An empty slot is saved as `empty <slot>`.

**Optional state records (batch 8, not verified in the game).** The state block's `s` records may also hold, after the allegiances: `attached` (an actor `ai_attach` made of a scripted object: object, encounter and squad by index and name, suspended, script flags, whether it is dead, its variant and where it stands), `board` (the script vehicles the player may board: a30's extraction Pelicans made enterable, which the state used to drop, so after a Continue a30's seat tests fell back to "boarded"), `boarded` (the vehicle and seat the player was aboard at the checkpoint, and where they got in; a revert or Continue puts them aboard again) and `gain` (the 51 sound class gains, a fade in progress as its target: c10's music fades survive a Continue). Also `attaching` (review fix): the scripted objects attached in play whose actor does not exist yet (a50's jailed marines, posed in their cells until the rescue), so that after a Continue they still become actors when freed; a revert likewise puts back the attaches as they stood at the checkpoint (an attach made after it is undone with the script, one waiting before it keeps waiting) and clears the retry of a spawn that found no floor. A save without them loads with their defaults; an engine from before them refuses a save that has them (unknown record) and restarts the script on the section. Since batch 5's critic's gaps 4 and 10 (not verified in the game): an `attached` record may end with the actor's health and shield fractions and a carried flag (1: one carried in from the section before that had not spawned yet; Continue spawns it behind the player again, as the section's start did), and `boarded` may end with a scripted flag (a seat the script holds the player in, such as a10's cryotube; seat -1 for a vehicle with no seats). Both are optional on reading; an engine from before them refuses such a record and restarts the script on the section. Since batch 5's critic's gap 11 (not verified in the game): an `attached` record may end, after the carried flag, with the actor's start (x, y, z and yaw in scenario units: where it took over, which `ai_teleport_to_starting_location` goes back to), the point before it being where it stood at the checkpoint; without them the two are the same, as before. An engine from before them refuses such a record (malformed) and restarts the script on the section, as for the other optional fields. Since the stood-members change (not verified in the game): an `s squad` record may end, after its major flag (now written as 0 or 1 on every record), with the member's health and shield fractions and then where it stood (x, y, z and yaw in scenario units; left out for a member riding a vehicle), and last (the script-vitality change) ` max <body> <shield>` when a script set its maximum vitality, which an `s attached` record may end with too. Since the carrier-squad change (unverified): `s spawned <e> =<encounter> <s> =<squad> <variant> <kind> <suspended> <flags> <health> <shield> <x> <y> <z> <yaw>`, a living form a carrier of the squad released, where it appeared, then optionally where it stood and ` max`; written only while one lives, so other saves still load in an older engine, which refuses one that has it (unknown record) and restarts the script on the section. Without them the member comes back at its start at full vitality, as before; an engine from before them refuses such a record (malformed) and restarts the script on the section, as for the other optional fields. Not added, on purpose: c40 section 4's Banshee of the engine's own needs no record (a checkpoint's vehicle list already holds it, and a Continue at the section's start makes another, as the section's start does); the EMP stun and the fraction of a health point exact hits leave over are under two seconds and under one point.

Limits: a save during a cutscene keeps no script state. Squad members come back where they stood at the checkpoint with their health and shield fractions since the stood-members change (below), but nothing else of an actor's own state is kept: its brain (target, firing position, alertness, what it knew of the player) starts afresh at the spot, as the respawn at a start always did; a member riding a vehicle at the checkpoint comes back at its squad start (with its fractions), and a member whose spot fails the floor or wall check falls back to its start too. Actors no squad placed (squadless bursts, the combat forms rising from bodies) are not kept at all; since the carrier-squad change a carrier's forms in its squad are (`s spawned`, below). A revert after Continue at a checkpoint clears the overshield and camouflage, as any revert does; Continue at a section's start makes the carried script the state a death before the first checkpoint reverts to, as a live session now does too (Script scenes and player moves). Re-importing the scripts (`import_halo_scripts.py`) changes the `.hscb` hash, so saves made before it continue with the script restarted. The file keeps only the checkpoint's loadout, so Restart section after a Continue at a checkpoint starts the section with that loadout, not the one the player first brought into it. Under the title menu at launch the stock level the engine boots into keeps running.

**Squad members where they stood (the stood-members change, not verified in the game; `tests/test_frontend_progress.py` item 16, not run).** Halo's checkpoint is the whole game state (`../Halo3DS/source/saved games/game_state.c:289-294` writes the state heap, `:312-324` a revert reads it back, `:329-343` the persistent save is the same block), so a revert keeps every living actor where it was and as hurt as it was. A checkpoint here used to keep living squad members only by encounter, squad and start, so a revert or a Continue put survivors back at their starts at full vitality and a half-cleared fight reset. Now the checkpoint's copy of each living member (`Scenario::noteStood` in `source/src/halo_script.cpp`, the attached actors' path of gap 4 extended to every member) keeps its health and shield fractions and, unless it rides a vehicle (`Scenario::riding`: an engine vehicle's seat or a script vehicle's cargo, seated or walking to or from it), where it stands; the live save at a section's start notes them as they stand. `Scenario::restoreSquads` brings a member back where it stood when the spot passes spawnCarried's two checks (`Scenario::stoodSpot`): on retail collision floor under it by the spawn helper's ring (`haloscenariospawnfloor`, new in `source/src/halo.cpp`, the same `retailfloorspot` the spawn uses) and nothing solid between where it stood and where that floor puts it; otherwise, and for a rider, at its start as before. Either way it comes back with its fractions and as the variant it was placed as (the major flag of the squad-count and major-upgrade batch). A member with no actor record (a body with no actors.txt variant, whose health fraction reads 0) keeps full health, as every member did before (the code review's guard in `noteStood`). The log says `HaloScript: the revert put N squad members back where they stood (M at their starts instead: no floor or a wall there)`. Judgement calls: spawnCarried's wall line runs from the player, since a carried actor is placed near them; a reverted actor's line starts where it stood. A rider comes back at its start, not in its seat (the engine does not seat a reverted actor; the restored scripts load their vehicles again as before). Dead and erased members are not kept, as before; attached actors keep their own record (`s attached`) and now fall back to their start when the spot fails, where they used to be lost. QA hooks: `qa_squad_stood <label>` (`SQUAD_STOOD` per living member: squad, start, object, major, feet, health, shield, riding; then `SQUAD_STOOD_STATE ... living= stood= fallback=`) and `qa_squad_hurt <health> <shield>` (`tests/native/frontend_qa.inc`).

## Nav points and HUD script commands

**Not verified in the game yet**: written and compiled while testing was on hold. `tests/test_nav_points.py` is written but has not been run.

The mission scripts' nav point commands are bound: `activate_team_nav_point_flag <type> <team> <flag> <offset>` and `activate_team_nav_point_object <type> <team> <object> <offset>`, the per-unit `activate_nav_point_flag` and `activate_nav_point_object`, and the four matching `deactivate_*` commands (single player: the team or unit is not checked). A point is a cutscene flag or an object (it follows the object), raised by the offset in Halo world units; activating the same flag or object again replaces it. Nav points are saved with each checkpoint and restored by a revert, carried across sections within a mission and cleared when the mission script starts afresh. `source/src/halo_hud.cpp` draws them with the retail waypoint arrows: `tools/halo_hud.py` now exports the HUD globals' `hud_waypoints` bitmap and its ten arrow types (name, colour, opacity, on-screen, off-screen and occluded sequences) as `waypoint` and `arrow` lines in `packages/halo/hud.txt` (re-run `python3 tools/halo_hud.py "$maps"`; the bitmap ids of the other sheets shift, and the exporter now compares page contents so the copied textures follow). A point on screen gets the type's on-screen marker in its colour (blue for `default`, red for `default_red`); a point off screen or behind the view gets the off-screen arrow on the screen's edge, turned towards it. Occlusion is not tested, so the occluded sequence is not used. Without those lines the points are drawn as diamonds and edge triangles. While any nav point is active, the engine's own route diamond is hidden.

`hud_show_health`, `hud_show_shield`, `hud_show_motion_sensor` and `hud_show_crosshair` hide and show those parts of the retail HUD (and of the fallback HUD), and `hud_blink_health`, `hud_blink_shield` and `hud_blink_motion_sensor` flash them, as a10's cryo tutorial does on Normal (it hides the health, shield, motion sensor and reticle and reveals each in its lesson). The state is saved with checkpoints and reset when the mission script starts afresh. `hud_set_objective_text`, `hud_set_help_text` and `show_hud_help_text` were already bound. `show_hud` (the chapter title scripts) and `display_scenario_help` stay no-ops.

The retail reticles now hang from their registration point measured from the sprite's own corner (as the exported sheets store it), not from the page's; reticles on combined sheets whose sprite is not in the page's top-left corner were drawn off centre before. Also unverified in the game.
